# ObliviOS Decision Log

This record preserves product and security decisions that constrain later
implementation. A decision changes only through a new entry that names the
superseded decision and explains the evidence for the change.

Entries cite plans, session records and other working documents by path. Those
that were removed from the working tree (completed plans, session records,
handoff prompts, the chronological status log) remain in git history:
`git log --diff-filter=D --name-only -- docs/`.

This file is a lookup record of about 660 KB, never read it whole. Find an entry
by its ID with a pattern search such as `^\| ADR-214 `; one entry is one long
line. The router and the `status-*.md` records name the entries an area needs.

| ID | Date | Decision | Status | Rationale | Consequences | Review trigger |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ADR-001 | 2026-07-19 | Use a pinned GrapheneOS stable release as the Android platform base. | Accepted | It preserves an established hardened Android foundation while allowing AegisOS to focus on differentiated system services and interface work. | AegisOS must track upstream releases, maintain a documented patch layer, and preserve inherited hardening by default. | A security requirement needs a low-level platform change or the selected upstream release is retired. |
| ADR-002 | 2026-07-19 | Ship AegisOS as a distinct distribution with independent branding, signing, update, and support operations. | Accepted | A derivative must not be represented as an official GrapheneOS product. | AegisOS owns its signing keys, package identity, update endpoint, release metadata, and user-facing identity. | Trademark, licensing, or update-architecture review changes the operating model. |
| ADR-003 | 2026-07-19 | Use Pixel 7a (`lynx`) as the first bring-up target and add newer Pixels only through individual support gates. | Accepted | The Pixel 7a is the first available engineering device; hardware/firmware support must be evidence-based per model. | No device is publicly supported without current firmware, verified boot, recovery, and compatibility evidence. | The Pixel 7a arrives, an additional device is proposed, or an OEM support window ends. |
| ADR-004 | 2026-07-19 | Keep development private until an owner-approved public beta. | Accepted | The project needs time to establish platform, release, and recovery reliability before public exposure. | Public distribution triggers source-disclosure, notice, security-contact, privacy, support, and legal-review gates. | A public beta is proposed. |
| ADR-005 | 2026-07-19 | Treat AegisOS as a worldwide distribution and require qualified legal review before public beta. | Accepted | Licensing, privacy, consumer, trademark, cryptography/export, and telecommunications obligations vary by jurisdiction. | Engineering records identify obligations; qualified counsel determines legal conclusions. | A public beta region, legal entity, pricing model, or distribution channel is selected. |
| ADR-006 | 2026-07-19 | Release public beta only when the owner accepts evidence-based readiness gates. | Accepted | A fixed date cannot substitute for security, recovery, and compatibility evidence. | Release planning uses measurable entry/exit criteria and documented known limitations. | The owner proposes public beta release. |
| ADR-007 | 2026-07-19 | Prohibit AI from receiving unrestricted root, shell, arbitrary Binder, arbitrary file-read, package-manager, vault, backup, or network-administration authority. | Accepted | Local AI can assist explanation and intent translation but must not become an unbounded privileged control path. | AI actions require typed, allow-listed validation and independent audit records. | A new AI feature or privileged AI action is proposed. |
| ADR-008 | 2026-07-19 | Use verified GrapheneOS manifest tag `2026071500` as the Phase 1 source baseline candidate. | Accepted with build gate | The annotated manifest tag was verified against GrapheneOS's published SSH signer and pins a stable upstream tree rather than a moving branch. | Phase 1 must sync the manifest, capture exact upstream revisions, verify vendor/device inputs, and pass a clean reproducible emulator build before any AegisOS build is treated as a release candidate. | A newer security release is selected or source/build verification fails. |
| ADR-009 | 2026-07-19 | Block privileged AegisOS implementation until its capability boundary and validation method are documented. | Accepted | Least privilege must be designed before a service gains integration with Android framework or policy controls. | Every privileged service requires allowed inputs/outputs, denials, audit, failure, and test records before implementation planning. | A service interface, permission, or threat model changes. |
| ADR-010 | 2026-07-19 | Use staged repositories with `aegisos-foundation` as the governance record and `aegisos-manifest` as the future source-provenance authority. | Accepted | A full Android distribution needs separate ownership for governance, source selection, platform patches, services, release operations, and public documentation. | No empty repository is created before a concrete responsibility exists; cross-repository releases require explicit revision records. | The first source sync, AegisOS patch, release pipeline, or public-beta documentation starts. |
| ADR-011 | 2026-07-19 | Treat AegisOS as the owner's mobile control plane, organized around Observe → Explain → Choose → Enforce → Verify → Undo. | Superseded by ADR-242 (the thesis and loop restated after ADR-240). Accepted | This unifies Guardian, data controls, Worlds, network policy, recovery, and AI into one understandable product experience rather than an unrelated feature list. | Product proposals must fit the control loop, remain calm by default, and expose expert depth only on demand. | A flagship capability, product position, or interaction model changes. |
| ADR-012 | 2026-07-19 | Require explicit owner approval after all evidence-based public-beta gates are accepted. | Accepted | Public release authority cannot be inferred from a timeline, a booting build, or partial test results. | Public beta remains blocked until device, security, recovery, legal, compatibility, support, disclosure, and release-operation evidence is reviewed and the owner records approval. | A public beta is proposed or any readiness gate changes. |
| ADR-013 | 2026-07-19 | Create an independently testable Guardian reference component in `aegisos-services` before platform integration. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted with bounded scope | The owner approved a real, reusable Guardian core and reference flow while source sync, platform build, and device access remain unavailable. | The component may implement contracts, deterministic rules, typed remediation state, audit, Undo, tests, and a simulated platform gateway. It must not claim Android enforcement or include GrapheneOS/AOSP source, privileged framework changes, signing, flashing, or a source sync. Later Android integration belongs in `aegisos-platform`. | The verified source checkout begins, a privileged interface is proposed, or the component's contracts/capability boundary change. |
| ADR-014 | 2026-07-19 | Use `AegisOS` as the canonical product name and `aegisos-*` as every repository slug. | Accepted | The owner selected a single unambiguous product identity and repository family. | Product-facing records use `AegisOS`; future repositories use `aegisos-foundation`, `aegisos-manifest`, `aegisos-platform`, `aegisos-services`, `aegisos-release`, and `aegisos-site`. Existing technical paths, scripts, and source references must follow the same naming standard when they are created or revised. | A trademark, package-identity, domain, or public-distribution decision changes the naming standard. |
| ADR-015 | 2026-07-22 | Show bounded local Guardian incident evidence to the device owner. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted with privacy boundary | Useful remediation decisions require owner-visible app label/icon, permission category, first/last seen, retention-window count, rule reason, impact, verification, and Undo state. | Guardian retains only the documented local evidence summary for the owner-selected period; it never retains coordinates or personal content, and never exports evidence to AI, cloud, analytics, backups, or other apps. Settings own retention and clear-history; a new incident type opens evidence once. | A new evidence field, retention location, export path, or privileged UI boundary is proposed. |
| ADR-016 | 2026-07-24 | Use verified-state Guardian safety briefings for owner-facing Overview. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | Owner-facing Overview copy must never claim protection before verification succeeds, and must never keep showing a stale success state after a verification, rollback, or Undo failure. | Overview requires typed local `GuardianActiveProtectionV1`/`GuardianMeaningfulChangeV1` summary records, successful verification before any active-protection copy, explicit failure/Undo states, bounded local retention, and specific per-entry destinations; the read-only Overview resolver gains no remediation, package-manager, network-policy, shell, root, Binder, file, AI, vault, backup, or cloud authority. | A new summary field, change category, automatic-intervention rule, export path, or platform integration proposal. |
| ADR-017 | 2026-07-31 | Accept the dedicated private LAN build server as the official AegisOS build workspace for Phase 1 and later, superseding the phase-1 plan's local-workspace architecture. | Accepted | The remote server already holds the verified GrapheneOS `2026071500` source sync and the completed, boot-validated primary emulator build (see `docs/reviews/phase-1-emulator-baseline-review.md`); discarding that and resyncing locally would repeat a ~6.5-hour build for no provenance or security benefit. | `aegisos-manifest` is created against the remote server's resolved manifest, retroactively (after the sync), per its own provenance record. Task 5 (independent reproducibility) is deferred, not cancelled: the Phase 1 exit decision stays `Pending` until an independent build and unsigned-image hash comparison actually run. | A second reproducibility workspace is provisioned, the build server's hardware/trust boundary changes, or production signing is proposed on this VM. |
| ADR-018 | 2026-07-31 | Waive Phase 1 Task 5 (independent reproducibility comparison) and accept the single-build primary emulator baseline as `Accepted baseline` so `aegisos-platform` patch work can proceed now. | Accepted | The owner explicitly chose to accept the schedule risk of skipping an independent ~6.5–8 hour rebuild in order to move forward on AegisOS platform work; the primary build's provenance (signed/verified manifest, single clean build, boot-validated emulator with `ENV-003`/`SEC-001` passing) is treated as sufficient interim evidence. | No independent confirmation exists that the build is deterministic; a compromised or non-deterministic toolchain would not be caught by this baseline alone. `docs/reviews/phase-1-emulator-baseline-review.md`'s exit decision is recorded as `Accepted baseline (Task 5 waived)`, not a plain `Accepted baseline`. Any `aegisos-platform` patch applied against this baseline inherits that caveat. | A signed/production release is proposed, evidence of build-environment compromise or non-determinism surfaces, or the owner wants retroactive reproducibility confirmation before going further. |
| ADR-019 | 2026-07-31 | Approve `aegisos-platform`'s boot-identity patch set — `frameworks-base-boot-background.patch` (clear color), `frameworks-base-boot-framerate.patch` (redraw cap raised 12fps&rarr;60fps), and the fade-corrected `android-logo-mask.png`/`android-logo-shine.png` assets — for application to the Phase 1 baseline tree. | Accepted | The design was verified against the hash-matched Superdesign export (project `662fa8b4…`, draft `05aa247d…` v5) and cross-checked line-by-line against the real, unmodified `BootAnimation::android()` in the build server's GrapheneOS source, closing the compliance gap in `docs/architecture/repository-topology.md` (the original background patch had no backing ADR). The 60fps figure was an explicit owner choice over both the stock 12fps and an uncapped redraw. | This inherits ADR-018's single-build-provenance caveat. The motion-model gap is accepted, not fixed: the renderer's constant-240px/s linear scroll cannot reproduce the approved design's `cubic-bezier(0.4,0,0.2,1)`-eased, non-repeating, track-clipped sweep — that would require dynamic per-frame geometry and an easing evaluator in the earliest boot path, out of scope here. | The motion-model gap is revisited (e.g. a native easing implementation is proposed), the 60fps cap needs adjusting, or real-hardware evidence shows a thermal/battery regression. |
| ADR-020 | 2026-07-31 | Authorize an owner-performed OEM bootloader unlock on the physical Pixel 7a (`lynx`) engineering unit, to proceed past bring-up sequence step 2 (`docs/devices/support-policy.md`). | Accepted | The unit's retail identity and locked state were verified read-only (stock fingerprint `google/lynx/lynx:15/BP1A.250505.005.B1/13277630:user/release-keys`, `ro.boot.vbmeta.device_state=locked`, `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=green`); GrapheneOS vendor blobs for `lynx` were generated via `adevtool`; an ARM64 `lynx-cur-userdebug` build carrying the ADR-019 boot-identity patch layer is in progress. Unlocking is the mandatory prerequisite to flash any test-key image (support-policy step 3) and cannot be skipped or emulated. | Unlocking irreversibly wipes all data on the device and moves its verified-boot state from `locked`/`green` to `unlocked`, losing the stock trust chain until AegisOS completes its own signing/relocking path (support-policy steps 5&ndash;6). Per ADR-007, the assistant has no flashing/bootloader authority; the owner enables OEM unlocking in Developer Options and performs the `fastboot` unlock and its on-device physical confirmation personally. Developer Options and USB debugging reset with the wipe and must be re-enabled before ADB access returns. | The unlock fails or produces an unexpected device state, the device needs to return to a locked/stock state for any reason, or the AegisOS release-key signing design (support-policy step 5) is proposed. |
| ADR-021 | 2026-08-01 | Execute Phase 1 Task 5 (independent reproducibility comparison), owner-directed after ADR-018's waiver, and record the result honestly rather than re-waiving it. | Accepted | The owner requested the deferred reproducibility rebuild. A second, fully independent workspace (`<build-root>/platform-repro`: own `repo init`/`repo sync`, own `CCACHE_DIR`, nothing shared with `platform-primary`) was built on the same remote build server from the same verified manifest tag `2026071500` (signature re-verified, commit `8903f9d28abc61da0928d32b653ba4f9a88afd8c`) and the same target `sdk_phone64_x86_64-cp2a-userdebug`. SHA-256 of every `target/product/emu64x/*.img` was compared against the primary build's recorded table in `docs/reviews/phase-1-emulator-baseline-review.md`, verified with a scripted join. | **18 of 25 images differ; 7 match exactly** (full table in the review record). Per the Phase 1 plan's own Task 5 Step 3 rule, a difference blocks baseline acceptance until an evidence-backed cause is documented and resolved — this has **not** been root-caused. The failure pattern (fixed-content/empty-container images match; every image built from a live filesystem or containing `vbmeta`/verity metadata differs) is consistent with ordinary unpinned build non-determinism (image timestamps, UUIDs, verity salts) rather than tampering, but that is an unverified hypothesis, not a confirmed cause. `docs/reviews/phase-1-emulator-baseline-review.md`'s exit decision is downgraded from `Accepted baseline (Task 5 waived per ADR-018)` to `Rejected baseline (Task 5 executed, reproducibility failed, root cause unknown)`. This reopens the single-build-provenance caveat already carried by ADR-019 (boot-identity patches) and the ARM64 `lynx` bring-up work in progress on physical hardware; neither is retroactively invalidated by this entry, but neither should be treated as resting on confirmed-reproducible provenance until root-caused. | A root cause is established (e.g. unpinned image timestamps/UUIDs/verity salts confirmed and fixed) and a follow-up comparison matches, or the owner otherwise reviews and accepts the difference with a documented reason; or evidence of actual build-environment compromise surfaces, per ADR-018's own trigger. |
| ADR-022 | 2026-08-01 | Record the confirmed (partial) root cause of the ADR-021 reproducibility failure: an unpinned, date-derived build number, not build-environment compromise. Baseline stays `Rejected` pending a re-run with it pinned. | Accepted | Investigated on the existing build artifacts (no rebuild): `repro`'s `system/build.prop` carries `ro.build.version.incremental=2026080100` / `ro.build.date=Sat Aug 1 04:01:57 UTC 2026`; the primary build's recorded fingerprint carries `2026073000` — each matches its own build's actual calendar date. Neither build's log sets `BUILD_NUMBER` or `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`. `build/make/core/version_util.mk` in the synced source confirms `HAS_BUILD_NUMBER` defaults `false` and build-number generation "moves to soong_ui" — i.e. `soong_ui` stamps a fresh date-derived value into every partition's `build.prop` unless explicitly pinned. | Explains, with high confidence, why `system`/`vendor`/`product`/`system_ext`/`system_dlkm`/`vbmeta` (and their `-qemu` counterparts) differ: `vbmeta.img` signs a dm-verity root over that content, so any `build.prop` change cascades into it. Images without `build.prop` (`dtb`, `encryptionkey`, `super_empty`, the `vendor_ramdisk*`/`vendor_boot-test-harness` set) correctly match. This is a **partial** root cause: `cache.img`, `userdata.img`, `ramdisk.img`, and `ramdisk-qemu.img` also differ but were not individually confirmed against this same mechanism — they may share it or stem from a related but distinct cause (unpinned filesystem-image creation timestamps/UUIDs, also not fixed via `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` in either build). This is a known, fixable non-determinism source, not evidence of tampering or a compromised toolchain, but it does not by itself flip the ADR-021 `Rejected baseline` decision — no re-run with the build number pinned has been performed to confirm a clean diff. | A follow-up comparison run pins `BUILD_NUMBER` (or the `soong_ui`-recognized override) identically between both workspaces and either reproduces a clean diff (supports re-accepting the baseline) or still shows a residual difference (narrows the cause further, e.g. to filesystem-image timestamps). |
| ADR-023 | 2026-08-01 | Correct ADR-022: the build number is not the primary cause of most ADR-021 mismatches. A second, source-confirmed cause — an unpinned AVB/dm-verity hashtree salt, defaulting to `/dev/urandom` — accounts for most of them. | Accepted | Two repackaging tests ran directly on the already-compiled `<build-root>/platform-repro` tree (minutes, no rebuild), both with `BUILD_NUMBER=TESTFIXED001` pinned. Test 1 falsely reported a clean diff — invalidated and discarded: a `sed` bug destroyed the hash column, and both runs shared one `OUT_DIR`, so run 2 overwrote run 1 before comparison. Test 2 fixed both flaws (preserved run 1's images; deleted only the final `.img` outputs before run 2 to force a real repackage of unchanged compiled intermediates) and found **15 of 25 images still differ** with the build number held constant. `external/avb/avbtool.py`'s `salt` parameter is documented as "a hexadecimal string or `None` to use `/dev/urandom`", read from `/dev/urandom` whenever unset; no `BOARD_AVB_*_ADD_HASHTREE_FOOTER_ARGS` override for this target (`device/generic/goldfish*`, `build/make/target/board/generic_x86_64*`) passes a `--salt`. | Of the 18 ADR-021 mismatches: 3 (`system`, `ramdisk`, `ramdisk-qemu`) are explained by the build number (confirmed fixed by pinning it, per the repackaging test). 12 (`vbmeta`, `product`, `product-qemu`, `vendor`, `vendor-qemu`, `system_ext`, `system_ext-qemu`, `system_dlkm`, `system_dlkm-qemu`, `system-qemu`, `vendor_boot`, `vendor_boot-debug`) are explained by the random AVB salt. 1 (`super.img`) is a cascade (it packages system/vendor/product internally), not independent. 2 (`cache.img`, `userdata.img`) remain unexplained by either mechanism — no `build.prop`, not AVB-protected; likely a third, unconfirmed cause (filesystem-image creation timestamp/UUID). This corrects ADR-022's claim that build number explained the `system`/`vendor`/`product`/`system_ext`/`system_dlkm`/`vbmeta` family "with high confidence" — true only for `system.img`. Both confirmed causes are well-understood and fixable; neither indicates compromise or tampering. | A follow-up comparison run pins `BUILD_NUMBER` identically and passes an explicit `--salt` to every hashtree/hash-footer invocation in both workspaces, and either reproduces a clean diff (supports re-accepting the baseline) or isolates the remaining `cache`/`userdata` cause. |
| ADR-024 | 2026-08-01 | Confirm the third and final cause of the ADR-021 mismatches: `cache.img`/`userdata.img` get no pinned timestamp because they carry no `build.prop` and `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES` was never set. All 18 original mismatches are now explained. | Accepted | Source-verified in `build/make/tools/releasetools/build_image.py`'s `TryParseFingerprintAndTimestamp`: for a partition with a `build.prop`, the image timestamp is set from `ro.build.date.utc` automatically (already effectively pinned once `BUILD_NUMBER` is fixed, per ADR-022) — explaining why the AVB-salt cause, not timestamp, dominates for those partitions. For a partition with **no** `build.prop` (`cache`, `data`/`userdata`), timestamp is only set if `use_fixed_timestamp` is present in the image-property dictionary, which `build/make/core/Makefile`'s `generate-image-prop-dictionary` only writes when `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES` (or `COPY_IMAGES_FOR_TARGET_FILES_ZIP`) is set — neither build set it. Without it, `mkuserimg_mke2fs.py` never receives `--timestamp`, so `mke2fs`/`e2fsdroid` embed the real wall-clock time of that specific packaging step. | All 18 ADR-021 mismatches are now accounted for: 3 by unpinned `BUILD_NUMBER` (ADR-022), 12 by the random AVB/verity salt (ADR-023), 1 (`super.img`) as a cascade of those two, and 2 (`cache`, `userdata`) by this unpinned wall-clock timestamp. None indicate compromise or tampering. `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES=true` is a simple, single-flag fix (like `BUILD_NUMBER`) that requires no source changes. The AVB-salt fix is not: `BOARD_AVB_*_ADD_HASHTREE_FOOTER_ARGS` is set per-partition across several device/board `.mk` files (confirmed not `.KATI_READONLY`, so a `make`-command-line override — not a source edit — can win over each `:=`/`+=` assignment), but doing this correctly requires finding and overriding roughly 6-8 separate variables (one per AVB-protected partition, plus whatever governs `vendor_boot`'s `add_hash_footer` salt) without dropping their existing non-salt args (e.g. `--hash_algorithm sha256`) — not attempted yet, real risk of a mistake if rushed before an ~8h run. | The owner decides whether tonight's rebuild only pins `BUILD_NUMBER` + `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES` (fixes 5 of 18, leaves the AVB-salt family as a known-cause-but-still-differing residual) or also attempts the full per-partition AVB-salt override (targets a fully clean diff, more setup, more risk of a new mistake). |
| ADR-025 | 2026-08-03 | Record three further confirmed causes beyond ADR-024 (random `sgdisk` GPT GUIDs; unpinned `BUILD_DATETIME`; mismatched `OUT_DIR` names leaking into ELF build-IDs and generated-source debug paths), and separately confirm that most of what remains after fixing all of them is **not** a build-system defect: uncommitted local edits sitting in `platform-primary`'s checkout. | Accepted | Applying the full ADR-023/024 fix set (pinned `BUILD_NUMBER`, `--salt 00` on every `BOARD_AVB_*_ADD_HASHTREE_FOOTER_ARGS`, `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES=true`) left the 5 `-qemu` images still differing. Cause: `device/generic/goldfish/build/tools/mk_qemu_image.sh` wraps each partition image in a fresh GPT via `sgdisk`, which generates random disk/partition GUIDs unless `--disk-guid`/`--partition-guid` are passed; no AVB mechanism touches this step. Overriding `SGDISK` — as a shell env var, then as a `m` command-line variable — had no effect even after forcing a full Kati regeneration (confirmed via direct ninja-rule inspection: the real, unwrapped `sgdisk` path was baked into the rule as a literal string both times); `SGDISK := $(HOST_OUT_EXECUTABLES)/sgdisk` in `build/make/core/config.mk` is a bare `:=` with no `+=`, unlike the `BOARD_AVB_*` variables (which are built via `+=` across several files and only *appeared* to honor a command-line override because append-only assignment can never discard a seed value — not because Kati implements GNU Make's real command-line-wins-over-`:=` precedence, which it does not for this variable). Replacing the installed `sgdisk` binary with a pinned-GUID wrapper also failed: ninja's own `Install:` rule detected and silently re-copied the real binary over it before the image-build steps ran. The build system would not cooperate at any layer, so the fix moved outside the build: a small Python script (`pin_gpt_guids.py`) patches the primary and backup GPT headers and partition entries of each `-qemu.img` post-build — deterministic per-partition-index GUIDs, both header and partition-entries CRC32 recomputed — without touching the source tree. Verified byte-identical across two independent repackaging runs, including on `system-qemu.img`'s two-partition (`vbmeta`+`super`) layout, which needed the patch generalized from a single- to multi-entry GPT. With that applied, a full independent rebuild (primary fresh, then repro fresh, `BUILD_NUMBER`+AVB-salt+timestamp+GPT-patch all applied from the start) still left 12 of 25 differing, all cascading from `system.img`'s dm-verity root digest. Root cause: `ro.build.date.utc` is driven by a **separate** variable, `BUILD_DATETIME`, not `BUILD_NUMBER` — confirmed both in source (`build/make/core/version_util.mk:242-245`: `ifndef BUILD_DATETIME` → `BUILD_DATETIME := $(shell date +%s)`, real wall-clock time if unset) and in GrapheneOS's own public build documentation (`https://grapheneos.org/build`), which documents exporting `BUILD_DATETIME` from `out/build_date.txt` as a required step to reproduce a past build. Neither build had ever set it. Pinning it to an identical value on both sides made `system.img` byte-identical in an isolated two-run repackaging test — but the next full independent rebuild (both sides now carrying all fixes) still showed 12 of 25 differing, this time with content-level (not just metadata-level) divergence: extracting and diffing `system.img`'s ext4 tree (via `debugfs rdump`, since neither `sgdisk` nor plain `strings`/`readelf` were sufficient) showed nearly every compiled ELF file differed, and `apexd` specifically contained the literal strings `out-final`/`out-repro-final` — the two builds' different `OUT_DIR` names — embedded in its own bytes. Smaller binaries (`toolbox`, `awk`, `bootanimation`) showed the identical pattern in miniature: exactly 16 contiguous differing bytes (the size of an MD5-based ELF build-id) at a fixed offset, with the rest of the file byte-identical. Mechanism: `-fdebug-prefix-map=/proc/self/cwd=` (present on every compile action) normalizes the absolute source-tree-root prefix, so `platform-primary` vs `platform-repro` does not matter — but it does not touch the `OUT_DIR`-relative path segment that follows it in generated/intermediate file references (AIDL/Rust-generated sources, object-file paths feeding the ELF build-id and debug sections), so `out-final` vs `out-repro-final` survives into the compiled output verbatim. Rebuilding both sides from scratch with an identical `OUT_DIR=out` (the only change) reduced the differing set from 12 to 6 of 25 (`product`, `product-qemu`, `super`, `system`, `system-qemu`, `vbmeta`). Investigating that residual by extracting and diffing `system.img`'s and `product.img`'s ext4 trees found only 4 of 2393 files, and 1 of `product.img`'s files, actually differ. Cross-referencing those specific files against `git status --porcelain` in each source tree found the actual cause: `platform-primary`'s `frameworks/base` checkout carries three **uncommitted, locally modified files** — `cmds/bootanimation/BootAnimation.cpp`, `core/res/assets/images/android-logo-mask.png`, `core/res/assets/images/android-logo-shine.png` — while `platform-repro`'s checkout of the byte-identical commit (`git rev-parse HEAD` matches; `git ls-tree` reports the identical committed blob hash for each file) is clean and matches that commit exactly. `platform-primary` is not a "third, unfixed build-determinism cause" here; it is simply the one tree with stale local edits nobody reverted or committed, predating this investigation. Disassembling `libbootanimation_preapex.so` (compiled from the modified `.cpp`) confirmed this is consistent: byte-identical instructions to the reference build, with only linker-assigned PLT call-target addresses shifted by a small constant offset — a small link-time change, not different program logic. `system/bpfprogs` (source of the two remaining differing files, `cyclePerUid.bpf`/`dmabufIter.bpf`, 25 and 58 bytes respectively) was independently confirmed clean (`git status --porcelain` empty) in `platform-primary` — these two do not share the dirty-checkout cause and remain unexplained. | The build system itself is now proven deterministic wherever it has actually been tested clean: every partition and every `-qemu` wrapper not affected by `platform-primary`'s local edits (`vendor`, `vendor-qemu`, `system_ext`, `system_ext-qemu`, `system_dlkm`, `system_dlkm-qemu`, `ramdisk`, `ramdisk-qemu`, `cache`, `userdata`, `dtb`, `encryptionkey`, `super_empty`, the `vendor_boot`/`vendor_ramdisk` family) matched byte-for-byte between two fully independent syncs and builds once `BUILD_NUMBER`, `BUILD_DATETIME`, AVB salt, `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES`, matching `OUT_DIR`, and the post-build GPT-GUID patch were all applied. The 6 that still differ trace to two causes outside the build recipe: `platform-primary`'s uncommitted local edits (explains `system`, `system-qemu`, and presumably `product`/`product-qemu` via the same overlay-resource path, cascading into `super`/`vbmeta`; not independently re-verified after a revert) and two small, unexplained `.bpf` differences unrelated to it. Neither is evidence of build-environment compromise or a non-deterministic toolchain. This does **not** by itself flip the exit decision to a clean, literal `Accepted baseline` — no re-run with `platform-primary`'s three files reverted (`git checkout --`) has been performed to confirm a fully clean diff, and the two `.bpf` differences remain genuinely unresolved. It does support a qualified `Accepted baseline`: the build recipe is proven deterministic; the residual is fully attributed to non-build-system causes rather than left as an open reproducibility question. | `platform-primary`'s three modified files are reverted and a follow-up comparison confirms those 4-5 images now match (or the owner accepts the residual with this documented reason instead); or the two `.bpf` differences are root-caused; or evidence of actual build-environment compromise surfaces, per ADR-018's original trigger. |
| ADR-026 | 2026-08-03 | Generate and use the first real AegisOS-owned signing key (development channel only) to re-sign the already boot-confirmed `lynx-cur-userdebug` build's `vbmeta.img`, replacing AOSP's public test key. | Accepted | The device previously booted only under AOSP's universally-known test key, providing no real authenticity guarantee. `docs/release/supply-chain-and-signing.md` already defines the development channel as needing only limited engineering access with free replacement after exposure — no ceremony required at this level. Per the approved design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-01-aegisos-development-signing-design.md`), only `vbmeta.img` was re-signed (not a full rebuild); individual partition images are unchanged. RSA 4096 development key generated on the build server at `<build-root>/keys/development/` (private key never left the server). `vbmeta-dev-signed.img` produced with `avbtool make_vbmeta_image` using `--include_descriptors_from_image` per partition (this build has no chain partitions, corrected from the original design's assumption — see Task 1's findings), verified with `avbtool verify_image` both on the server (Task 4) and independently after transfer to the local machine (Task 5): 13/13 checks passed each time (1 top-level vbmeta struct + 12 partition Hash/Hashtree descriptors, all reporting "Successfully verified"); the resulting public-key fingerprint `7cf180b00535d72a70396995ab68b580aae05648` matched exactly between the server and local runs, and a full descriptor diff against the original vbmeta.img showed all 25 Prop entries and all 12 Hash/Hashtree descriptors identical, Rollback Index unchanged at `1783209600` — only the signing public key differed, as expected. Flashed via `fastboot flash vbmeta vbmeta-dev-signed.img` (not a full flashall). Post-flash `adb` evidence: fingerprint `google/lynx/lynx:17/CP2A.260705.006/2026073100:userdebug/test-keys`, `sys.boot_completed=1`, `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange`, `ro.boot.vbmeta.device_state=unlocked` (unchanged), zero Dropbox crash/ANR entries (clean boot). Per the design's own explicit deferral, independent reproducibility of this signing event was not attempted (same "waived, not done" posture as ADR-018, a separate instance of that gap, not inherited from it). Execution surfaced and fixed four real issues along the way, documented here per this log's established practice (ADR-021 through ADR-025) of recording the actual investigative path rather than only the clean end state: (1) Task 1 found the target vbmeta.img has zero chain-partition descriptors — all 12 partitions are embedded via `--include_descriptors_from_image`, correcting the design's original `chain_partition` assumption; (2) Task 3's first re-signing attempt, run with Task 1's original 25 `--prop` flags plus the 12 descriptors, produced a defective 50-prop, 10240-byte vbmeta, because `--include_descriptors_from_image` already imports each partition's own embedded Props from its AVB footer — fixed by dropping `--prop` entirely and adding `--padding_size 65536` to match the original's fixed 65536-byte size, verified content-identical to the original afterward; (3) Task 4 found two `avbtool` tooling bugs, neither a defect in the signed image itself: `verify_image --key` only accepts PEM format, not the AVB binary pubkey struct, so using `dev_key.avbpubkey` misrouted to an unsupported "ML-DSA" code path — fixed by verifying with `dev_key.pem` instead — and `avbtool` resolves partition images via `dirname(--image)` rather than the shell's working directory, fixed with a throwaway temporary symlink directory; (4) Task 6's first flash attempt used `fastboot flash --apply-vbmeta vbmeta`, misread from `fastboot-info.txt`'s internal flashall scripting syntax as a literal CLI flag, and failed immediately with `unrecognized option '--apply-vbmeta'` and zero bytes written (harmless) — `fastboot --help` confirmed the real syntax is plain `flash PARTITION [FILENAME]`, and the corrected `fastboot flash vbmeta vbmeta-dev-signed.img` succeeded with `OKAY`. | This development key now signs the reference build; any future re-signing or key rotation must reference this entry. Scope remains explicitly limited — no OTA, no `aegisos-release`, no alpha/beta/stable key, per the design's exclusions. | Before any alpha-channel key is proposed, before this development key is ever exposed/rotated, or if reproducibility of this specific signing event is later requested. |
| ADR-027 | 2026-08-03 | Correct two claims in ADR-026: the new key is not yet an enforced root of trust, and its "zero crash entries" evidence used a command that would report zero regardless of actual device state. | Accepted | A same-day final review of ADR-026 found both issues before any further work built on the entry. (1) ADR-026's rationale opens "providing no real authenticity guarantee" for the old test key, implying the new key now supplies one — it does not yet: the device remains `ro.boot.vbmeta.device_state=unlocked` / `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange` (both already recorded in ADR-026, just not connected to this implication), and no `avb_custom_key` has been enrolled via `fastboot flash avb_custom_key`. The bootloader does not enforce this signature as a root of trust; a clean boot demonstrates the image is well-formed and self-consistent, not that the device verified it against the AegisOS key. Enforcement is `docs/devices/support-policy.md` bring-up step 6 / validation-matrix item `LYNX-001`, not yet reached. (2) ADR-026 cited `adb shell dumpsys dropbox --print \| grep -c "^Dropbox"` returning 0 as evidence of a clean boot. Re-run and inspected directly: dropbox entries are headed `Drop box contents: N entries` and individual records start with a timestamp (e.g. `2026-08-01 20:56:35 system_server_strictmode ...`), never a line starting with the literal string `Dropbox` — the grep pattern cannot match real output regardless of device health, so the "0" was not evidence of anything. Corrected check: `grep -c "^===="` (316 total entries) cross-referenced against each entry's tag, matching `docs/devices/support-policy.md`'s existing established pattern for this device. | The underlying claims survive correction with real evidence: post-flash boot is genuinely clean — of 316 dropbox entries, 181 are `system_app_strictmode`, 131 `system_server_strictmode`, 2 `storage_trim`, and zero match `crash`/`anr`/`tombstone` (case-insensitive) — the same benign categories `support-policy.md` already established as expected for this device, this time with a real denominator. What ADR-026 actually achieved stands: AegisOS now owns and controls a real signing key, and the sign→verify→transfer→flash→boot pipeline is proven end-to-end — a genuine and necessary prerequisite for enforced verified boot, just not enforcement itself. ADR-026's text is left as originally committed (append-only, per this log's own convention) rather than edited in place; read it together with this entry. | Bootloader relock and `avb_custom_key` enrollment (support-policy step 6 / `LYNX-001`) is proposed or completed — at that point the "root of trust" claim becomes actually true and should be recorded as its own decision, not retrofitted onto ADR-026. |
| ADR-028 | 2026-08-03 | Adopt a single-operator interim role model for alpha-channel signing: the owner performs all five roles `supply-chain-and-signing.md` defines (source custodian, build operator, release reviewer, channel signer, incident lead), with compensating controls in place of true separation of duties. | Accepted | AegisOS currently has one human operator. The policy's roles table assumes multiple people and states what each role "must not do alone" — that guarantee cannot be literally satisfied yet. Rather than skip the ceremony requirements (as the development channel's ADR-026 explicitly waived reproducibility) or pretend separation exists, this records the actual model in force. | Compensating controls for this cycle: (1) independent reproduction on a genuinely separate environment (Task 4) stands in for a second build operator; (2) a written, dated self-review checklist completed after Task 5's verification and before Task 6's key generation stands in for the release-reviewer gate; (3) post-signing verification (Task 7) is run in a separate terminal session from the signing session, mirroring "the resulting signature is verified on a separate system"; (4) this entry itself is the recorded acknowledgment the ceremony-requirements section demands. This model is explicitly valid **only** for the alpha channel, single-device, non-public distribution scope in this plan. | Alpha-channel candidates in this plan are signed under a single-operator model, not full separation of duties — the integrity guarantee is weaker than the policy's default and this is a known, tracked gap, not a completed control. | Before any beta-channel key is proposed (`supply-chain-and-signing.md` requires beta ceremony access be "independently reviewed") or any stable-channel work begins (requires "designated custodians," plural) — those channels cannot use this model and need an actual second person. |
| ADR-029 | 2026-08-04 | Accept that the 2026-07-31 `lynx` alpha candidate is not byte-reproducible by construction, replace it with the already-completed, correctly-configured reproduction build as the new alpha candidate, and accept that finding without a second confirming rebuild. | Accepted | Task 4's independent reproduction (`<build-root>/platform-verify`, same-server independent checkout per Step 1b, full sync+build, 8h12m, 177452 ninja steps) found 8 of 12 partitions differing from the reference candidate. Root-cause investigation (methodology matching ADR-022 through ADR-025) explained all 8 with no residual — stronger than ADR-025's own 2-file residual. Two causes, both independently re-verified by the controller directly against primary evidence, not merely trusted from the investigation report: (A) the reference build actually used `OUT_DIR=out-preflight` (confirmed via `grep OUT_DIR` on the reference build's own log), not `out-lynx` as this plan's Task 2 wrongly recorded from stray fingerprint-stub files rather than the real build log — leaking into ELF build-IDs and generated-source debug paths via the same mechanism ADR-025 already proved on the emulator target (`-fdebug-prefix-map` normalises the source-tree prefix but not the `OUT_DIR`-relative suffix that follows it); (B) `USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES` was set on the verify build only (zero occurrences in the reference build's log; controller-independent `debugfs -R "show_super_stats -h"` on `system_dlkm.img` confirmed real wall-clock `2026-07-31 21:32:06` on the reference side against the fixed epoch `2009-01-01` on the verify side). A targeted single-module spot-check corroborated Cause A specifically for this target without a full rebuild: rebuilding the `toolbox.recovery` module in `platform-primary` with `OUT_DIR=out-lynx` (matching `platform-verify`) produced a `toolbox` binary with SHA-256 `3d274be480492a2e5d44341850c32c7eb8bda9df632a99989511532af56d1447`, byte-for-byte identical (`cmp -l`: zero differing bytes) to `platform-verify`'s own build of the same module — full detail in `.superpowers/sdd/alpha-task4-toolbox-spotcheck.md`, including a controller misread mid-check (an idle process snapshot landed seconds before the build's actual completion and was initially reported as a failure; corrected after the subagent's independent re-check) and a real, separate cost finding: that single-module attempt still consumed ~10G on a cold `out-lynx` from Kati/Soong full-graph regeneration alone, not the module compile itself, which drove `/srv` to 2.7G free / 100% full and required deleting `platform-verify`'s now-redundant `out-lynx/soong` (86G, owner-approved, useful output already preserved) to recover to 89G free. | Cause A is fixable by using a consistent `OUT_DIR` name. **Cause B is not fixable after the fact**: the reference build's ext4 inode/superblock timestamps are real wall-clock values with no build-input origin, so no future rebuild — of either tree — can ever reproduce the 2026-07-31 candidate's exact bytes. That candidate is therefore permanently disqualified from an alpha-channel reproducibility claim, not merely "not yet verified." Per Task 4's own text ("either fixed or explained with the same rigor those ADRs used"), the owner accepted this root-cause explanation in place of a second ~8h confirming rebuild — ccache was checked and found unable to meaningfully help (`<build-root>/ccache` capped at 5GB/90% full with an 18.7% historical hit rate, and `out-lynx` appears 2,025,265 times in the verify build's own ninja file, meaning a change of `OUT_DIR` re-keys nearly every compile action regardless of cache size). The already-completed `platform-verify` build — which already carries every correct flag, `OUT_DIR` included, since it is the one being adopted as the reference name going forward — is promoted to be the alpha candidate itself, recorded at `<build-root>/evidence/alpha-candidate-accepted-hashes-20260804.txt` (candidate_id `alpha-candidate-20260804-01`, supersedes `alpha-candidate-20260803-01`), independently hash-verified by the controller against `<build-root>/evidence/verify-task4-final-result.txt` before this entry was recorded. ADR-026's development-channel signing is not invalidated (reproducibility was explicitly waived there, same posture as ADR-018) but its provenance caveat now has this specific, documented reason. Task 5 onward targets the new candidate. This weakens one of ADR-028's four named compensating controls for the single-operator model — "independent reproduction on a genuinely separate environment stands in for a second build operator" — from a full independent-build confirmation to a root-cause explanation plus a single-module spot-check; recorded here explicitly rather than left implicit. Full investigation: `.superpowers/sdd/alpha-task-4-rootcause-report.md`. The plan's own Task 8 draft had pre-reserved the label "ADR-029" for the eventual signing-ceremony record; that record is renumbered to ADR-030 since this entry now occupies ADR-029 by sequence. | A rebuild of `platform-primary` with the verify build's exact flag set is later performed and produces a clean diff against the promoted candidate (strengthens this entry, does not require reopening it); or the owner later decides the weakened compensating control is insufficient for a channel beyond alpha, single-device scope (beta/stable explicitly cannot use this model per ADR-028 already). |
