| ADR-032 | 2026-08-06 | Add `android.permission.PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS` to Guardian's platform-integration permission set, beyond the three permissions ADR-013/the Guardian platform integration design originally scoped. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | During Task 4 of the Guardian platform integration plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-05-guardian-platform-integration.md`), `BackgroundLocationWatcher` was found to need a UID's live process importance (foreground vs. background) to correctly scope `REVOKE_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` to genuinely background access — the design's stated purpose. Verified directly against the real, synced GrapheneOS source (`platform-primary`, manifest `2026071500`): every API that exposes this (`ActivityManager.getUidImportance(int)`, `ActivityManager.addOnUidImportanceListener`) is `@RequiresPermission(Manifest.permission.PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS)` with no narrower alternative (`getBindingUidImportance` does not apply — it only works for UIDs already bound to the caller, not arbitrary apps). `PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS`'s confirmed protection level, `signature\|privileged\|development\|appop\|retailDemo` (`core/res/AndroidManifest.xml:7851-7853`), carries the `privileged` flag, so it is obtainable through the same `privapp-permissions` allowlist mechanism already used for `WATCH_APPOPS`/`GET_APP_OPS_STATS` — no platform-signature special case. Full research trail in `docs/research/guardian-platform-api-verification.md`'s "`getUidImportance` permission requirement" section. Presented to the owner as a plan-conflict decision (the plan's own Global Constraints capped Guardian at three permissions and required this exact escalation before adding a fourth) rather than added unilaterally; approved 2026-08-06. | `docs/security/capability-boundaries.md`'s Guardian sensor row is updated to name this narrowly: live process-importance reads only, explicitly excluding the broader usage-stats history/data-usage query surface that permission also happens to unlock. `guardian/app/AndroidManifest.xml` and `guardian/app/privapp-permissions-org.aegisos.guardian.xml` (in `aegisos-platform`, branch `guardian-platform-integration`) both declare it; `guardian-platform-integration.md`'s Global Constraints section is corrected to list every permission Guardian actually holds at this point — the three `privapp-permissions.xml`-allowlisted permissions plus `PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS` (also allowlisted), and `REVOKE_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS`/`GRANT_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS` (granted via platform signing, not the allowlist — `GRANT_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS` was added during Task 3 but never reflected in that section's text until now). | If a future Guardian feature would use `PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS` for anything beyond live process-importance reads (e.g. actual usage-stats history), that is new scope requiring its own review against `capability-boundaries.md`, not an extension of this entry. |
| ADR-033 | 2026-08-06 | Accept that Guardian's `REVOKE_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` remediation is now real, working, and independently verified end-to-end on the Phase 1 emulator baseline — a real privileged app (`GuardianApp`, `certificate: "platform"`, `privileged: true`), not a design document or unit test. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | Task 8 of the Guardian platform integration plan booted the real `sdk_phone64_x86_64-cp2a-userdebug` image built in Task 7 (same emulator baseline as `docs/reviews/phase-1-emulator-baseline-review.md`) and ran the full detect -> approve -> verify -> Undo cycle against it, cross-checking every result against `adb shell dumpsys package` rather than trusting the app's own UI or logs (`docs/quality/validation-matrix.md`'s new `GBL-001`..`GBL-006` rows). This superseded two provisional names/assumptions from earlier design/planning text with what was actually confirmed working: `ActivityManager.RunningAppProcessInfo.IMPORTANCE_FOREGROUND` (not the nonexistent `RUNNING_APP_PROCESS_INFO_IMPORTANCE_FOREGROUND` the Task 4 brief had used, caught by Task 7's real `javac` failure), and `PackageManager.checkPermission(String, String)` / `revokeRuntimePermission` / `grantRuntimePermission` against arbitrary target packages, which is real and correct but requires `android.permission.QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` (`protectionLevel="normal"`, `frameworks/base/core/res/AndroidManifest.xml:9284-9291`) — found only by running the real pipeline: `AegisOSPlatformGateway.snapshot()` was silently returning `granted=false` for a permission `dumpsys` independently confirmed was `granted=true`, because Android's API 30+ package-visibility filtering hides a non-platform-signed target app's state from a caller with no `<queries>`/visibility grant, with no exception thrown to signal the failure. Root-caused via `superpowers:systematic-debugging`, presented to the owner with the narrower `<queries>`-allowlist alternative explicitly and rejected as insufficient (Guardian must observe arbitrary third-party apps by design, not a fixed enumerable set), approved 2026-08-06. A second real, independent gap found and fixed the same session: `GuardianWatcherService` was never actually running — `android:persistent="true"` keeps the process alive but starts no `Service` on its own, and the Task 5/6 controller-designed trigger flow had silently dropped the "start the watcher" step the original plan text implied; fixed by starting it from `GuardianApplication.onCreate()`. | `docs/security/capability-boundaries.md`'s Guardian remediation row now names `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`, scoped explicitly to permission-state checks on the registered action's own target app, not package-content/install/usage-data access. Guardian's real installed permission set on the built image was independently confirmed (`GBL-006`) to match exactly six permissions — `WATCH_APPOPS`, `GET_APP_OPS_STATS`, `REVOKE_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS`, `GRANT_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS`, `PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS` (ADR-032), `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` (this entry) — plus only the standard Android-generated `DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION`, no extra or missing entries. The fail-closed verify/rollback design was proven against real behavior, not just code review: a deliberate, temporary, never-committed fault injection in `apply()` (skipping the real revoke call while still reporting success) was rebuilt, run, and confirmed to produce an honest failure/rollback UI state with the permission provably untouched throughout (`GBL-004`), then fully reverted before any further work. `guardian/app/tests/test_revoke_background_location.sh` is corrected to actually simulate the required "Protect app" tap (it previously only waited passively, which would report `FAIL` even when detection worked correctly, since Guardian never auto-remediates by design). Guardian remains emulator-only; no physical `lynx` validation of this feature has occurred. | Before `BLOCK_BACKGROUND_NETWORK`, `REMOVE_OVERLAY_PERMISSION`, or `FREEZE_APPLICATION` are proposed for the same real-build-and-validate treatment (each will need its own AOSP API and permission verification cycle, not an assumption that this entry's findings generalize); or before physical `lynx` hardware validation of any Guardian remediation action is proposed. |
| ADR-034 | 2026-08-06 | Fix a Critical finding from the final whole-branch code review of the Guardian platform integration branch: `GuardianActivity` was declared `android:exported="true"` with no permission check, while its own `detectBackgroundLocation()` path (via `GuardianUiController.start()`, vendored, unmodifiable) destructively consumed the single pending-detection slot on every read — meaning any other installed app could launch it via an implicit start and silently suppress a real incident before the owner ever saw it, and (separately) the owner had no legitimate way to open Guardian at all (no launcher icon, no notification; only manual `adb shell am start`, itself only possible because `exported="true"` happened to allow it). | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | An independent final-review subagent (dispatched per `superpowers:subagent-driven-development`, most-capable model, read-only against the full 19-commit branch diff) found this and eight further Important/Minor issues; all findings were independently re-verified against real source (not merely trusted from the review) before any fix: `GuardianUiController.start()`'s vendored source confirmed it calls `scenario.detectBackgroundLocation()` unconditionally, and the real built APK's manifest (`aapt2 dump xmltree`) confirmed `exported="true"` before the fix. Setting `exported="false"` alone was verified live to be airtight — even `adb shell am start -n` (uid 2000, shell) now fails with `Permission Denial: ... not exported from uid 10084` — but this also meant Guardian's own validation flow could no longer reach the UI at all, turning the deferred "no entry point" Important finding into an immediate functional blocker, not a follow-up. Fix: `GuardianWatcherService` now opens `GuardianActivity` itself (`startActivity` with `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK`) the instant it records a genuine `DetectedIncident`, from within Guardian's own `android:persistent="true"` process. Verified against real source before implementing, not assumed: `BackgroundActivityStartController.java:1296-1298` grants `BAL_ALLOW_ALLOWLISTED_COMPONENT` to any caller whose process state is `<= PROCESS_STATE_PERSISTENT_UI`, which a persistent app satisfies, so this background-activity-start is legitimately exempt from Android's BAL restrictions rather than relying on undocumented behavior. The destructive-read half was fixed independently of the exported change: `GuardianApplication.consumeDetection()` (nulled the slot on every read) was split into a non-destructive `peekDetection()` plus an explicit `clearDetection()`, the latter wired to `GuardianReferenceScreen`'s `onDone` callback (fires on "Not now", "Back to overview", and "Dismiss" — i.e. only once the owner has actually resolved or explicitly declined the incident, never on a mere reopen). Six further findings were fixed in the same pass: `GuardianApplication.onCreate()`'s `startService()` call wrapped in try/catch (an uncaught throw would crash-loop a persistent system process during boot); `test_revoke_background_location.sh` no longer prints `PASS` before its final foreground-location assertion runs, and its now-broken manual `am start -n .../.GuardianActivity` step (would fail with the same Permission Denial) is replaced with reliance on the new auto-open; a stale Android.bp comment claiming three Compose module names were unconfirmed (Tasks 7/8's real build has since confirmed them); an unused import; ADR-032's Consequences text corrected (it had wrongly implied `GRANT_RUNTIME_PERMISSIONS` was `privapp-permissions.xml`-allowlisted, when it is actually granted via platform signing — no allowlist entry exists or should exist for it); the Serena MCP server's local state directory added to `.gitignore`. Every fix was rebuilt and re-run against the real emulator before being accepted, including a full detect -> auto-open -> protect -> verify -> Undo cycle confirmed working end-to-end on the corrected build (not just "it compiles"). | `guardian/app/AndroidManifest.xml`'s `GuardianActivity` is now `exported="false"`; this is the durable, correct state going forward and future changes to it require the same security review this finding got. The remaining findings from the same review are accepted as documented, non-blocking residuals rather than fixed in this pass, each with a real reason rather than deferred by default: (1) `InMemoryGuardianAuditStore` (vendored) is genuinely in-memory-only, so Guardian's audit trail does not survive a process restart — a real gap against `capability-boundaries.md`'s audit-event requirement, not yet closed; (2) `BackgroundLocationWatcher`'s AppOps callback and the in-memory stores it feeds are correct only because `AppOpsManager.startWatchingNoted`'s no-`Executor` overload delivers on the main thread (confirmed against real source) — undocumented in code, a future switch to the `Executor` overload would silently introduce a data race; (3) detection covers `OPSTR_FINE_LOCATION` only, not `OPSTR_COARSE_LOCATION` — background coarse-only location access is undetected; (4) the single-slot detection holder (even with the peek/clear fix) still means a second real detection before the first is resolved overwrites it; (5) no `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on the Compose surface, so the Undo control is theoretically tapjackable; (6) `GuardianLocationFixture` in `PRODUCT_PACKAGES` was confirmed (via the real `device/generic/goldfish` product-inheritance chain on the build server) scoped to the `sdk_phone*` emulator product family only, never a physical-device tree, but is not further gated by `TARGET_BUILD_VARIANT` within that family. | Before `BLOCK_BACKGROUND_NETWORK`, `REMOVE_OVERLAY_PERMISSION`, or `FREEZE_APPLICATION` reuse any part of this branch's `Activity`/entry-point pattern, confirm the same exported/BAL/destructive-read analysis rather than assuming it transfers; or when any of the six documented residuals above is proposed to be closed, whichever comes first. |
| ADR-035 | 2026-08-07 | Port Guardian's `REVOKE_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` remediation from the emulator baseline to the physical `lynx` (Pixel 7a) device: build, sign, and flash a Guardian-bearing `lynx-cur-userdebug` candidate per `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-06-guardian-lynx-hardware-port.md`. In the process, fix two real build/signing-infrastructure defects and one real Guardian detection defect that emulator-only validation could not have surfaced, and leave a second real Guardian detection defect open and documented rather than resolved. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted (qualified) | Task 1 committed `frameworks/base`'s outstanding boot-identity residual for real (branch `aegisos-boot-identity`, commit `7d7001f9c4e4`), closing one of ADR-025/031's two open reproducibility causes. Task 2 wired `GuardianApp` into `lynx`'s product config (branch `aegisos-guardian-lynx` in `vendor/adevtool`, commit `43b73335`). Task 3's build hit two plan errors caught before they caused wasted work: the plan's literal `lunch lynx-userdebug` is not a valid combo (the real target, `lynx-cur-userdebug`, was confirmed via release-config artifacts already on the server from the original physical-device bring-up); and `vendor/adevtool`'s Task 2 commit broke `vendor/google_devices/lynx/adevtool-version-check.mk`'s pin of `vendor/adevtool`'s exact git `HEAD` (any commit there breaks it, not just content changes) — fixed by hand-patching the pinned hash, justified because that generated file is confirmed not itself git-tracked and the change was purely additive with no blob/firmware implications, avoiding the much heavier `adevtool generate-all -d lynx` pipeline. The resulting cold `m -j6` build of `out-lynx` (never built before on `platform-primary`) took 8h10m32s across 175981 ninja steps, consuming roughly 97G of disk. Task 4's signing diffed 11 of 12 partitions as different from the last accepted candidate — wider than the plan's own "expect at minimum system/system_ext" assumption, but explained by this candidate's deliberately new `BUILD_NUMBER`/`BUILD_DATETIME` propagating into most partitions' embedded build fingerprint, not a reproducibility regression. Signing itself found two real, previously-undocumented `avbtool` defects: (1) `add_hashtree_footer` needs the `fec` host tool on `PATH` (`out-lynx/host/linux-x86/bin/fec`) and, when it is missing, does not fail cleanly — it partially modifies the target image in place before erroring, corrupting it in a way a second signing attempt does not detect or reject, discovered only because the pristine build hash was independently diffed before trusting the signed output; (2) `add_hashtree_footer` needs an explicit `--partition_name` — omitting it leaves the descriptor's partition-name field blank for every image signed that way, and `make_vbmeta_image` silently drops all but the last of multiple same-(blank-)named descriptors when merging `--include_descriptors_from_image` sources, which in the first signing attempt produced a `vbmeta.img` with **no descriptor for `system` at all** (the partition carrying Guardian) — a security-relevant integrity gap, not a cosmetic one, caught before flashing by inspecting the full `avbtool info_image` descriptor list rather than trusting a "N/N verified" summary alone. Both were fixed and the corrected candidate independently AVB-verified 13/13 on two machines with two separate `avbtool` binaries, matching ADR-030's evidentiary bar. Task 5 flashed all 11 changed partitions plus `vbmeta` (`pvmfw` excluded as confirmed byte-identical) — a deliberate deviation from the plan's literal system/system_ext/vbmeta-only instruction, made necessary by Task 4's wider diff to avoid an AVB hash mismatch against stale on-device content for the other partitions. Fresh boot evidence: fingerprint `google/lynx/lynx:17/CP2A.260705.006/2026080601:userdebug/test-keys`, `sys.boot_completed=1`, zero unexplained crash/ANR/tombstone matches (the two seen mid-Task-6 were independently attributed: the test fixture's own permission-less first launch, and a harmless SystemUI StrictMode diagnostic — neither a regression). Task 6's real-hardware validation found Guardian's `BackgroundLocationWatcher` watched only `AppOpsManager.OPSTR_FINE_LOCATION`; real GNSS-backed fixes on physical hardware note `OP_GPS`/`OP_MONITOR_LOCATION` instead (confirmed as genuinely distinct, non-aliased ops by reading `AppOpsManager.java` directly, not assumed), which the emulator's simulated location provider never exercised because it notes `FINE_LOCATION` directly — masking this gap through every prior emulator validation including `GBL-001`..`GBL-007` and ADR-034's own residual-3 note about `COARSE_LOCATION`. Fixed by adding `OPSTR_GPS`/`OPSTR_MONITOR_LOCATION` to the watched-ops array; rebuilt, re-signed, re-flashed, and confirmed live via diagnostic logging that the OS-level `startWatchingNoted` callback now genuinely fires (observed for a real system IMS-service location request). A second, distinct defect was found and left open: even with the watched-ops fix, the callback still never fires specifically for the test fixture's own location requests, despite fresh AppOps access records for it confirmed present at the exact expected moments, and despite ruling out (by reading `AppOpsService.java` directly) four plausible causes — switch-op remapping (`scheduleOpNotedIfNeededLocked` keys on the raw noted op code, not its switch-op), `opRestrictsRead`'s `MANAGE_APPOPS` gate (none of the location ops set `restrictRead`), per-uid watch scoping (`startWatchingNoted`'s `watchedUid` stays unrestricted whenever `WATCH_APPOPS` is held, which Guardian's is), and the batched `noteOperationsInBatch` path (it still funnels through the same dispatch). The strongest remaining lead, not yet confirmed: `AppOpsService.notifyOpChecked()` silently swallows any `RemoteException` raised while delivering the callback across the Binder boundary (`catch (RemoteException e) { /* do nothing */ }`), which would produce exactly the observed symptom (zero log output, not even the earliest log line inside Guardian's own callback lambda) for a cause outside Guardian's own code. Confirming this would require temporarily instrumenting `frameworks/base` itself, a materially larger and more invasive step than the two build/signing and one Guardian-source fixes already made in this entry, and was deliberately not pursued further given the cost/reward tradeoff and this project's stated discipline against unnecessary low-level upstream modification — recorded as a real, well-characterized open finding for a future session rather than pursued to unbounded cost or silently left unexplained. | The physical `lynx` device now runs a Guardian-bearing candidate at three successive build-log versions in this one session — `1.03` (initial Guardian-lynx candidate, signed but superseded before being trusted further), `1.04` (the `OPSTR_GPS`/`OPSTR_MONITOR_LOCATION` fix, confirmed working for the OS-callback layer), and `1.05` (currently flashed; adds temporary diagnostic `Log.i` instrumentation to `BackgroundLocationWatcher`/`GuardianWatcherService` for the still-open second defect). **`1.05` is not a clean release candidate** — it ships debug logging added purely for this investigation and must not be treated as the terminal state of this work. `docs/quality/validation-matrix.md` gets real-hardware `GBL-0xx` rows mirroring `GBL-001`..`GBL-007`, each marked against its actual outcome (auto-open detection: not yet demonstrated end-to-end on hardware, pending the open defect above) rather than inherited wholesale from the emulator rows. `docs/devices/support-policy.md`'s currently-flashed-candidate fields are updated to this build number and fingerprint. Bootloader remains unlocked; this remains not an enforced root of trust, same posture as ADR-030/033. Separately, and newly discovered while patching the two Guardian source fixes above: the entire `vendor/aegisos/guardian` source tree on the build server is **not git-tracked** (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails inside it, confirmed before either edit) — every prior "merged"/"committed" claim about Guardian's own application source (ADR-032/033/034) refers to it being finalized in review and evidence terms, not to it living under version control on the server; a real source-durability gap against this project's own build discipline, not yet closed. | Before this work is picked back up, decide explicitly whether to pursue the `frameworks/base` instrumentation lead above or find an app-level diagnostic path first; either way, `1.05`'s debug logging must be removed and the candidate rebuilt/re-signed/re-flashed clean before any claim that Guardian's real-hardware `REVOKE_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` detection is fully working, and before `BLOCK_BACKGROUND_NETWORK`/`REMOVE_OVERLAY_PERMISSION`/`FREEZE_APPLICATION` are ported to `lynx` using this entry's build/signing fixes as precedent. |
| ADR-036 | 2026-08-07 | Root-cause and close ADR-035's open Guardian real-hardware detection defect (GBL-105/106); rebuild, re-sign, and re-flash a clean (non-debug) `lynx` candidate; complete the previously-blocked real-hardware remediation/undo validation. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | Followed `superpowers:systematic-debugging`: gathered evidence before touching any fix. A full-system (`-b all`) logcat sweep around a freshly confirmed AppOps note found zero framework-level signal — no exception, no StrictMode, no crash. A freezer hypothesis (Guardian's watcher process suspended when the fixture's op fires) was formed and cleanly ruled out via `dumpsys activity processes`: Guardian is `persistent=true`, `isFrozen=false`, `cached=false` throughout. With non-invasive avenues exhausted, added a single temporary `Slog.w` to the one `catch (RemoteException e)` block in `AppOpsService.notifyOpChecked()` (`frameworks/base`), rebuilt `services.jar` alone, live-pushed it to the running device (bypassing a full image flash for a throwaway diagnostic), and re-triggered the fixture: the instrumentation never fired, disconfirming ADR-035's leading hypothesis. The real cause was in the evidence already gathered but initially read past: `dumpsys appops`'s own output format for the fixture's GPS/MONITOR_LOCATION access (`duration=+Xms`, `Running start at:`) is the signature of a duration-tracked (`startOp`/`finishOp`) access, not an instantaneous `noteOp` one. Reading `AppOpsService.java`/`AttributedOp.java` directly confirmed this structurally: `AttributedOp#started()` — the method a real `startOp` call reaches — dispatches exclusively through `scheduleOpActiveChangedIfNeededLocked`/`notifyOpActiveChanged` (the "active" callback path, registered via the public `AppOpsManager.startWatchingActive(String[], Executor, OnOpActiveChangedListener)`), and `scheduleOpActiveChangedIfNeededLocked` has exactly one caller in the entire tree — `AttributedOp.java`, never `AppOpsService.java`'s own one-shot `noteOperationUnchecked`. Guardian's `BackgroundLocationWatcher` was registered only on the "noted" channel (`startWatchingNoted`); for ops that only ever go through `started()`, no noted-dispatch is ever attempted for them at all — not filtered, not swallowed, simply never scheduled — which is also why ADR-035's four previously-ruled-out noted-path hypotheses (switch-op remapping, `MANAGE_APPOPS` gate, per-uid scoping, batched-note path) were all correctly ruled out: none of them were the mechanism, because the noted path was never engaged in the first place. Fix: `BackgroundLocationWatcher.start()` now registers a *second*, independent watcher via `startWatchingActive` for `OPSTR_GPS`/`OPSTR_MONITOR_LOCATION` (acting only on `active=true`), alongside the existing `startWatchingNoted` registration (left in place, unchanged, for whatever may still reach it via a one-shot path); both funnel into a renamed shared handler (`onAccess`, replacing the now-inaccurate `onNoted`). Verified end-to-end on real hardware, each step independently cross-checked via `dumpsys`, never trusted from the app's own UI alone: fresh AppOps note -> Guardian auto-opens (`topResumedActivity=.../.GuardianActivity`, confirmed via two `onDetection: startActivity succeeded` log lines, one per watched op) -> "Protect app" -> `dumpsys package` confirms `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION: granted=false` while `ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION` stays untouched -> Undo -> `dumpsys package` confirms `granted=true` again. This closes GBL-105/106 and completes Task 6 Steps 4-6 of the lynx hardware-port plan, previously blocked. Separately, cleaning up the temporary framework instrumentation surfaced and fixed a real, independent signing-quality regression before it shipped: re-running `avbtool add_hashtree_footer` without an explicit `--hash_algorithm` silently defaults to `sha1` (a real AVB tooling behavior, not specific to this candidate) — caught only because the newly-signed `system.img`'s hash algorithm was diffed against the previously-accepted candidate's (`sha256`) before accepting the signing output, matching this project's own established "diff before trusting N/N verified" discipline. Corrected with an explicit `--hash_algorithm sha256`, then independently AVB-verified 13/13 on two machines (build server's `python3 external/avb/avbtool.py`, local machine's `/usr/bin/avbtool`) — full 12-partition cross-check on both, not a partial re-check, despite tight local disk pressure (a shared, RAM-backed `/tmp` briefly hit 99% full transferring all 12 images; managed by deleting already-verified, no-longer-needed local scratch files rather than skipping partitions). Before trusting the reflash, also identified and closed a real, non-obvious integrity gap: the scratch-overlay remount used earlier in the session for fast debug-iteration (`adb remount`, backing `/system` with a writable `overlay` at `/mnt/scratch/...`) persists its upper-directory content across reboots and continues to shadow reads even while the overlay mount itself reports `ro` — so simply reflashing a new, properly signed `system.img` underneath an untorn-down overlay would have left the device silently still running the earlier unsigned, adb-pushed files, not the verified image, while every AVB/boot check would still report clean. Resolved by `adb enable-verity` + reboot *before* flashing (re-enabling dm-verity enforcement, which is incompatible with the overlay and tears it down), confirmed after flashing via `/proc/mounts` showing a real `/dev/block/dm-N` block device (not `overlay`) and `ro.boot.veritymode=enforcing`, and via `services.jar`/`GuardianApp.apk` on-device `md5sum` exactly matching the pristine/staged expected content — not merely inferred from a clean boot. | Build-log version progression this session: `1.05` (session start, debug logging shipped, superseded) -> `1.06` (this entry's fix, clean: no Guardian-side trace logging, no framework instrumentation, `system`+`vbmeta` rebuilt via `m systemimage` with `BUILD_NUMBER=2026080701`/`USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES=true`, rollback index `1783209600` re-confirmed fresh via `avbtool info_image` rather than assumed, 13/13 AVB-verified on two machines, flashed, verity re-enforced, byte-for-byte confirmed on-device). Note `ro.build.fingerprint`'s visible incremental value still reads `2026080601` (unchanged) because only `system.img` was rebuilt this pass — the device-wide fingerprint is sourced from `vendor`'s (unchanged) build identity, confirmed via `ro.vendor.build.fingerprint`/`system/build.prop`'s own `ro.build.version.incremental=2026080701`, not a sign the new build number failed to take. `docs/quality/validation-matrix.md` gets `GBL-107`..`GBL-112` closing GBL-105/106. `vendor/aegisos/guardian`'s not-git-tracked state (ADR-035) remains open and unaddressed by this entry. | Before `BLOCK_BACKGROUND_NETWORK`/`REMOVE_OVERLAY_PERMISSION`/`FREEZE_APPLICATION` are ported to `lynx`: any watcher registration for a new capability must be checked against whether the underlying AppOps op (or equivalent OS signal) is a one-shot or duration-tracked/stateful mechanism *before* assuming a single watch API covers it — this entry's root cause generalizes beyond location. Also before further hardware work on this device: close the `vendor/aegisos/guardian` git-tracking gap, and revisit whether the scratch-overlay-remount workflow used for fast debug-iteration should be a documented, standard step (with its teardown requirement made explicit) rather than discovered ad hoc each time. |
| ADR-037 | 2026-08-12 | Accept Location Anchor (see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-location-anchor-design.md`) as a narrow, explicit, owner-approved exception to the product compass's anti-falsification boundary, scoped to the device's own outbound location signal only. | Accepted | During brainstorming for Location Anchor, the owner chose a system-server-level mechanism that makes the anchored location fix indistinguishable from a real one to every consumer on the device, not only location-sharing contacts — including third-party services that use location as an anti-fraud signal (delivery/rideshare trip verification, attendance/clock-in geofencing, safety check-ins). The compass's existing "AegisOS never falsifies ... anti-fraud controls" sentence plainly covers that case. The owner's initial response ("it's opt-in, so that's fine") was pushed back on directly: opt-in does not itself remove the third-party fraud-detection exposure — a delivery app's fraud system cannot tell that the owner chose this deliberately. The actual, narrower justification accepted here is that this exception concerns only the device's own owner-authored location signal (the owner's own data about themselves), not a third party's attestation, signature, DRM, payment, subscription, or identity verification, which the owner does not control and which remain absolute. | The product compass is amended (Daily-driver promise section) to carry this exact carve-out, naming this ADR. Location Anchor may proceed to an implementation plan. The two safety carve-outs inside Location Anchor itself are unaffected by this ADR and remain as designed: emergency location is always real, unconditionally; AegisOS's own theft-recovery lookup defaults to real location with an owner-facing opt-out. This ADR creates no precedent for any other feature; it applies to location falsification only. | Any future feature proposing to falsify attestation, signatures, DRM, payment status, subscriptions, or identity checks must not cite this ADR — it is scoped to location only. Revisit if third-party legal/ToS exposure from anti-fraud circumvention becomes concrete (e.g. a platform policy conflict, partner integration request, or regulatory inquiry), or if Location Anchor's mechanism changes from Option B to something that no longer defeats third-party detection. |
| ADR-038 | 2026-08-15 | Keep GrapheneOS's existing wipe-on-duress-PIN feature (`DuressPasswordHelper`/`DuressWipe`) active and unmodified alongside AegisOS's new decoy-profile duress mechanism (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-duress-unlock-design.md`) as two independent, coexisting duress credentials on the same device, rather than replacing wipe with decoy or pausing to redesign. | Accepted | Tracing the keyguard credential call path for the implementation plan (`docs/research/duress-unlock-api-verification.md`) found that GrapheneOS already ships a complete duress-PIN feature — `LockSettingsService.doVerifyCredential`'s `finally` block already calls `duressPasswordHelper.onVerifyCredentialResult(...)` on every failed credential check, and a match triggers `DuressWipe.run()`: crypto-erasing KeyMint keys and powering off. Neither the design doc nor the research doc had previously confirmed this mechanism via source, though the design doc's "Why alternatives were rejected" section already discussed the abstract idea of a destructive duress PIN and rejected it as AegisOS's *own* feature's behavior. That prior rejection was about what AegisOS's new feature should do, not about whether the pre-existing GrapheneOS mechanism should keep running. Presented to the owner as a genuine fork (coexist / replace wipe with decoy / stop and redesign) rather than assumed, per this project's standing rule to ask rather than pick the convenient interpretation on a security-posture question. The owner chose coexistence. | The design doc is amended with a "Coexistence with upstream GrapheneOS duress password" section naming this ADR. The wipe-PIN path is inherited GrapheneOS hardening and is not touched, weakened, or replaced — consistent with this project's standing rule to never regress a GrapheneOS mitigation even incidentally. The follow-up implementation plan (not yet written) must specify: how a single failed real-credential attempt is checked against both the wipe-PIN and the decoy-PIN without either check leaking the other's existence via timing or one shadowing the other; how the decoy path's need for a synchronous "matched" response (to actually dismiss the keyguard into the decoy user) coexists with the wipe path's existing asynchronous fire-and-forget hook inside `doVerifyCredentialInner` without changing either mechanism's guarantees; and a setup UX letting the owner configure two distinct, non-overlapping duress credentials without confusing which does which (the existing `DuressPasswordSetupActivity` flow is wipe-only and not directly reusable for a second, differently-consequenced credential). | Revisit if the implementation plan finds the two mechanisms cannot cleanly coexist inside `doVerifyCredentialInner` without a timing or collision risk neither alternative in this decision anticipated — at that point the choice between replace-wipe-with-decoy and stop-and-redesign returns to the owner with concrete evidence instead of a hypothetical. |
| ADR-039 | 2026-08-15 | Accept the Duress Unlock keyguard integration design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-15-duress-keyguard-integration-design.md`), resolving all three items ADR-038 left open, and retire two pieces of the already-merged `duress-unlock-core` module's original scope: its PBKDF2 credential-hash matching, and its consumption as a runtime Binder-called app module. | Accepted | Brainstormed through the three ADR-038 open items in order. (1) Dispatch order/collision: resolved by always paying the real per-profile Gatekeeper check's cost on every failed real-credential attempt — a registered decoy gets a genuine `verifyCredential(credential, decoyUserId)` call, an unregistered one gets an equivalent-cost dummy — which closes the timing side-channel a conditional call would create, and naturally avoids the wipe/decoy hooks shadowing each other since a decoy match already returns "matched" before the wipe hook's own `finally` block runs. (2) Because the real Gatekeeper check is now always paid and authoritative, `duress-unlock-core`'s own PBKDF2 hash comparison stops mattering for the match decision — keeping it would mean maintaining two independent security-critical comparison paths for no remaining benefit, so it is retired in favor of Gatekeeper's hardware-backed, anti-hammering guarantees. That, in turn, meant confronting where the routing logic actually runs: `duress-unlock-core` is a plain JVM/Gradle module built for an app process, but the hook must execute synchronously inside `system_server`, where an out-of-process Binder dependency on a separate app's liveness would be a reliability regression at the OS's single most security-critical hook — exactly why GrapheneOS's own `DuressPasswordHelper` is in-tree/in-process rather than a separate app. Decided to port the routing logic into `frameworks/base` as new framework-internal Java instead, with `duress-unlock-core` demoted to validated-reference-implementation status. (3) Setup UX: a new AegisOS Settings flow (not a reuse of the wipe-only `DuressPasswordSetupActivity`) that re-authenticates the owner, creates/selects a Private-Space-type profile, and rejects a candidate decoy PIN that collides with the configured wipe-PIN using the wipe feature's own existing `DuressCredentials.verify()` — real collision prevention without ever storing or comparing plaintext across the two setup flows. | `duress-unlock-core`'s `DuressProfileState` conceptually shrinks to `Off`/`Registered(profileId)` — the credential-hash fields and `DuressCredentialMatcher`'s comparison logic are no longer part of the runtime path; the implementation plan decides their literal fate (deleted vs. left as reference). No code changes landed from this ADR alone — it authorizes the design, not an implementation. The actual `frameworks/base` patch, new Settings activities, and framework-internal tests remain future implementation-plan work against the real platform source tree, which this repository does not hold. | Revisit if the implementation plan finds the framework-internal port cannot cleanly reuse `LockSettingsStorage` or the `DuressCredentials.verify()` collision check as assumed here, or if real-hardware timing measurement (still deferred, per the design's own "Testing" section) finds the symmetric-cost dispatch is not actually symmetric in practice. |
| ADR-040 | 2026-08-15 | Adopt a hardware-backed "Candidate B" dummy-cost credential check (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-15-duress-dummy-candidate-b-design.md`) in place of the software-only "Candidate A" sketched in Stage 1's plan, and record that ADR-039's own review trigger has fired: real-hardware timing measurement found the symmetric-cost dispatch was not symmetric with a software-only dummy. | Accepted | Stage 1 Task 1 measured two already-shipped code paths on the emulator: a real per-profile check (`unlockLskfBasedProtector`, real GateKeeper round-trip) at 38.51ms mean, versus GrapheneOS's existing wipe-PIN check (`DuressCredential.verify`, software-only scrypt) at 2.37ms mean — a 16.3x gap, non-overlapping ranges. A dummy mirroring only the wipe-PIN's software cost would be a measurable timing side-channel, so Candidate A is ruled out by real data, not preference. Candidate B instead adds a new special-user sentinel (`USER_DURESS_DUMMY`, alongside the existing `USER_FRP`/`USER_REPAIR_MODE` pattern) whose "pay" operation is a direct call to the already-shipped `SyntheticPasswordManager.verifySpecialUserCredential`, so the real HAL round-trip (GateKeeper or Weaver, auto-detected the same way `createLskfBasedProtector` already does) is the literal same class of operation as a real per-profile check, not an approximation. Storage deliberately mirrors `USER_REPAIR_MODE`'s plain-file pattern, not `USER_FRP`'s, after source review confirmed FRP's persistent data is backed by `PersistentDataBlockManagerInternal` — a dedicated factory-reset-protection partition unrelated to this feature and unsafe to entangle with. Provisioning is eager (at `LockSettingsService` startup, idempotent) with a lazy self-healing fallback, chosen over pure lazy-on-first-use to avoid any first-attempt timing tell. | **Updated 2026-08-16.** Code has landed and is emulator-validated: `frameworks/base` commits `e37c96c8` (primitive) and `e6e5746` (a real defect fix — see below), `system/sepolicy` `a99b535e`, `system/core` `c55eb4e7`, all on branch `aegisos-duress-dummy-primitive`. The `LockSettingsService` startup hook point this design left open is confirmed safe against real source: `onBootPhase`'s `PHASE_ACTIVITY_MANAGER_READY` branch, the same phase repair-mode's own boot-time maintenance already uses. Real execution found one scope gap this design did not anticipate — `system_server` cannot write an arbitrary new `/metadata` subdirectory by design (a deliberate GrapheneOS SELinux hardening boundary); fixed with a narrowly-scoped dedicated SELinux type mirroring the existing `watchdog`/`repair_mode` pattern, not a widening of the generic grant. Task review also found `provisionDuressDummyIfNeeded` never cleared stale GateKeeper/Weaver state before re-enrolling (unlike the real per-profile path it mirrors) — fixed and independently re-reviewed. Real measured dummy-check HAL round-trip: 147.03ms, ~3.8x this ADR's own 38.51ms per-profile figure rather than closely matching (plausible cold-boot-vs-warm-HAL explanation, not confirmed) — see the review trigger update. Physical Pixel 7a hardware validation (Weaver-vs-GateKeeper auto-detection, real-hardware timing) is in progress, not yet complete. | Revisit if physical hardware validation finds a materially different timing profile than the emulator (e.g. Weaver adds enough cost asymmetry of its own to reopen a side-channel), or if provisioning at `LockSettingsService` startup proves unsafe against real GateKeeper-service-readiness ordering. **Partially live, not yet resolved:** the emulator's own real timing (147.03ms dummy vs. 38.51ms real per-profile) already shows a 3.8x gap on the same platform — not yet treated as a confirmed trigger since the two figures were measured in different HAL warm/cold states, not a like-for-like comparison, but physical hardware measurement (Task 3, in progress) should either explain this gap or confirm it's real and reopen this decision. |
| ADR-041 | 2026-08-19 | Accept the alpha-channel OTA signing infrastructure design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-18-ota-signing-infrastructure-design.md`) and its implementation plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-18-ota-signing-infrastructure.md`) as complete for the ceremony's control-flow and fail-closed behavior, with the real sign/publish success path still unexecuted. | Accepted | Two-VM split-signing design (the build host produces candidates, the signing host holds the key and signs) was hardened this session (access, key-storage and network hardening of the signing host) and then implemented against real infrastructure, not simulated. A real 9-key + `avb.pem`/`avb_pkmd.bin` bundle (`script/generate-keys`) was generated inside the vault; `tools/signing/sign-ota.sh` was built to require an interactively-supplied, independently-sourced hash before signing (never a co-located hash file), run GrapheneOS's own `script/generate-release.sh`, independently re-verify the signature (`check_ota_package_signature.py`) before publishing, and always leave the machine powered off and the vault closed. Real execution — not code review alone — found and fixed several genuine defects a design pass would not have caught: a Unix symlink-crossing quirk in `generate-keys` (`cd` through a symlink sets the process's *real* working directory to the symlink's target, breaking `../../` relative references used afterward); a `/dev/shm` capacity bug (a full-tree tooling copy into a 984M tmpfs on a 1.9GB-RAM VM, guaranteed to fail against any real multi-GB candidate); a shutdown interaction with the access hardening of the signing host that broke the "machine always powers off" guarantee for non-interactive invocation (fixed narrowly); and, found only by a final whole-branch review after all five plan tasks individually passed, a Critical regression where the shutdown trap itself could abort under `errexit` before reaching `sudo poweroff` if an internal cleanup command failed — reproduced empirically before and after the fix — plus a real design gap where `otatools.zip` executed (via GrapheneOS's own `generate-release.sh` prepending its unzipped `bin/` to `PATH`) with the decrypted key bundle already staged, while being entirely outside the ceremony's hash-verification gate. All Critical/Important findings were fixed, independently re-verified by a separate reviewer pass, and re-tested for real against the corrected script on the physical the signing host afterward, rather than trusted on the strength of a plausible-looking diff. One independent-reviewer dispatch was blocked twice by a weekly usage limit; a controller self-review was substituted and disclosed as such rather than silently treated as equivalent, and was itself later checked by a successful independent pass. | The vault-resident key bundle, `tools/signing/sign-ota.sh` (tracked in this repo, deployed to the signing host's home directory, never into the virtiofs-shared paths), and a persistent the signing host network egress block now exist. Both fail-closed paths (missing/incomplete candidate; hash mismatch on either `target_files.zip` or `otatools.zip`; fake-content-after-hash-match) are confirmed on real hardware, including after the final-review fixes. The real success path — an actual `generate-release.sh` completion, a real `check_ota_package_signature.py` pass, and a real atomic publish to `<build-root>/signed/` — has never executed, because no real `lynx-target_files.zip`/`lynx-otatools.zip` candidate has been built (a full build is a separate 8+ hour undertaking, deliberately out of this plan's scope). Real disk capacity on the signing host for a full-size candidate is consequently unmeasured. `generate-release.sh` also produces factory/img bundles alongside the OTA update zip; the ceremony currently deletes those along with everything else in cleanup, preserving only the signed OTA — a real, undecided product-scope question, not something inferred from cleanup code. | Revisit before any alpha-channel OTA is actually distributed: run one real ceremony against a genuine built candidate, start to finish including a real publish, checking the signing host's real free disk space first. Revisit sooner if that run's disk usage approaches or exceeds the signing host's current headroom, or if the owner decides factory/img bundles should be preserved per-ceremony rather than discarded. |
| ADR-042 | 2026-08-21 | Amend ADR-039's decoy-profile architecture (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-21-duress-decoy-user-switch-design.md`): the decoy is a new AegisOS-defined `FLAG_FULL` user type (`USER_TYPE_FULL_AEGISOS_DECOY`) with delayed locking explicitly opted in, switched into via a new dialog-free `ActivityManagerInternal` path — not the `USER_TYPE_PROFILE_PRIVATE`-based profile ADR-039 originally specified. | Accepted | Real source verification on the build server found ADR-039's original choice cannot work: `UserTypeFactory.getDefaultTypeProfilePrivate()` sets `FLAG_PROFILE`, and `UserController.switchUser()` requires `FLAG_FULL` (or a switchable headless system user) via `doesUserSupportSwitchTo()` — a Private-Space-type profile fails this by construction, not a bug. Private Space's own defaults (`setCredentialShareableWithParent(true)`, `setStartWithParent(true)`) are also the wrong shape for a decoy, which needs an independent credential and zero indication a parent identity exists. Further findings: the public `switchUser()` path is asynchronous and by default shows a real "Switching users..." dialog (`mUserSwitchUiEnabled`, fixed at boot) — incompatible with a silent unlock; AOSP's own internal `UserController.sendStartUserSwitchFgMessage` is dialog-free and reused instead; `USER_TYPE_FULL_SECONDARY` does not get `allowStoppingUserWithDelayedLocking` for free (confirmed `false` by default, same as the device-wide `config_multiuserDelayUserDataLocking` override) and must explicitly opt in the same way Private Space does, or the design's core fast-PIN-to-landing-latency requirement would be silently defeated. Everything else in the 2026-08-15 design (ADR-039) — the keyguard hook insertion point, wipe-PIN coexistence, setup-flow re-authentication requirement, corrupted-routing-state fail-closed rule — carries over unchanged. | Owner-approved 2026-08-21. No code has landed from this ADR alone. The follow-up implementation plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-21-duress-decoy-user-switch.md`, 9 tasks: new user type, dialog-free silent switch, routing-state storage, `DecoyProfileHelper` keyguard hook, wiring into `doVerifyCredentialInner`, setup-flow shell command, emulator validation, physical `lynx` hardware validation and latency measurement, findings) is written but not yet executed. | Revisit if implementation finds the delayed-locking opt-in does not achieve fast PIN-to-landing latency on physical hardware (Task 8), if the freeze (`stopUserWithDelayedLocking`)/silent-switch sequence proves unsafe or racy in practice, or if `switchToUserSilently`'s bypass of `switchUser()`'s dialog/profile-type gating is later found to skip a safety check that mattered. |
| ADR-043 | 2026-08-23 | Resolve ADR-041's flagged "real success path unexecuted" and "real disk capacity unmeasured" revisit triggers: the real ceremony against candidate `2026081901` ran end to end for the first time and published a genuine signed OTA, independently verified on two separate machines. | Accepted | The 2026-08-20 storage design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-the signing host-ceremony-storage-design.md`) proved insufficient by roughly an order of magnitude when actually executed: real attempts against the built candidate consumed ~20-23GB inside `sign_target_files_apks` alone and were repeatedly killed by the disk-safety watchdog, never reaching later stages. Root-cause investigation (full account: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-21-the signing host-storage-rootcause-report.md`) found this was not a bug but GrapheneOS's own `sign_target_files_apks` unpacking the entire uncompressed target-files tree (measured: 8.28GB uncompressed from a 3.76GB zip, 8,705 files) plus 44 separate APEX payload re-signs, all before any interim cleanup — inherent to the tooling, not fixable by AegisOS-side scripting. Two real defects were found and fixed in `tools/signing/sign-ota.sh` along the way: (1) `sign_target_files_apks`'s and, later, `check_ota_package_signature.py`'s own `common.MakeTempDir()`/`common.MakeTempFile()` calls are bare `tempfile.mkdtemp()` with no `dir=` argument, silently defaulting to `/tmp` on the signing host's tiny root filesystem instead of the relocated pool, invisible to the watchdog — fixed by exporting `TMPDIR` to match the convention GrapheneOS's own multi-device batch wrapper (`generate-releases.sh`) already used; (2) a missing `PATH` entry for the `delta_generator` host binary in the same later verification step, since `generate-release.sh`'s own `PATH` export dies with its process — fixed by adding the correct otatools-unpacked `bin/` directory to `PATH` for that one command. A minimal, independently-reviewed patch (`tools/signing/generate-release-ota-only.patch`, applied to the signing host's vendored `generate-release.sh` since the signing host has neither `git` nor `patch` installed) makes the ceremony stop right after the real OTA and its metadata exist, skipping GrapheneOS's separate factory-image/`img_from_target_files`/`ssh-keygen` tail this pipeline never consumed — confirmed via real upstream GrapheneOS source that `ota_from_target_files` and `img_from_target_files` are mutually independent, so skipping the latter cannot affect OTA correctness. The owner separately grew the hypervisor-backed storage behind the `build-signed`/`build-unsigned` virtiofs pool from 70G to 129G total (24G to 81G real available headroom, confirmed identically from both VMs), which is what actually let a real run get far enough to expose the two `TMPDIR`/`PATH` defects above — neither would have been found by code review alone. | `tools/signing/sign-ota.sh` (final hash `27556ea6cb7f3f96816e1a25362898aa`) and the vendored, patched `generate-release.sh` on the signing host are deployed and proven against real hardware, not merely reviewed. First real signed alpha OTA: `<build-root>/signed/2026081901/lynx-ota_update-20260823134826.zip`, SHA-256 `615858baaf9db0608926d4acc8caa1b3c112a125b077a2712177c3c5ff90dba2`, signed-data digest `1dda2783ae4b3bd76f27ab258da8781c312524865d537b39b34e0401f9882906` confirmed identically by independent `check_ota_package_signature.py` runs on both the signing host and the build host (separate Python/OS environments, only the public certificate relayed between them, no private key material left the vault). Real peak disk usage for this first complete run was not precisely captured (live monitoring did not cover the run continuously through to publish) — bounded as "stayed under ~81G, watchdog never fired" but not measured exactly. | Revisit if a future candidate (a different device, or a `lynx` build with a substantially larger target-files tree or APEX count) approaches the new ~81G headroom — re-run with continuous monitoring through to publish first to get a real peak number before assuming margin is adequate. Revisit the `generate-release-ota-only.patch` if GrapheneOS's own `script` repository changes `generate-release.sh`'s structure around the patched insertion point (the patch's context-line match will fail closed rather than silently misapply, per its own dry-run-first deployment instructions). |
| ADR-044 | 2026-08-23 | Record closure of ADR-035/036's flagged `vendor/aegisos/guardian` source-durability gap: the tree on the build server is git-tracked, with a real, active commit history. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | This plan's Task 9 (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-23-session-hygiene-and-serena-fix.md`) was written to investigate and, if needed, fix this gap on a read-only-first, human-approval-gated basis. A read-only check (`[ -d .../vendor/aegisos/guardian/.git ]`) found `git` already initialized, working tree clean, 19 commits deep from baseline commit `afeb157` ("Baseline: current Guardian Android app state before residuals hardening") to `91cea5c`, on branch `master` — matching the Guardian residuals-hardening and visual-identity work already reflected elsewhere in this project's records. The gap was closed in an earlier working session that never recorded it here; no state-changing action was taken by this task, only a read-only confirmation, so Task 9's proposal-and-approval step and Task 10's execution step were both unnecessary and were not run. | Future Guardian source changes on the build server should continue as real commits in this tree, matching the practice already established for `frameworks/base`'s boot-identity patch (ADR-019/035). | If a future audit finds `vendor/aegisos/guardian` un-tracked or in an inconsistent git state again, treat it as a regression worth investigating (e.g. an accidental `.git` deletion), not a repeat of the original gap. |
| ADR-045 | 2026-08-24 | Deploy the private lynx OTA update server (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-23-lynx-ota-update-server.md`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-23-lynx-ota-update-server-design.md`, Tasks 1-5 of 9), with two real deviations from the approved design: manual `certbot --manual` TLS issuance instead of Caddy's native `tls { dns vercel ... }` ACME automation, and a broadened `a private /16 range` firewall allow-range instead of the design's single `/24`. | Accepted | Caddy's built-in DNS-01 automation could not be made to work end to end. Root causes, in order found: (1) `github.com/caddy-dns/vercel` has had exactly one release ever (`v0.0.2`, 2021-08-08) and does not compile against current `libdns/libdns` (confirmed real compile errors, and confirmed this is why `caddyserver.com/api/download`'s own build-from-source also 400s for this plugin specifically — the bare download endpoint with no plugin returns `200`). Forcing `libdns/vercel@v0.1.0` fixes the compile. (2) Neither library ever sends the `teamId`/`slug` query parameter Vercel's own REST API documents as required for a Team-owned zone (`https://vercel.com/docs/rest-api/dns/create-a-dns-record`) — every write from a Team-scoped token failed with a content-free `Forbidden (403)` (the library also silently discards Vercel's real JSON error body, compounding the diagnosis difficulty). A local patch adding a `TeamID` field/`team_id` Caddyfile subdirective, tracked in this repo at `tools/ota-server/vercel-dns-patch/` (see `docs/research/ota-server-caddy-vercel-notes.md` for the full investigation), fixes both the missing parameter and the swallowed error detail. (3) Even with `teamId` correctly wired (confirmed: the error changed from a bare 403 to a real authorization denial naming the exact team/domain/action, i.e. Vercel resolved the request correctly and then still refused it), a Team-scope token held by that team's Owner, with no granular permission step ever offered at token creation, was still denied — `{"message":"You don't have permission to create the domain record."}` — while the *same* record type succeeded immediately via the Vercel dashboard UI under an identical login. This API-vs-dashboard gap remains unexplained; ruled out during investigation: wrong team scope, domain ownership, account role, and (separately) a `Full Account`-scope token, which fails differently (`"invalidToken":true`) rather than better. Given the UI path is proven reliable, the server's real TLS certificate was instead obtained via one manual `certbot certonly --manual --preferred-challenges dns` run against real production Let's Encrypt, with the operator pasting the `_acme-challenge` TXT record into the Vercel dashboard by hand when certbot paused (confirmed working). Separately, the firewall CIDR was broadened after discovering the design's assumed single `/24` (the build host's own internal VM-bridge subnet) is not actually where client devices live: a workstation VPN session got an address in another `/24` and the physical `lynx` device (over the same home OpenVPN server, different pool) got an address in yet another pool — both would have been silently dropped by the original single-`/24` rule. `a private /16 range` was chosen because the design's own stated real security boundary is the home router's absence of port-forwarding (DNS-01 needs no inbound reachability at all), making this nftables rule "defense in depth" rather than the primary control, and all private RFC1918 space is equally non-routable from the public internet regardless of which specific `/24` a client happens to land in. | Certificate expires 2026-11-22 with no auto-renewal hook (`--manual` mode requires `--manual-auth-hook` for that, not configured); re-running the same manual certbot command before then is a required recurring operational task, not a one-time step, until/unless the Vercel API issue is resolved and native DNS-01 automation is retried. The patched Vercel Go modules are preserved in-repo specifically so that retry doesn't require redoing this investigation. Two unrelated real bugs were also found and fixed in the same session: `caddy run --environ` in the systemd unit leaked `VERCEL_API_TOKEN` into the journal in plaintext on every start (removed; the leaked token was rotated); and `file_server browse=false` is not valid Caddyfile syntax (`browse` is a bare flag, present-or-absent, not `=value`) and was a hard parse error, fixed to plain `file_server`. | Revisit the certbot/Caddy split before 2026-11-22 (cert renewal due) or sooner if Vercel support clarifies the API-vs-dashboard permission gap, at which point retrying native `tls { dns vercel { team_id ... } }` with the tracked patch would restore real automation. Revisit the `a private /16 range` range if a legitimate client ever needs to reach the server from outside that block (e.g. a different VPN pool), or narrow it back if the home network's actual subnet layout becomes fully known and stable (see the open question raised in this same session about the physical device's real at-home WiFi subnet, never confirmed live). |
| ADR-046 | 2026-08-24 | Accept the root-cause resolution of ADR-045's Task 7 flash failure (lynx AVB red-state "corrupt" boot, `docs/devices/lynx-ota-server-flash-rollback-corruption-incident.md`): two independent, stacked bugs in the signed `vbmeta`, neither in the device or in `system.img`. | Accepted | Following this project's own systematic-debugging discipline (root cause before fix, hypothesis testing over assumption): the initial leading hypothesis (device hardware rollback counter drifted above the reused `1783209600`) was investigated and found unsupported — direct inspection instead showed the flashed `vbmeta` itself carried `Rollback Index: 0`, the exact `make_vbmeta_image`-without-`--rollback_index` slip ADR-030 already hit once, confirmed via the signing host's own `.bash_history` rather than reconstructed. Re-signing with the correct index alone was insufficient: the corrected file still failed identically on real hardware, proving a second bug. Root-caused by direct `sha256sum` comparison: the 11 partitions Task 7 treated as "unchanged" were sourced from the 2026-08-04 alpha candidate, but the device's real last-flashed state (from the duress dummy-cost primitive's Task 3, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-15-duress-dummy-cost-primitive.md`, independently confirmed booting 2026-08-20) used Task 3's own full-build copies instead — a full `droid` build run without this project's usual `BUILD_NUMBER`/`BUILD_DATETIME`/`USE_FIXED_TIMESTAMP_IMG_FILES` discipline had silently produced different real bytes for 10 of those 11 functionally-unchanged partitions. Before trusting Task 3's files as the correct reference, cross-verified that Task 3's own vbmeta independently verifies 13/13 against them in an isolated directory (avbtool's `verify_image` was found to implicitly and silently resolve partition files by name in the vbmeta's own working directory with no ambiguity warning — a real, separate footgun hit and isolated with a symlink-only verification directory, not the cause of this incident but discovered investigating it). The corrected `vbmeta-ota-server-signed-v2.img` (today's `system-ota-server-signed.img` + Task 3's real 11 files + `--rollback_index 1783209600`) was independently verified 13/13 on two separate machines (the signing host and a separate workstation, different `avbtool` builds), diffed descriptor-by-descriptor against both the insufficient first fix and Task 3's own proven-good vbmeta before flashing, then flashed and confirmed via real device evidence, not just static verification: `sys.boot_completed=1`, `ro.build.fingerprint` incremental (`2026081401`) matching the value this investigation's own descriptor analysis predicted from `vendor`'s embedded `Prop:` entry, zero Dropbox crash/ANR/tombstone entries, `verifiedbootstate=orange`/`vbmeta.device_state=unlocked` unchanged from this device's existing posture. Separately traced (not left unexplained): today's signing used the signing host's vault-generated key (`cd7b74b3fb5c84cfc518ac961cc066d6473a4722`) rather than the alpha key this device has booted with since ADR-030 (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`) — confirmed via key-file mtimes (a `.orig-encrypted-backup` written 19 seconds before the "current" `avb.pem`) that this is ADR-041's documented deliberate key-bundle generation, not drift or an accidental swap; confirmed the two candidate fingerprints via AVB's native `.avbpubkey` struct format after an initial generic-OpenSSL-DER comparison produced a third, meaningless value. | The physical `lynx` device boots clean on `vbmeta-ota-server-signed-v2.img`, now signed with the signing host's vault key rather than the alpha key — the first time that key has been used for a direct device flash rather than the OTA pipeline ADR-041 generated it for. This is confirmed non-blocking only because the device remains unlocked (`verifiedbootstate=orange`); AVB does not enforce embedded-key identity on an unlocked device, only signature and descriptor self-consistency. Five new durable findings recorded in `docs/research/build-and-signing-engineering-notes.md`, independent of this specific incident: a full (not just targeted) build's non-reproducibility blast radius across functionally-unrelated partitions; sourcing a partial re-sign's "unchanged" partitions from a device's actual last-flashed state rather than the most recent or best-documented named candidate; an unopened the signing host vault's misleading `ML-DSA is not supported by the system openssl` error signature (confirmed twice, not a one-off); `avbtool verify_image`'s undocumented same-directory partition-name resolution; and computing AVB key fingerprints only via the native `.avbpubkey` format. | Before any future ad-hoc `vbmeta` re-signing that reuses "unchanged" partitions from a named candidate: re-derive the reference from the device's actual last real flash, not the most recent or best-documented candidate. Before `lynx` bootloader relock or `avb_custom_key` enrollment (support-policy step 6 / `LYNX-001`) is proposed: resolve which key (alpha or the signing host's vault key) is meant to be the device's actual enrolled root of trust going forward — today's non-blocking result relies entirely on the device staying unlocked, and a locked device would enforce exactly the key-identity check that was found to differ here. |
| ADR-047 | 2026-08-25 | Resolve a project-wide gap found while executing Tasks 6-8 of the lynx OTA update server plan: no `lynx` build this project has ever produced actually included GrapheneOS's real Updater app, because `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` was never exported on any of them; fixed, re-signed, re-flashed, and Task 8 (real device confirms the private update server) now passes on real hardware. | Accepted | Task 6's rebuild of `system.img` (carrying Task 5's private-URL Updater patch) produced a build that, on inspection of `installed-files.txt`, contained zero trace of the Updater app despite Task 5's source patch compiling cleanly and the build reporting `#### build completed successfully ####`. Root-caused to `build/make/target/product/media_system.mk`'s `ifeq ($(OFFICIAL_BUILD),true) PRODUCT_PACKAGES += Updater endif` gate — never set on any build this project has run (the 2026-08-04 alpha candidate, the Aug-16 duress-primitive build, and this first Aug-24 rebuild all share the same gap). A bounded search of `device/`, `vendor/`, `build/make/`, and the Updater app's own tree found `OFFICIAL_BUILD` referenced in exactly one other place (`vendor/adevtool`'s own state-collection build script, which already sets it as standard practice) — confirming this is expected GrapheneOS convention this project's own build invocations were silently missing, not evidence of a wider structural gap (e.g. no `vendor/grapheneos` overlay is absent by mistake; GrapheneOS patches AOSP source directly for this class of change). Rebuilt with `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` added (`BUILD_NUMBER=2026082417`), confirmed `Updater.apk` present in `installed-files.txt` before proceeding. Task 7 (re-sign `system`+`vbmeta`, flash) was then re-executed a second time against this corrected image, reusing the exact fix procedure ADR-046 established (11 unchanged partitions sourced from the device's real last-flashed state, i.e. Task 3's Aug-16 files, not the 2026-08-04 candidate; rollback index `1783209600` preserved) — independently verified 13/13 on two machines before flashing, confirmed booting clean (0 Dropbox entries) with `app.seamlessupdate.client` now genuinely present via `pm list packages`. Task 8's "up to date" check then hit two further, unrelated real blockers before passing: (1) an earlier test publish from Task 4 was still live on the server and, being older than Task 5's patch, would have silently reverted the Updater URL back to `releases.grapheneos.org` if installed — cleared, and republishing the metadata file as an outright absence produced a `404` the app misread as a hard failure rather than "up to date" (a real mistake made and corrected in the same session: an absent metadata file is not equivalent to a non-newer one, and the fix was to republish a non-newer entry, not delete the file); (2) a genuine device-side WiFi MAC-randomization/router-recognition outage, unrelated to this project's own infrastructure, confirmed via live-correlated `adb logcat`/`dumpsys netpolicy`/`dumpsys connectivity` evidence showing zero on-device policy restriction throughout the failure window — the blocking was entirely upstream of the device, at the household router, and resolved on the owner's own end. Once real connectivity was restored, the check passed immediately with no further changes: `fetching metadata for lynx-alpha` / `targetBuildDate: 1786724916 not higher than sourceBuildDate: 1786724916`. | Every `lynx` build this project produced before this fix was missing the real Updater app — a durable, general finding now recorded in `docs/research/build-and-signing-engineering-notes.md` (the `OFFICIAL_BUILD` entry under Soong/Android build system behavior) and as a standing cross-session rule: verify the relevant `installed-files-<partition>.txt` for any app whose presence actually matters, rather than trusting a clean build exit. The device now runs `BUILD_NUMBER=2026082417`'s `system.img` + `vbmeta-ota-server-signed-v3.img`, independently verified 13/13, booting clean, with the Updater app functional against the private server end to end (Tasks 1-8 of 9 complete). Also confirmed during this session, independent of the `OFFICIAL_BUILD` fix: the the signing host/the build host virtiofs mount read/write permissions are the *opposite* of what `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-the signing host-ceremony-storage-design.md` documents (`<build-root>/unsigned` is read-only on the signing host, `<build-root>/signed` is read-write there — confirmed via live `mount` output on both VMs rather than trusted from the older doc), which changed where scratch signing work had to be staged this session. | Task 9 (build, sign, and publish a genuinely newer candidate from this same `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true`-corrected tree; a real end-to-end OTA install test) is in progress as of this entry, not yet complete — revisit this record once it finishes. Also revisit if a follow-up audit (requested by the owner, in progress as of this entry) finds another similarly-gated `PRODUCT_PACKAGES` conditional silently affecting a security- or function-relevant component beyond Updater. |
| ADR-048 | 2026-08-25 | Accept `tools/signing/sign-baseline-images.sh` (the otacerts/test-key incident's fix tooling, `docs/devices/lynx-ota-otacerts-testkey-incident.md`) as fixed and working after five real bugs; reject the "swap only `system.img`, keep the device's other 11 partitions unchanged" baseline-flash approach the whole fix plan was built on, after a real flash produced a cross-partition package-signature crash; device reverted to its last known-good state, safe but not fixed. | Accepted | Code review plus real execution against candidate `2026082507` found and fixed five bugs across four ceremony runs, each exposed only once the previous one let the script run further: (1) `/dev/shm` disk exhaustion during the script's own verification step, reproduced and fixed by relocating that step's working directory to the larger shared pool; (2) the same class of write-error-on-full-disk could still recur unprotected at the new location and had no non-interactive-safe handling — fixed with a real `df`-based precheck, `</dev/null` on the extraction, and explicit failure logging, all reproduced locally with `ulimit -f` before trusting the fix; (3) `avbtool verify_image` requires every one of this device's 12 partition images present in the same directory as `vbmeta.img`, not just `system.img` (confirmed by reading AOSP's real `external/avb/avbtool.py` — `desc.verify()` is called unconditionally for every descriptor, no CLI flag skips it) — the original "isolate just two files" design had misread `docs/research/build-and-signing-engineering-notes.md`'s existing same-directory-footgun finding as calling for a *minimal* file set, when the established fix there was actually an isolated directory of *all* correctly-named partition images; (4) the otacerts.zip path the script checked, `META/otacerts.zip`, was never real — confirmed by reading AOSP's real `sign_target_files_apks.py` (`ReplaceOtaKeys`/`WriteOtacerts` rewrite whatever path already exists in the *input* zip ending `/otacerts.zip`, no hardcoded path) and by directly listing the real candidate's own zip contents (`SYSTEM/etc/security/otacerts.zip`, the path `RecoverySystem.verifyPackage()` actually reads); (5) the test-key detection's string match (`*"CN=Android"*`) would have missed a real leftover test cert on the signing host's actual OpenSSL, which formats `-subject` output with spaces around `=` unlike the OpenSSL version an earlier local reproduction had used — found by inspection after a real run already succeeded, fixed by stripping whitespace before matching. All five verified against real upstream source or real local reproduction, not assumed. The fourth, fully-corrected ceremony run then completed cleanly end to end: AVB verification passed, `otacerts.zip` confirmed carrying the real release cert (`CN = GrapheneOS`, not `CN = Android`), image bundle published. That `system.img` was re-signed (fresh `add_hashtree_footer` + `make_vbmeta_image`, rollback index `1783209600` freshly re-confirmed against the real currently-flashed `vbmeta-v3.img` rather than reused from memory, 11 unchanged partitions sourced from the same `duress-dummy-task3-signing` evidence ADR-046/047 already proved correct, sha256-verified byte-identical before use) and independently verified 13/13 on two separate machines before flashing — same bar as every prior ceremony in this project. The flash itself and AVB verification were both clean. The device still never reached the lock screen: `adb logcat` (device stayed reachable, this was a crash loop, not a hang) showed `system_server` repeatedly crashing with `IllegalStateException: Signature mismatch on system package com.google.android.iwlan for shared user ... android.uid.system/1000`. Root cause, one level up from AVB: Android's package manager requires every APK sharing a `sharedUserId` to carry the *same* signing certificate regardless of which partition it lives on. `sign_target_files_apks` had swapped every `system`-partition APK's signature to the vault's real release key; `vendor` (left unchanged, carrying `com.google.android.iwlan`, part of the same shared UID) still carried whatever key Task 3's 2026-08-16 build used. Neither AVB `verify_image` nor the otacerts subject check could have caught this — both passed cleanly — because cross-partition package-signature consistency is a third, independent trust mechanism neither one checks. Recovered by re-flashing the last known-good `system.img`/`vbmeta-v3.img` pair (kept locally, already proven to boot clean before this session); confirmed clean: `ro.build.version.incremental` back to `2026081401`, 0 Dropbox entries. | `tools/signing/sign-baseline-images.sh` is now a correct, working, real-hardware-validated tool for producing a release-signed image bundle from a candidate — that part of this incident's fix is durably complete (final hash `0c7c93294e7f6d10881abf92eb7b37de`; the whitespace fix lands before any future run, not retroactively). The "splice a newly-signed `system.img` onto the device's otherwise-unchanged partitions" design this whole fix plan assumed, however, cannot work as designed and is rejected — a real, reproduced, general finding recorded in `docs/research/build-and-signing-engineering-notes.md` and `docs/devices/lynx-ota-otacerts-testkey-incident.md`: cross-partition shared-UID package-signature consistency is a genuine third constraint, independent of AVB and of whole-package release signing, invisible to every verification this project has used until an actual boot is attempted. The physical device is back on `BUILD_NUMBER=2026082417`'s known-good `system.img`/`vbmeta-ota-server-signed-v3.img` (ADR-047's state), confirmed booting clean — safe, stable, but the otacerts/test-key incident remains genuinely open; the already-published OTA (build `20260825082317`) needs no changes, only the device's trust store does. | Before any further baseline re-flash attempt: decide and design (not improvise ad hoc) whether the fix release-signs every partition carrying a package in a shared-UID group crossing partition boundaries (at minimum `vendor`, not yet fully enumerated which others) or instead builds and signs one fully internally-consistent 12-partition candidate end to end rather than splicing a new partition onto old ones. Revisit `sign-baseline-images.sh` itself only if a future real run surfaces a sixth defect this review didn't find. |
