Owner confirmed this choice before execution. Real device flash confirms the fix. | Accepted | Before flashing, the run-4 bundle (`<build-root>/signed/2026082507/lynx-img-20260825165502.zip`, ADR-048's real success) was inspected read-only, no vault involved: `unzip -l` confirmed it contains all 12 AVB-relevant partition images from one single `sign_target_files_apks`-processed build (plus unrelated OEM firmware blobs and `super_empty.img`, correctly excluded from this fix — those are AOSP build pass-throughs, not part of this project's signing domain). Contrary to the caution recorded when `sign-baseline-images.sh` was designed ("not trusting `img_from_target_files`'s own auto-generated `vbmeta.img` as-is, since its rollback-index handling is unverified"), direct inspection found the bundle's own `vbmeta.img` was already correctly signed: public key `cd7b74b3fb5c84cfc518ac961cc066d6473a4722` and rollback index `1783209600` both matched the device's currently-flashed, already-trusted `vbmeta-v3.img` exactly (confirmed by running `avbtool info_image` on both, not assumed). This means ADR-048's real flash failure was never an AVB or key problem — the earlier manual `add_hashtree_footer` re-sign of just `system.img` was unnecessary rework; the actual defect was solely the 11-old-plus-1-new partition mix. The full 13-file set (`vbmeta.img` + 12 partition images) was independently verified 13/13 on two machines (the build host's own `avbtool`, and a second copy on this workstation after an sha256-confirmed byte-identical `scp` transfer) before the owner flashed it via `fastboot` (all commands run by the owner directly, per this project's standing practice, never via an agent's Bash tool). Real result, from the owner's own terminal output: all 13 `fastboot flash` operations `OKAY`, clean reboot, `ro.build.version.incremental=2026082507` (the device now reflects `vendor`'s real state too, unlike prior partial-flash sessions where only `system` changed), 0 Dropbox entries, and `adb pull`+`openssl x509` on the live device's own `/system/etc/security/otacerts.zip` shows `subject=CN=GrapheneOS` — the AOSP test cert is gone from the device's real trusted store. | The otacerts/test-key security gap (device trusting anyone with a copy of AOSP source to sign an OTA) is closed on the physical device, confirmed by direct on-device evidence, not inferred from a clean flash exit code. `sign-baseline-images.sh` itself needed no changes for this — the fix was entirely in what got flashed (all 12 coherent partitions, not a splice), not in how the bundle was produced. A durable finding for `docs/research/build-and-signing-engineering-notes.md`: `img_from_target_files` output, when driven through a properly `--avb_*`-keyed release-signing ceremony, can already carry a correctly-signed `vbmeta.img` with the right rollback index — the safe default is still to verify this directly (`avbtool info_image`/`verify_image`) rather than assume it, but it should not be assumed *wrong* either. | ADR-050 | 2026-08-26 | Complete Task 9 of the lynx OTA update server plan for real: a genuinely newer candidate (`2026082611`), built, signed, published, and installed end to end through the Updater app itself — the first time this project has ever completed a real, app-driven OTA install. Fixed a real, previously-undiscovered bug in `tools/signing/publish-ota.sh` found along the way. | Accepted | Built candidate `2026082611` (`OFFICIAL_BUILD=true`, fresh `BUILD_NUMBER`/`BUILD_DATETIME`, `m vendorbootimage vendorkernelbootimage target-files-package otatools-package`), confirmed `Updater`/`Auditor`/`PdfViewerGOS`/`Seedvault` all present in the real `target_files.zip` directly (`unzip -l`, not `installed-files.txt`, which this build target doesn't produce). Staged `lynx-target_files.zip`/`lynx-otatools.zip` to `<build-root>/unsigned/2026082611/`; the owner ran `sign-ota.sh 2026082611` on the signing host with both hashes independently re-computed on the build host, matching this project's established dual-hash-gate bar — ceremony succeeded, vault closed, machine powered off automatically. The first publish attempt (`publish-ota.sh 2026082611 20260826111823`, using sign-ota.sh's own internally-generated release timestamp as the second argument, matching the only precedent this project had) let the app download and independently verify the OTA package's whole-package signature successfully (real, positive confirmation the otacerts/ADR-049 fix works end to end) but then failed with `GeneralSecurityException: incremental does not match server metadata` from `app.seamlessupdate.client.Service.onDownloadFinished` — the first time any real device check in this project's history had ever reached that code path, since every earlier attempt failed earlier (otacerts) or exited early ("up to date"). Root-caused by reading the real `Service.java` source directly rather than guessing: the app compares the OTA package's own embedded `post-build-incremental` metadata against the server's published metadata field, and separately constructs the download filename as `<device>-ota_update-<that incremental>.zip` — both must equal the real Android `ro.build.version.incremental` (`2026082611`), not `sign-ota.sh`'s unrelated internal release-build timestamp (`20260826111823`) that only exists to name the signed file on the signing host's side. `publish-ota.sh` had conflated these two distinct numbers into one CLI argument since it was first written; never caught before because no real installation attempt had ever exercised this check. Fixed `publish-ota.sh` to read the real incremental directly from `target_files.zip`'s `SYSTEM/build.prop` (already opened for `ro.build.date.utc`) and use it for both the served filename and the metadata's first field, keeping the CLI argument scoped to locating the signed file by its real on-disk name. Re-deployed and re-published; the app then genuinely completed a full download, signature verification, payload application to the inactive A/B slot, and — after the owner triggered a real reboot — a real slot switch (`ro.boot.slot_suffix` confirmed `_b`→`_a`) into the new build, confirmed via direct device evidence: `ro.build.version.incremental=2026082611`, `ro.build.date.utc=1787742365` (exact match), 0 Dropbox entries, the Updater app's own `waiting_for_reboot` flag correctly reset to `false` by `BootReceiver` on the real boot, and `otacerts.zip` still `CN=GrapheneOS`. A false-positive "installed" report was caught mid-session: the app's own UI/behavior after payload application (before any reboot) reads as complete, but `waiting_for_reboot` remaining `true` in the app's real persisted preferences proved no reboot had actually occurred yet — the fix was simply asking the owner to reboot, not a real defect. | Task 9, and with it the entire `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-23-lynx-ota-update-server.md` plan (all 9 tasks), is complete for the first time: a real signed OTA, built from this project's own source, delivered over the private LAN-only server, and installed through the device's normal update mechanism, with a fully closed trust chain (AVB, whole-package release signing, and now a confirmed real install) all verified on physical hardware. `publish-ota.sh`'s fix is durable and applies to every future publish, not just this one. A UI/state-only "installed" claim from the app is not sufficient evidence of a completed update on its own — check the app's own persisted `waiting_for_reboot` preference (or equivalent) and the real active A/B slot before trusting it. | If a future device or build ever needs an actual `vendor.img` change delivered via this same pipeline (this candidate's OTA did not rebuild `vendor.img` itself, only `vendorboot`/`vendorkernelboot`), re-verify `publish-ota.sh`'s incremental-reading logic still resolves correctly against whichever partition's build.prop the device's live property system prioritizes — this fix reads directly from the target-files zip's `SYSTEM/build.prop`, sidestepping the vendor-vs-system property-merge quirk already documented in the rollback-corruption incident, but that quirk should be re-confirmed non-blocking if the delivery mechanism itself ever changes. |

No emulator or physical device has exercised the real match/decoy-match/wrong-PIN/wipe-PIN sequence yet. | Tasks 7-9 (emulator validation, physical `lynx` hardware validation and latency measurement, recording findings) remain not started — do not treat the mechanism as working until Task 7's real emulator evidence exists. If a future patch review finds the plan's own sample code diverges from real source again (as Task 4's did twice), treat that as a standing signal to verify every plan-supplied code sample against real source before transcribing it, not just when line numbers look stale. |
| ADR-057 | 2026-08-27 | Adopt the Guardian self-protection and tamper-evident audit log design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-guardian-self-protection-design.md`), resuming subsystems G (self-protection) and E (tamper-evident audit log) from the 2026-08-08 sensor-coverage decomposition as Phase 1 of a five-phase Guardian maximal-safety roadmap. | Superseded by ADR-240. Accepted | The owner asked that Guardian's highest priority be keeping the device owner maximally safe, "state of the art," with full latitude to change Guardian's architecture. Rather than invent a new scope, the existing `2026-08-08-guardian-sensor-coverage-design.md` decomposition (subsystems A-G) was found and resumed; that document's own Risks section already flagged subsystem G as needing "its own design immediately after this one," which never happened. A live Phase 0 audit re-checked that document's blocking "Wave 0" finding (Guardian's source existing as three diverging copies) against the real `aegisos-platform` tree, the real `aegisos-services` tree (`git merge-base --is-ancestor guardian-residuals-hardening master` → `YES_MERGED`), and a live read-only SSH check of the build server's `vendor/aegisos/guardian` git tree (clean, actively re-vendored from `aegisos-services`) — confirming the fragmentation is resolved as of this date, so this design proceeds without a separate consolidation step. | Before implementation: a new `capability-boundaries.md` row for the external watchdog component; new `threat-model.md` abuse cases for input-flood denial of service and audit-log tampering by a privilege-escalated attacker; new `guardian-contracts.md` record additions for the checkpoint/signature shape; and its own ADR covering the watchdog's authority and the StrongBox key's lifecycle, per ADR-032's precedent. Design is written generically against standard Android StrongBox Keystore APIs (not device-specific) and will be hardware-validated on the Pixel 7a per ADR-003; a newer target device mentioned during brainstorming is not yet the subject of its own support gate. Phases 2-5 of the roadmap (sensor/intervention expansion, remediation completion, behavioural detection engine, AI broker) remain separate, not-yet-started cycles, named in the design document for continuity only. | Revisit if Phase 0's resolved-fragmentation finding no longer holds by the time implementation actually begins, or if a Pixel 10a (or other newer device) support gate is opened under ADR-003 before this phase's Pixel 7a hardware validation completes. |
| ADR-058 | 2026-08-27 | Land `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-27-duress-real-user-quiescing-and-return.md` in full: replace the broken "freeze the real user via stopUserWithDelayedLocking" mechanism (found unworkable for user 0 during Task 7 of the original decoy-switch plan) with app-level quiescing (`RealUserQuiescer`: existing cached-app freezer + notification suppression, including a call/media-exemption fix found during this plan's own Task 3 review), and add the previously-unspecified return-to-real-user path (`ReturnToRealUserHelper`). | Accepted (qualified — two items left open, see Consequences) | `UserController.stopUsersLU` unconditionally refuses to stop `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM` (confirmed via real source, `services/core/java/com/android/server/am/UserController.java:1323-1324`) -- true for every AOSP device, not a project-specific gap, since the primary owner is always user 0 on a normal (non-headless-system-user) device including `lynx`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-duress-real-user-quiescing-and-return-design.md` (owner-approved). 8 commits landed on `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch` in order (two tasks needed a real follow-up fix, landed as separate commits rather than amends): `6ef616c714af` (`RealUserQuiescer`, Task 1), `35dee8ebff74` (`ActivityManagerInternal`/`ActivityManagerService` quiescing methods, Task 2), `15162045ce05` (notification suppression, Task 3), `834412c4b589` (generic unquiesce-on-become-current hook, Task 4), `1f4a79aabfa4` (`DecoyProfileHelper` sequencing fix, Task 5), `f15c3d86962e`+`079d5588f483` (`verifyPrimaryCredentialNoRouting`, Task 6 -- the second commit zeroizes the synthetic password, a real gap the implementer found in this plan's own sample code), `62b84f2aa8d1`+`c1c367950ab6` (`ReturnToRealUserHelper` + wiring, Task 7 -- the second commit fixes a wrong file path the first commit landed under), `295f6f9cf610` (call/media notification exemption fix, Task 8, found during Task 3's own review -- `isCallNotification`/`isMediaNotification` previously let a real call for the quiesced user bypass suppression entirely, independently truth-table-verified by two different reviewers). Consolidated build confirmed all of `0001`-`0015` compile together: `ninja: 1h3m33.34s Build Succeeded: 2444 steps`, `#### build completed successfully (01:03:38) ####`. Emulator validation (Task 10 of this plan, real HEAD `295f6f9cf610`) confirmed with direct evidence: decoy match (real switch, verified via `mCurrentUserId` and absence of failure logging); frozen-process evidence (32/36 user-0 processes `isFrozen=true`, remaining 4 plausibly excluded by pre-existing AOSP freezer non-cached rules, not a `RealUserQuiescer` defect); notification-suppression evidence (`dumpsys notification` usage-stats proof with a differential control case -- quiesced user's notification blocked, active user's own notification posts normally); return-path result (real match against the real owner's credential, real switch back to user 0, unquiescing proven via a user-0 notification posting normally again afterward); wrong-PIN and wipe-PIN both confirmed correct and not confused with decoy routing (real `DuressWipe`/storage-key-deletion/device-poweroff evidence for the wipe path). Two items are explicitly left open, not swept under the rug: the call/media-exemption fix (Task 8) has strong static confirmation (live source re-read matches its own published boolean-logic proof) but no successful dynamic trigger of a real CallStyle/session-token MediaStyle notification against the specific backgrounded-user-0 scenario in the available emulator tooling (a real GSM call routes to the current/decoy user rather than the SIM-owning user 0 in this multi-user configuration -- a newly-observed platform behavior, not a bug); and a post-wipe emulator reboot characterization was time-boxed at ~71 minutes across two relaunches and left genuinely inconclusive (QEMU process and console stayed healthy throughout, the guest never became adb-responsive, no crash-loop found or ruled out) -- assessed as very likely an environment artifact (a resource-shared server's slow first-boot-after-wipe), not a regression, since none of Tasks 1-8 touch early-boot code and the wipe mechanism itself independently fired correctly before this open item began. A real, unrelated infrastructure defect was also found and fixed during validation: the emulator's initial cold boot hung 1h46m because it loaded a stale quickboot snapshot from an earlier build against this session's freshly built system image; fixed by adding `-no-snapshot-load` to the documented manual launch sequence. | Exported as `aegisos-platform/credential/duress-unlock-decoy/patches/frameworks-base/0008`-`0015`. The decoy mechanism is functionally complete and validated end to end on the emulator: entry, freeze, notification suppression, and return all work with real evidence. Task 7 of the original decoy-switch plan is superseded by this plan's own Task 10; Task 8 of that plan (physical hardware) gained a new battery-measurement requirement and remains not started. Two residual gaps remain open for future work: a dynamic call/media-notification test against the backgrounded user (needs either a self-managed VoIP-style test call or physical-hardware testing where a real GSM call can be more directly controlled), and confirming whether the post-wipe reboot slowness is real or purely an artifact of this shared build server (worth re-checking on a less loaded host or on physical hardware). | Revisit if physical-hardware validation (still-pending Task 8 of the original plan) finds the freeze/unfreeze timing or battery numbers unacceptable, or if either of the two open items above resolves to a real defect rather than an environment/platform artifact -- this ADR only covers emulator-level functional correctness for the mechanism's own code paths, not real-hardware performance or the two explicitly-flagged open items. |
| ADR-059 | 2026-08-28 | Complete Tasks 8-9 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-21-duress-decoy-user-switch.md` — physical Pixel 7a hardware validation, real PIN-to-landing latency and battery measurement, and findings recording — closing out all 9 tasks of the original decoy-switch plan. | Accepted (qualified — two real open items, see Consequences) | Real hardware validation on `lynx` (build path: `frameworks/base` HEAD `295f6f9cf610` → instrumented `49e4d939dc61` → instrumentation removed `5f3a562a4eef`, each build signed with the alpha key over the full coherent 12-partition set per ADR-049's established method, 13/13 AVB-verified on two independent machines each time) confirmed with direct device evidence: real match, decoy match (via both `cmd lock_settings verify` and, incidentally, genuine physical keyguard PIN entry witnessed in logcat and confirmed by the owner as their own real interaction), real-user freeze (44/57 `u0a*` processes `isFrozen=true`), wrong-PIN handling (both real and decoy checks confirmed running via logcat), and the return-to-real-user path (`ReturnToRealUserHelper`, its first-ever physical-hardware exercise) all worked as designed. Real PIN-to-landing latency measured directly via temporary `IUserSwitchObserver` instrumentation (`UserSwitchObserver` subclass, verified against real AOSP source before use so it could not itself stall the real switch): three same-boot runs, 1549ms/1120ms/643ms, average ≈1.1s with a clear cold/warm split — no target threshold exists in the design to score against, but this does not read as instantaneous, especially cold. Real battery draw was measured via a detached on-device sampling script (necessary because the device's WiFi radio was found to fully disconnect during unplugged idle, and a first sampling attempt was discovered contaminated — still plugged in — and discarded before being used); the valid sample showed quiesced-state draw dropping roughly 5-25x below the non-quiesced baseline (-123,897 uA) once settled, directionally supporting the "really important" battery-savings goal from ADR-058's design but too small and irregular a sample (10 points, gaps up to 93s from the device's own deep-idle CPU suspend) to state as a precise ratio. Along the way, a process-wide gap recurred and was fixed for real: `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` was omitted from the first Task 8 build despite the ADR-047 lesson already being written into `AGENTS.md` — caught only because the owner asked before flash commands were sent, not by any check performed here; `AGENTS.md` and the corresponding memory record were both rewritten from a conditional judgment call into an unconditional, mechanical two-step requirement (export the flag on every real-device build; grep the fresh `installed-files.txt`/`installed-files-product.txt` for all four gated apps before any signing ceremony, every time). | Task 8's own two temporary-instrumentation patches (add, then remove) were fully reverted and are not exported to `aegisos-platform` — the real branch's `frameworks/base` tree is byte-identical to before Task 8 for the touched file, so nothing new ships. Two real, confirmed-but-unresolved findings are carried forward, not fixed here: (1) the device's own multi-user UI chrome shows a visible two-profile indicator (distinct colors), a real-hardware confirmation of the "post-switch hiding" gap this project's own `docs/research/duress-unlock-api-verification.md` already flagged as out of scope for this plan — now known to be externally observable, not theoretical; (2) the decoy user does not land on a normal home screen after switching, most likely because it has never completed Android's own first-use setup flow (unconfirmed hypothesis). The wipe-PIN path was not re-exercised on this physical device this session (owner declined a real, irreversible crypto-erase on an in-use test device) — its physical-hardware validation still rests solely on Stage 1 Task 3 Step 4 (2026-08-20). | Revisit before treating Duress Unlock Stage 2 as feature-complete: both open UI findings need their own follow-up investigation and fix, and the battery number needs a longer, uninterrupted remeasurement before it can be quoted as a specification-grade result. Also revisit if a future real-device build ever again omits `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` despite the now-unconditional `AGENTS.md` rule — that would mean the rule itself needs a stronger enforcement mechanism than documentation. |
| ADR-060 | 2026-08-29 | Approve `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-28-duress-decoy-session-indistinguishability-design.md` and land Tasks 1-9 of its implementation plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-28-duress-decoy-session-indistinguishability.md`) across four AOSP repositories under the established local-first patch-authoring discipline, executed via subagent-driven-development with an independent task review for every task. | Accepted (qualified — see ADR-061 for a real defect found in Component A during validation) | The design was approved after a source-verified audit inventoried every real way the decoy session fails the threat model's "a decoy unlock is, from every observable signal, a normal unlock" requirement (`docs/security/threat-model.md:54`): a P0 double-PIN-prompt bug (reproduced on physical hardware before this plan), P1 identity leaks (SystemUI's user switcher and power menu, Settings' Storage screen, the `com.android.multiuser` widget), a P2 setup-incomplete behavioral cascade (screenshot/clipboard overlays, full-screen intents, the alarm icon, Material You theming, the QS gear), and minor P3 inconsistencies. Task 1 (research) confirmed `STRONG_AUTH_REQUIRED_AFTER_USER_LOCKDOWN` is lockdown-only, not reachable by an ordinary wrong-PIN attempt (independently re-verified by a second subagent before being trusted), and found one new unscoped, decoy-reachable call site (`packages/SettingsLib/.../StorageMeasurement.java:148`) the original audit had missed. Tasks 2-8 landed one new shared API (`UserManager.getUsersForUiDisplay`, `frameworks/base` patch `0019`) and migrated every real leaking call site onto it (SystemUI `0020`, Settings `0001`+`0021`, the multiuser widget's own new `0001` patch package), added the decoy setup-complete write (`0022`, plus a same-session fix `0023` after the controller independently found and confirmed a missing `Binder.clearCallingIdentity()` around the new `Settings.Secure` write — the real invocation path lacks `WRITE_SECURE_SETTINGS`, a defect the task's own reviewer had missed), and Component A's own two patches (`0018` on `frameworks/base`, `0001` on a newly created `build/release` package) — later found broken, see ADR-061. Task 9's consolidated `m systemimage` build across all four repositories' final state passed clean: `ninja: 1h20m55.67s Build Succeeded: 4272 steps`, `#### build completed successfully (01:21:01) ####`. | All four repositories' patches exported to `aegisos-platform/credential/duress-unlock-decoy/patches/{frameworks-base,build-release,packages-apps-settings,packages-apps-multiuser}/`, `SOURCE.md` updated for each, including two new patch packages (`build-release`, `packages-apps-multiuser`) receiving their first-ever AegisOS patches. Components B, C, and D are functionally sound per this build and Task 10's subsequent emulator validation (ADR-061); Component A is not. | See ADR-061 for Component A's real, confirmed defect and its resolution, and ADR-062 for physical-hardware validation of the components that do work. |
| ADR-061 | 2026-08-29 | Revert Component A (the `android.multiuser.credential_capture` aconfig-gated keyguard-skip mechanism, ADR-060) to inert after real emulator validation found it does not achieve its stated goal and introduces a genuine, previously-masked device-wide keyguard-bypass regression for ordinary (non-decoy) user switches. Keep the two unrelated, genuine bug fixes discovered along the way (release-config directory placement, a `READ_ONLY`-permission build requirement). Defer a safe redesign of the underlying double-PIN-prompt fix to a future session. | Accepted (the regression is confirmed closed by direct re-test; the original P0 bug is intentionally left open) | Real, hands-on emulator validation (`uiautomator` UI-tree dumps, `logcat`, `dumpsys`, and real on-screen PIN taps — not exit codes alone) found Task 2's `credential_capture` flag override was placed under `build/release/aconfig/trunk_staging/android.multiuser/`, a directory `sdk_phone64_x86_64-cp2a-userdebug`'s real release config never reads (`aconfig/cp2a/Android.bp`'s `aconfig_value_set-platform_build_release-cp2a` only globs `aconfig/cp2a/*/Android.bp`; confirmed `lynx-cur` shares the identical `RELEASE_ACONFIG_VALUE_SETS` chain, so the same defect would have affected physical hardware too) — the flag had zero on-device storage slot (`aflags list`, 3119 flags, none named `credential_capture`) and the plan's own P0 bug was reproduced end-to-end via real UI interaction on the "fixed" build. Root-caused and fixed by the controller (moved the file to the already-existing `aconfig/cp2a/android.multiuser/` directory, patch `0002`), which surfaced a second real build-time defect (`permission: READ_WRITE` rejected outright because `cp2a` inherits `RELEASE_ACONFIG_REQUIRE_ALL_READ_ONLY=true`, fixed to `READ_ONLY`, patch `0003`). A full rebuild (`Build Succeeded: 7036 steps`, 87m) and fresh emulator boot confirmed the flag now genuinely has a storage slot and fires (`UserController: Skipping keyguard due to skipKeyguardWhenSwitchingToUnlockedUsers`, absent before this fix) — but re-testing then found two real problems, not one: (1) the original P0 bug was still not fixed — a different code path (`dismissKeyguardThenExecute`→`PrimaryBouncerInteractor`) re-shows a live bouncer, because the skip mechanism only suppresses a *new* forced keyguard show and does not dismiss one already showing before the switch, which is exactly the real coerced-owner scenario (the phone is already locked when the decoy PIN is typed); (2) far more seriously, with the mechanism genuinely active, switching to an ordinary PIN-protected secondary user (`am switch-user`) now reached a fully unlocked desktop with **zero credential check performed at all** — no `LockSettingsService: Verifying primary lockscreen credential` log line exists for that switch — a real, previously invisible, device-wide keyguard bypass that the inert flag had been masking ever since Task 2 first landed. The owner was presented with this finding directly and explicitly chose to revert rather than attempt a same-session redesign of a live security-critical keyguard mechanism. The revert (patch `0004` on `build/release`, `state: ENABLED`→`DISABLED`, keeping the correct `cp2a` placement and `READ_ONLY` permission from `0002`/`0003`) was landed, rebuilt (`Build Succeeded: 7224 steps`, 1h23m), and the regression's closure was independently re-confirmed via a fresh emulator boot and the exact same non-decoy switch test: `isKeyguardShowing=true` again, and the `LockSettingsService` credential-verification log line fires again. | `build/release` patches `0002`-`0004` exported to `patches/build-release/`. `frameworks/base`'s Task 2 resource flip (`0018`, `config_multiuserSkipKeyguardWhenSwitchingToUnlockedUsers=true`) is left in place, confirmed harmless on its own (`UserController.java:542`'s `Flags.credentialCapture()` guard means the resource default is never consulted while the aconfig flag stays disabled). Component A does not exist as working functionality in this plan's outcome: the original double-PIN annoyance that motivated it remains unfixed, as an explicit, accepted tradeoff, not an oversight. No physical device was ever touched by the mechanism's briefly-active, broken state — the entire find/fix/regress/revert cycle happened on the build server and a disposable emulator only. | A safe fix for the double-PIN bug needs a genuine redesign — most plausibly, scoping the keyguard skip to a signal that specifically means "this switch was just decoy-routed," rather than AOSP's generic (and, per this finding, too permissive) `isCeStorageUnlocked && isTrustAllowedForUser` conditions — as its own future brainstorming/spec/plan cycle, not a reactive patch. Do not re-enable `credential_capture` for this product without that redesign and a real re-test of the exact non-decoy regression case this entry found. |
| ADR-062 | 2026-08-30 | Build and sign a physical `lynx` hardware-validation candidate for this plan, rescoped per ADR-061's revert to validate only Components B, C, and D — Component A is intentionally not re-tested on physical hardware since it is reverted to inert. Flashing is handed to the owner to execute personally, per this project's standing rule that an agent never runs `fastboot flash`. | Accepted, partial — build and signing complete and independently verified; flashing, on-device validation, and closing findings (Task 11 Steps 2-4) not yet done as of this entry | Built from all four repositories' current real state (`frameworks/base` `3941aa4131fe`, `build/release` `10209de65d2accda1d7cdb8e178f41f9a20f166f` with Component A's flag `DISABLED`, `packages/apps/Settings` `229434697ac`, `packages/apps/Multiuser` `0ab4e93`) via `lunch lynx-cur-userdebug`, `export OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` in the same command block as `OUT_DIR`/`envsetup.sh` — an incremental build against the warm `out-lynx` tree, chosen deliberately over a fresh `OUT_DIR` given only 49GiB free on `/srv` against this project's own ~92GB cold-build-cost precedent; disk stayed at 49-50GiB free throughout, confirming the choice. `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true`'s effect was independently confirmed by the controller directly on the build server, not just accepted from the implementer's report: `Updater`/`Auditor`/`Seedvault`/`PdfViewerGOS` all present in the fresh `installed-files*.txt`, with a build-matching mtime — grepping the build log for the literal string is not a meaningful test, since `OFFICIAL_BUILD` is a Makefile conditional never echoed anywhere. The signing key was determined from real evidence rather than assumed: the device's actual current AVB trust root was confirmed, via `avbtool info_image` against the most recent real signing evidence anywhere on the server (`duress-task8-sign-workdir/vbmeta.img`), to be the original alpha key (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`) — not the signing host's vault key that ADR-049/050 had moved the separate OTA-publish path to — resolving an apparent contradiction in ADR-059's own prose in the implementer's favor (it was read literally rather than assumed to be loose terminology, and turned out correct). All 12 partition images plus a freshly signed `vbmeta.img` (rollback index `1783209600`, unchanged) were independently verified 13/13 on two separate machines (the build host's AOSP-tree `avbtool.py` and this workstation's distinct `/usr/bin/avbtool` install); the controller independently re-ran this verification a third time, directly, against the locally staged files, before handing anything to the owner. | The full `fastboot flash` command sequence (14 commands, fastbootd for the dynamic partitions, vbmeta last, mirroring ADR-049's proven-correct ordering) was handed to the owner to run themselves; not executed by any agent. The verified image set's only durable copy is on the build host (`<build-root>/evidence/task11-phaseA-sign-workdir/`) — the locally staged copy used for flashing lives in this session's own ephemeral tmpfs scratchpad and will not survive a workstation reboot. | Once flashed, Task 11 Steps 2-4 remain to be done: repeat Task 10's Component B/C/D checks with real evidence on physical hardware (explicitly skipping Component A's now-known-broken/reverted mechanism), decide whether the double-PIN latency remeasurement (the plan's original Step 3) is even meaningful now that Component A is inert, and write the closing findings record this plan's own eventual completion ADR will need. This workstation's `/home` being at 100% (1.5GiB free) was flagged as a real, current friction point for staging future signed images — worth addressing before the next hardware-validation cycle. |
| ADR-063 | 2026-08-30 | Close `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-28-duress-decoy-session-indistinguishability.md`: the owner flashed ADR-062's candidate to the physical `lynx` device, and Task 11 Steps 2-4 (rescoped Component B/C/D on-device validation, the latency-remeasurement scope decision, and this closing record) are complete. All 11 tasks of the plan are now done. | Accepted (qualified — several surfaces remain honestly unchecked, and Component A's original P0 bug remains open by design per ADR-061) | The controller independently verified the flash before trusting the owner's report: `ro.build.version.incremental=20260829213857` matches the signed candidate exactly, `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange`/`vbmeta.device_state=unlocked` unchanged as expected, and a full `dumpsys dropbox` scan for `crash\|anr\|tombstone\|fatal` returned zero matches both immediately post-flash and again at the end of the whole test session. Real, direct evidence on physical hardware: Component B's QS user switcher and Settings ▸ Storage both show only the decoy's own identity, no leak; Component C's `USER_SETUP_COMPLETE` registration-time write was proven with a genuine controlled before/after (the controller reset the flag to a known `0` baseline, the owner ran the real `set-decoy-profile` re-registration themselves, the flag flipped back to `1` as a direct, timed effect — not a stale-state coincidence), and its screenshot-overlay and QS-settings-gear consequences both fire correctly; the non-decoy regression check passed with stronger evidence than the emulator run (`am switch-user` to an ordinary locked user is refused outright by AOSP itself — `SWITCHABILITY_STATUS_SYSTEM_USER_LOCKED` — while the device sits locked, and a legitimate switch away from an unlocked non-owner session correctly re-shows keyguard for a different locked target, with no bypass in either case). A genuine device constraint was found along the way and worked around, not ignored: `org.aegisos.usertype.full.DECOY` enforces a real max-count-of-one, so Component C's registration-time-write proof had to reuse the device's single existing decoy user (reset-and-re-register) rather than spinning up a clean second one. The credential-handling hard rule was upheld throughout by construction: the controller never entered, requested, or extracted the owner's real PIN at any point — the one command that needed it (`set-decoy-profile --owner-credential`) was handed to the owner to run in their own terminal, exactly as this project's standing rule already requires for `fastboot flash`. A related, unplanned finding: simulated `adb shell input tap` sequences consistently failed to dismiss the decoy's own on-screen PIN bouncer even with verified-correct button coordinates and a controller-confirmed-correct PIN — consistent with GrapheneOS hardening against synthetic touch input on `FLAG_SECURE` keyguard surfaces, a real positive security property noted for the record but not investigated further as out of this plan's scope; the owner dismissed it via their own real physical touch, matching ADR-059's precedent. Step 3 (latency remeasurement) was explicitly skipped, not silently dropped: Component A never shipped (ADR-061), so there is no double-PIN removal on this candidate to measure against ADR-059's baseline — re-running the old instrumentation would only reproduce the same already-on-record figures. Several surfaces remain honestly UNCHECKED, consistent with Task 10's own reporting standard rather than forced into a pass: the lock-screen avatar switcher, the `com.android.multiuser` widget (impractical via `adb` alone), the clipboard overlay (impractical UI automation), the alarm icon (environment-wide limitation, not decoy-specific), and Material You theming (now confirmed non-emulator-specific — the same non-correlating data pattern appears on real hardware too). Component D's individual P3 surfaces were not independently re-tested; judged sufficiently corroborated by Component B's now-confirmed-working shared filter mechanism. | Full evidence: `.superpowers/sdd/task-11-phaseB-report.md`. Disposable test artifacts created during validation (`aegisos-hw-regression-check`, id 11) were removed; the pre-existing decoy user (id 10) and its now-correct `user_setup_complete=1` state were left in place, matching the device's state as handed back by the owner. `screen_off_timeout` was changed to `600000`ms during testing and not reverted, since its original value was never recorded — flagged for the owner to reset via Settings if it mattered to them. | This closes the Stage 3 plan as a whole. | ADR-064 | 2026-08-30 | Task 12's closing whole-branch review of the entire third plan (dispatched to an independent reviewer, most capable model, after ADR-063 had already declared the plan complete) found a real, Critical gap every per-task review in this plan had missed: `DecoyProfileHelper.setDecoyProfile` never validated that the registered `decoyUserId` is an existing `USER_TYPE_FULL_AEGISOS_DECOY` user, only that it isn't the real user. The controller independently confirmed the gap against the real live branch (not the reviewer's own disposable-worktree copy, which the reviewer itself flagged as unverified given no SSH/shell tool in its environment) before fixing it. Three lower-severity findings were fixed in the same patch: `config_multiuserSkipKeyguardWhenSwitchingToUnlockedUsers` reverted to `false` at the resource layer too (belt-and-suspenders — the aconfig flag alone, `build/release` `0004`, is sufficient on this device's current release config, but a future rebase or different release config could otherwise silently reactivate ADR-061's regression); a third unscoped Wi-Fi call site (`WifiUtils.java:387`, `packages/apps/Settings` `0003`) the design doc named but Task 8 missed; and a needless `@UnsupportedAppUsage` removed from a brand-new method with no prior reflective callers by construction. One reviewer finding was investigated and rejected as a mischaracterization, not fixed: a claimed Settings ▸ Storage "System" bucket leak turned out, on reading the real `getSystemSize()` source, to be a fixed volume-vs-partition figure identical for every user on the device including the real owner — not scoped to the per-user loop at all, so there is no differential tell there; this predates the plan and is out of Component B's scope, not a regression. Both patches were rebuilt for physical `lynx` (`OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` reconfirmed via a fresh `installed-files*.txt` grep for all four gated apps across `/system` and `/product`; only `system.img` needed regenerating, the other 11 partition images were byte-identical to the prior candidate's own output since none of their inputs changed — one fully coherent 12-partition set signed together, not a splice of new and old partitions), signed with the same alpha key and rollback index, 13/13 independently re-verified on two machines, flashed by the owner, and independently re-confirmed clean (matching fingerprint, unchanged boot state, zero Dropbox crash/ANR/tombstone entries). The fix itself was then decisively confirmed on physical hardware, not just by inspection: a disposable ordinary user was created, the owner ran the real `set-decoy-profile` registration against it themselves (the controller never saw or handled the real credential, same handoff pattern as `fastboot flash`), and it failed with `java.lang.IllegalArgumentException: decoyUserId must be an existing org.aegisos.usertype.full.DECOY user` at exactly the new check's own line, full real call chain intact. The device's actual, correctly-typed decoy (id 10) was confirmed unaffected throughout. | Full evidence: `.superpowers/sdd/task-11-phaseB-report.md`'s successor investigation (this review-and-fix cycle was done directly by the controller, not a task-numbered SDD entry, given its cross-cutting nature); `aegisos-platform/credential/duress-unlock-decoy/` `SOURCE.md`/`README.md` updated with `frameworks-base` `0024` and `packages-apps-settings` `0003`, and corrected to name ADR-059 through this entry (README previously stopped citing at ADR-058 and still claimed physical hardware validation was "not started," both stale since ADR-059/062/063). | This is the actual close of the third plan's Task 11/12 — ADR-063's "fully closed" language should be read as superseded by this entry, not as an independent, still-valid milestone: a Critical gap existed in what ADR-063 called complete, and it is this entry, not ADR-063, that reflects the plan's real final state. Task 12's remaining step (`superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch`) proceeds from here; the owner has already indicated the long-lived `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch` branch should be kept as-is across stages, matching every prior stage's precedent, rather than merged or discarded. |

| ADR-066 | 2026-08-30 | Fix `UserManagerService#getUsersForUiDisplay` to key its decoy special-case off the device's actual current user (via the existing `getCurrentUserId()` helper) instead of `UserHandle.getCallingUserId()`, after that same UI verification sweep found a real, live, reproducible identity leak on physical `lynx` hardware. | Accepted, urgent (this corrects a Critical gap in what ADR-063 claimed was hardware-verified) | The QS footer's "Select user" dialog (reached by tapping the user icon in the notification shade, distinct from the lock-screen/keyguard switcher) showed the real Owner identity alongside the decoy's own while the decoy session (`aegisos-test-decoy`, id 10) was genuinely current and unlocked — directly reproduced and screenshotted, not inferred. Root-caused by tracing the real call chain (`UserSwitchDialogDelegate` → `UserDetailView.Adapter` → `BaseUserSwitcherAdapter` → `UserSwitcherController` → `UserSwitcherInteractor` → `UserRepositoryImpl` → `getUsersForUiDisplay`) and confirming via `dumpsys window` that `mCurrentFocus=Window{... u0 Select user}` while `mFocusedApp` correctly showed `u10 com.android.launcher3` — SystemUI's floating windows render under user 0's Binder/window context regardless of which user is actually foregrounded (independently confirmed the power menu's `GlobalActionsDialogLite` shares this same `u0`-owned pattern, though it carries no leak today only because `fw.power_user_switcher` is inactive on this device). `getUsersForUiDisplay`'s decoy check therefore never applied to any SystemUI-hosted floating surface, silently defeating the exact protection ADR-060 claimed to have added for "the QS user switcher." Accepted | Confirmed by reading the real source, not inference: `startWatchingNoted`/`startWatchingActive` (`vendor/aegisos/guardian`, `master` branch) are registered with no uid/user filter, and `onAccess(op, uid, packageName)` checked only whether `uid` was backgrounded, never whether it belonged to the calling process's own user. Cross-checked the actual data model this feeds (`aegisos-services`' `guardian-core`, present locally): `GuardianOverviewEntry`/`GuardianActiveProtectionV1` carry no `userId` field anywhere, so an ingested cross-user event has no downstream point where it could be filtered back out. Fixed by comparing `UserHandle.getUserHandleForUid(uid)` against `Process.myUserHandle()` before doing anything else with the event, discarding it on a mismatch; both are genuine public, non-`@hide` SDK APIs, and `Process.myUserHandle()` matches the pattern `GuardianComponents.java` already uses elsewhere in this same app. Authored under the established local-first worktree discipline (fetched file confirmed byte-identical via `sha256sum` before editing, landed via `git am` on the real `master` branch after confirming no build was in flight, patch regenerated fresh from real post-`git am` branch history) and build-verified (`m GuardianApp`: `Build Succeeded: 21 steps`, no diagnostics on the edited file). | Since `vendor/aegisos/guardian`'s `app/` is first-party AegisOS source, not a patch against upstream, this fix has no separate patch-package entry — it lives directly in `aegisos-platform/guardian/app/src/`, which was synced byte-for-byte to the real post-`git am` file and committed alongside `SOURCE.md`/`README.md` updates recording this fix under its own "Fixes since initial integration" section (distinct from the Duress Unlock package, since this is a different feature and branch, `master` not `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch`). Build-verified only; runtime confirmation would need two real Android users, one triggering a genuine background-location access, confirming it does not surface in the other's Guardian Overview — not yet done. | Ten of the 21 Duress Unlock audit items are now fixed. Signed with the alpha key (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`), matching ADR-062/064's exact parameters (`SHA256_RSA4096`, rollback index `1783209600`, `--padding_size 65536`), 13/13 independently verified on two machines (the build host and the controller's own workstation, two distinct `avbtool` 1.4.0 installs). **On the owner's explicit choice, this candidate is AVB-only signed, not whole-package release-signed** — its `otacerts.zip` carries the AOSP test cert, matching ADR-062/064's own precedent for physical duress-unlock validation candidates (which are hand-flashed via `fastboot`, not delivered via OTA), not ADR-049/050's fixed state; the owner was shown this explicitly before choosing, given the alternative (redoing whole-package release-signing) is a materially bigger, riskier step that has bitten this project before (`docs/devices/lynx-ota-server-flash-rollback-corruption-incident.md`). The owner flashed the candidate personally (never by an agent, per this project's standing rule) and dynamic validation followed via `adb`: the QS footer's "Select user" dialog — the exact ADR-066 leak — and Settings ▸ Users both directly confirmed fixed on physical hardware, screenshot and full UI-text-dump evidence, zero trace of "Owner" anywhere. The factory-reset "erase all data" screen appears unreachable for the decoy at all (only "Reset app preferences" is offered), consistent with, not contradicting, item 16's admin-gate finding. | Six items (Wi-Fi shared badge/dialog, uninstall dialogs, the status-bar icon, the Multiuser widget, Guardian's cross-user AppOps check, and the new `0007` fix) remain honestly **UNCHECKED** — no valid trigger fixture existed for any of them (no shared Wi-Fi network, no multi-install app, no DO/PO app) and none were forced into a pass; all six share the same `getUsersForUiDisplay` primitive already proven correct at the two most severity-critical sites. | Revisit the six UNCHECKED items only if a real fixture (a shared Wi-Fi network, a genuinely multi-install app, a real device/profile-owner app) becomes available during some other, unrelated task — do not construct one solely to close this list. |
| ADR-073 | 2026-09-03 | Find, root-cause, fix, and hardware-verify a real crash-to-full-device-reboot bug in the Duress Unlock decoy-switch mechanism, discovered while attempting ADR-072's own dynamic validation. | Accepted | While validating ADR-072's candidate, entering the decoy PIN on a device that had already fully booted and settled — but where the real owner's own credential had never been entered this boot — caused `system_server` to crash fatally and the whole device to reboot, dropping the just-established decoy session and landing back on user 0's lockscreen. This is arguably the single most realistic real-world coercion scenario Duress Unlock exists for (a freshly-booted, never-yet-unlocked phone), and it broke. Root-caused via the device's own event log (`uc_finish_user_unlocking`/`ssm_user_unlocking` firing for user 10, the decoy, never 0, immediately before the crash — ruling out `DecoyProfileHelper`/`RealUserQuiescer`, neither of which calls anything backup- or unlock-lifecycle-related) down to one exact line: `frameworks/base/services/backup/java/com/android/server/backup/UserBackupManagerService.java:622` passed `mContext` (the un-scoped, implicitly-user-0 construction-time context) instead of `userContext` (created six lines above it, for exactly this purpose, with the comment "We need a context for the user in question") to `UserBackupPreferences`'s constructor, so its `getSharedPreferences()`/`credentialProtectedStorageCheck()` call always checked user 0's CE-storage-unlocked state regardless of which user's backup service was actually being started. Confirmed via `git blame` as genuine six-year-old upstream AOSP code (commit `9e111f19df8e5`, 2019-11-15) that no AegisOS patch has ever touched (`git log --grep=^aegisos -- services/backup/` returns nothing) — harmless on every ordinary Android flow, since user 0 is always already unlocked by the time any other user's backup service starts on a normal multi-user switch (you must unlock the primary user to even reach the switcher UI); Duress Unlock's decoy-routing mechanism is the first and only path in AOSP's own history, as far as this investigation found, that starts and unlocks a secondary user's session while user 0 has never unlocked at all. Fixed with a one-line substitution (`mContext` → `userContext`) plus an explanatory comment. Authored under the established local-first worktree discipline (fetched file confirmed byte-identical via `sha256sum` before editing, landed via `git am` on the real branch after confirming no build was in flight) and rebuilt (`m -j8`, `lynx-cur-userdebug`, `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` reconfirmed, 224 steps, 3m34s — fast, since only this one file changed). Independent verification found `system_ext.img` had genuinely changed between the two build runs despite the patch never touching anything in that partition, consistent with this project's own documented build non-determinism history (ADR-021 through 029) — traced to a stale locally-cached comparison file, not a build defect, and resolved by re-downloading the fresh partition before re-verifying; the signed candidate itself (all 12 partitions plus `vbmeta.img`, same alpha key/rollback index/padding as ADR-072) is 13/13 independently verified on two machines. The owner flashed it personally and re-ran the *exact* original trigger scenario for real: a fresh boot, decoy PIN entered directly with user 0 never credential-unlocked. The device's own event log shows the full unlock sequence completing cleanly through `ssm_user_unlocked: 10` with no crash and no reboot; `dumpsys dropbox` shows no new crash entry, only the prior incident's own record. | Patch exported to `aegisos-platform/credential/duress-unlock-decoy/patches/frameworks-base/0030`, `SOURCE.md` updated; incident detail recorded in `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-backup-context-crash-incident.md`. This is now hardware-confirmed fixed for the exact reproduced scenario — not merely compile-verified, unlike most of this audit's other fixes. | If a future AOSP/GrapheneOS merge or upstream sync touches `UserBackupManagerService.java`, re-check this exact line survived the merge; a naive upstream rebase could silently reintroduce the wrong-context bug if the surrounding code is refactored. Separately, the owner has raised a materially larger, distinct open question after this session: hiding other users' identity from the decoy session is not the same as making the decoy session *look like* the device's one true primary profile (e.g. `UserInfo.isMain()`/admin status, setup-wizard-completion semantics, "End session" vs. primary-user UI affordances all currently still mark the decoy as an ordinary secondary profile even where its content is correctly filtered) — flagged as a real, separate design question for a future dedicated brainstorming/spec/plan cycle, not something resolved by this entry or the audit that preceded it. ADR-065's Wallet-tile "treat decoy as main-user-equivalent" exemption is the one existing precedent to build from. |
| ADR-074 | 2026-09-03 | Add `UserManager.isMainForUiDisplay(int)` display-only primitive (`frameworks/base` patch `0031`). | Accepted | Generalizes the ADR-065 Wallet-tile precedent per the owner-approved 2026-09-03 design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-duress-decoy-primary-profile-visual-parity-design.md`): a single shared, current-user-keyed helper for display-only main/system-user spoofing, reused by Tasks 2-4 instead of duplicating detection logic per call site. | No real capability check changes; the method is additive and unused by any call site until Tasks 2-4 land. | A future call site needs admin-specific (not main/system-user-equivalent) display spoofing not covered by this primitive. |
| ADR-075 | 2026-09-03 | Suppress the QS footer's "End session" affordance for a decoy session by consuming `isMainForUiDisplay` in `UserManagerService.getUserLogoutability()` (`frameworks/base` patch `0032`). | Accepted | Task 2 of the same plan closes a genuine, 2026-09-03-audit-confirmed gap: `getUserLogoutability()` only ever exempted the literal `userId == UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM` from "End session," so a decoy user (always a non-zero ID) never tripped it and could see and use an affordance the real single-owner profile never sees.
