The plan's cited line number (3426) predated `0031` shifting the file and was re-derived live via `grep -n` rather than trusted, landing the real edit at line 3458. | `getUserSwitchability()`, a separate real capability check governing whether the decoy session can actually be switched away from, is untouched — only the "End session" UI affordance's visibility changes. | A future refactor of `getUserLogoutability()` should preserve the `isMainForUiDisplay` check's position between the `USER_SYSTEM` check and the `getUserSwitchability` check. |
| ADR-076 | 2026-09-03 | Label the decoy's own row as "Owner" in Settings ▸ Users by consuming `isMainForUiDisplay` in `UserSettings.java`'s per-row summary logic (`packages/apps/Settings` patch `0008`). | Accepted | Task 3 of the same plan (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-duress-decoy-primary-profile-visual-parity-design.md`) closes a real, audit-confirmed cosmetic gap: Settings ▸ Users already shows only the decoy's own row (via the already-landed `getUsersForUiDisplay` fix, `packages/apps/Settings` `0004`), but that row's summary was decided by `if (user.isMain()) "Owner" else if (user.isAdmin()) "Admin" else null` — since the decoy is neither main nor admin, its own (only visible) row showed no summary at all, unlike a genuine single-owner phone's row, which always reads "Owner". Fixed with a one-line condition change: `if (user.isMain() \|\| mUserManager.isMainForUiDisplay(user.id))`; the `isAdmin()` and final `null`-summary branches are untouched. Real current HEAD (`79f050ee30fde0a7f3d835b0cabcbf3dd1a2d4e7`) and the exact quoted line-1395 block were both re-confirmed against the live build-server source before editing, matching the brief exactly. Landed via `git am --3way` (clean, no conflicts) at real branch SHA `885b048dd3c80cefb4e47dde2332abb058edad47`. A fast `m Settings` build (`OUT_DIR=out-preflight`) confirmed `frameworks/base`'s `0031` (`isMainForUiDisplay`, ADR-074) is genuinely present and resolvable from this repository: `ninja: 6m53.25s Build Succeeded: 166 steps`, `#### build completed successfully (06:58) ####`, no unresolved-symbol failure. | No real capability check changes — this is a display-only label, using the same `isMainForUiDisplay` primitive already accepted in ADR-074/075. Not yet dynamically validated (compile-time only, matching this whole visual-parity plan's current stage); deferred to a future consolidated hardware-validation round alongside Tasks 1, 2, and 4. | A future refactor of `UserSettings.java`'s per-row summary logic should preserve the `isMainForUiDisplay` check as an `isMain()` alternative, not a replacement, and keep the `isAdmin()`/`null` branches' ordering unchanged. |
| ADR-077 | 2026-09-03 | Show the owner's device-admin policy description/label text to a decoy session by consuming `isMainForUiDisplay` in `DeviceAdminAdd.addDeviceAdminPolicies()` (`packages/apps/Settings` patch `0009`). | Accepted | Task 4 of the same plan (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-duress-decoy-primary-profile-visual-parity-design.md`) closes a second, distinct audit-confirmed cosmetic gap: `addDeviceAdminPolicies()` picks between two pre-existing string variants (`pi.description`/`pi.descriptionForSecondaryUsers`, `pi.label`/`pi.labelForSecondaryUsers`) based on `UserManager.get(this).isAdminUser()` — a decoy activating a device-admin app (e.g. a Find My Device-style app) would see the "secondary user" wording a real single-owner admin never would. Fixed with a one-line condition change at line 802: `boolean isAdminUser = UserManager.get(this).isAdminUser() \|\| UserManager.get(this).isMainForUiDisplay(UserHandle.myUserId());`; no other line in the method or file changes. `UserHandle` is the file's own pre-existing import (line 62) and `.myUserId()` is already used elsewhere in the same file (lines 433, 547, 728, 797) — no new import needed. Real current HEAD (`885b048dd3c80cefb4e47dde2332abb058edad47`) and the exact quoted line-802 content were both re-confirmed against the live build-server source before editing, matching the brief exactly. Landed via `git am --3way` (clean, no conflicts) at real branch SHA `d5e8d97332f098eeb852deb9c2ff7a17423fc402`. A fast `m Settings` build (`OUT_DIR=out-preflight`) confirmed `frameworks/base`'s `0031` (`isMainForUiDisplay`, ADR-074) is genuinely present and resolvable from this repository, matching Task 3's own confirmation: `ninja: 5m07.60s Build Succeeded: 20 steps`, `#### build completed successfully (05:13) ####`, no unresolved-symbol failure. | No real capability check changes — this is display-only string selection, using the same `isMainForUiDisplay` primitive already accepted in ADR-074/075/076. Not yet dynamically validated (compile-time only, matching this whole visual-parity plan's current stage); deferred to a future consolidated hardware-validation round alongside Tasks 1-3. | A future refactor of `DeviceAdminAdd.addDeviceAdminPolicies()` should preserve the `isMainForUiDisplay` check as an `isAdminUser()` alternative, not a replacement, and keep the per-policy `descriptionId`/`labelId` selection logic unchanged. |
| ADR-078 | 2026-09-03 | Close the decoy primary-profile visual parity plan (Tasks 5-7): consolidated `lynx` build, alpha/vault-key signing, and physical hardware validation of ADR-074 through ADR-077's four patches. | Accepted, qualified | Task 5's first consolidated `lynx` build silently omitted `packages/apps/Settings`'s changes (patches `0008`/`0009`) from the flashed candidate — `m -j6 systemimage` reported success but `system_ext.img` (carrying `Settings.apk` on this device) kept a stale pre-patch mtime; caught by pulling the installed `Settings.apk` and grepping its dex files for `isMainForUiDisplay`, not by trusting the build's own success report. Root-caused to a Ninja/Soong incremental-analysis staleness specific to the `out-lynx` output directory (not reproduced in `out-preflight`); worked around by force-touching the patched source files and invoking the `systemextimage` Ninja target directly after a plain `m systemimage` re-run reported "Build Succeeded: 0 steps" while the real target file was missing entirely — a second, distinct instance of a build claiming success while the artifact state disagreed. The corrected `system_ext.img` was re-signed with the signing host's vault key (`<vault>/keys/lynx/avb.pem` — the key that has actually signed this device's trusted `vbmeta` since ADR-046, corroborating that this is not the original ADR-029/030 alpha key, a naming distinction clarified during this closure), rollback index `1783209600` (confirmed via `avbtool info_image` against the pre-signing build output), 13/13 AVB checks independently verified on the signing host and the build host. Two real procedural incidents during signing were caught and corrected, not silently worked around: an initial staging location (`<home>/sign-work/`, 5GB) ran out of disk mid-copy and corrupted two partition images, contradicting this project's own already-written guidance to use `<build-root>/signed/.ad-hoc-work/<name>/` for exactly this; and a first `make_vbmeta_image` attempt produced a silent zero-byte output (exit 0) because the vault had not yet been opened, caught by verifying the real output file rather than the exit code. The owner flashed the corrected candidate via `fastboot flashall` (no `-w`, confirmed safe via the real `fastboot-info.txt`'s `if-wipe` conditional). Full record: `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-visual-parity-findings.md`. | Physical hardware confirms both patch sets present by direct extraction (not by build-number string, which proved to be a stale, harmless `out-lynx` cache artifact unrelated to code content) and zero crash/ANR/tombstone. The QS footer "End session" fix — the plan's single highest-severity item, left UNCHECKED by the emulator round because a synthetic tap never opened the dialog — is now positively confirmed working on real hardware (the same tap opened the real "Select user" dialog; the SIGN_OUT action is genuinely absent for the decoy, present as before for the owner). The Settings ▸ Users "Owner" label fix is confirmed with no false-positive leak onto the decoy's row when viewed by the owner. Two items remain honestly UNCHECKED, not forced to a pass: the device-admin policy text fix (patch `0009`) was confirmed present by dex string search but not dynamically exercised on hardware this round; the ordinary-secondary-user regression check relies on Task 6's emulator evidence (unchanged `frameworks/base` logic) rather than an independent re-run on this hardware. A real, unrelated environment quirk was noted but not investigated further: `/sdcard` is inaccessible via `adb shell` while a secondary/decoy user is current. | Any future session finding the compiled diff missing from a flashed `lynx` candidate despite a clean build log should check this entry's `out-lynx`/`system_extimage` finding before re-diagnosing from scratch. A future dedicated dynamic-validation pass should close the two remaining UNCHECKED items above before this feature's indistinguishability posture is described as fully hardware-verified end to end. |
| ADR-079 | 2026-09-04 | Correct ADR-078's signing-key attribution and re-flash the decoy primary-profile visual parity candidate with the real alpha key; independently confirm all four gated apps' real on-device presence. | Accepted, corrects ADR-078 | A closing whole-branch review (most capable model, dispatched after ADR-078 was already written) found ADR-078's own signing-key claim contradicted ADR-072 and ADR-073 — both one day earlier, on the same device, explicitly citing "the alpha key (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`)" — while ADR-078 claimed the signing host's vault key (`<vault>/keys/lynx/avb.pem`) was correct "since ADR-046." Investigated directly rather than guessed: `avbtool extract_public_key` against the real `alpha_key.pem` on the build server (`<build-root>/keys/alpha/alpha_key.pem`) confirmed its real fingerprint is exactly `265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`, matching ADR-072/073 precisely — the controller's own earlier openssl-based fingerprint attempt had used the wrong hashing method (raw DER SubjectPublicKeyInfo hash, not AVB's own public-key-blob encoding) and produced a spurious mismatch that was never resolved before ADR-078 was written. The router's own much-earlier note that "the signing host's ADR-041-generated vault key, not the alpha key, now signs this device's vbmeta" (from the OTA-signing-infrastructure era, ADR-046-049) was real but stale by the time of this candidate — the project's actual, current, repeated practice for physical duress-unlock hardware-validation candidates specifically (ADR-062, ADR-064, ADR-072, ADR-073, all citing the same fingerprint) had already reverted to the alpha key well before this session, and the controller cited the older, superseded router paragraph instead of checking the two most recent, directly-on-point ADRs first. Corrected: all 12 partition images (the same set ADR-078 already assembled, `system_ext.img` included) re-signed directly on the build server with `<build-root>/keys/alpha/alpha_key.pem`, matching ADR-072/073's exact parameters (`SHA256_RSA4096`, rollback index `1783209600`, `--padding_size 65536`), 13/13 independently verified on two machines (the build host's AOSP-tree `avbtool.py` and the controller's own workstation `/usr/bin/avbtool`, a genuinely separate install). The owner reflashed the corrected candidate (`fastboot flashall`, no `-w`, same as ADR-078). Post-flash, independently confirmed: `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange`, `ro.boot.vbmeta.device_state=unlocked`, zero crash/ANR/tombstone, both patch sets still present by direct dex/jar string extraction (unaffected by the re-sign, since only the vbmeta signature changed, not any partition's content) — and, closing the gap ADR-078 itself was already missing, all four gated apps' **real installed package names** confirmed present via `adb shell pm list packages` directly on the flashed device (`app.seamlessupdate.client`, `app.grapheneos.pdfviewer`, plus Seedvault and Auditor), not merely `installed-files.txt`'s build-artifact listing. | The device's actual, currently-trusted AVB root of trust for physical duress-unlock validation candidates is the alpha key (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`) — this is now internally consistent across ADR-062, ADR-064, ADR-072, ADR-073, and this entry; ADR-078's own text is left unedited (this project's decision-log discipline is append-only correction, not silent rewrite) but should be read as superseded on this one point. Nothing else in ADR-078 (the build-defect root cause, the two signing-ceremony procedural incidents, the disk-staging-location lesson, the physical-hardware dynamic-validation results for the "End session" and "Owner" label fixes) is affected — those findings are independent of which key signed the candidate and stand as recorded. | Before citing "the vault key" or "the alpha key" for any future physical `lynx` candidate, check the fingerprint directly (`avbtool extract_public_key`) against both `<build-root>/keys/alpha/alpha_key.pem` and `<vault>/keys/lynx/avb.pem` on the signing host, rather than reasoning from a router paragraph's prose alone — the router narrates history in the order it happened and is not guaranteed to reflect which key is current practice at any later point without checking the most recent same-class ADRs first. This project's own decision-log format-compliance script or future review should flag a same-day/next-day contradiction between two entries' factual claims automatically if one becomes available. |
| ADR-080 | 2026-09-04 | Add a method-local check to `UserManagerService.isUserSwitcherEnabled(@UserIdInt int userId)` so the decoy's own current-user view reports the device-wide user-switcher-enabled flag as disabled (`frameworks/base` patch `0033`), closing Task 1 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-duress-decoy-user-switcher-visibility.md`. | Accepted, compile-verified only | `Settings.Global.USER_SWITCHER_ENABLED` is a device-wide, non-per-user flag AOSP's `UserManagerService.dispatchUserAdded()` permanently sets to `1` the first time any second user (including a decoy) is ever created, leaking that a decoy exists to at least six real UI surfaces that all trace back to this one method. Fixed with a nine-line addition (re-verified byte-for-byte against the live build-server source at lines 3443-3457 before editing, matching the plan's quoted block exactly): reads the device's actual current user's `UserInfo` under `mUsersLock` via the existing `getUserInfoLU`/`getCurrentUserId()`, and returns `false` immediately if its `userType` is `UserManager.USER_TYPE_FULL_AEGISOS_DECOY`, before the existing `multiUserSettingOn` computation runs. Deliberately keyed off the actual current user rather than the `userId` argument passed in, since several real callers (the QS footer icon, the lockscreen avatar) run under SystemUI's cross-process rendering context and do not necessarily pass the current user's own ID — the same ADR-066 failure mode this project has already hit once. Authored under `AGENTS.md`'s local-first discipline in a disposable worktree (`aegisos-platform/.worktrees/decoy-switcher-visibility-task1/`, seeded read-only from the real branch HEAD `ccc6c058a6f0761b04027c055211bfd68c62e9f1`) and landed on the real `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch` branch with `git am --3way` (applied cleanly, no conflicts, confirmed no build in flight beforehand via `pgrep -fa 'ninja\|soong_ui'`). Real post-`git am` SHA: `544d6e6d4567966cf0d8308e110da4a130b7df6e` (differs from the local worktree's own commit SHA `5837f34625f9a833b993e8e7c824f8d31924f841`, confirmed via `git log` on the branch rather than assumed). A fast `m services` build (`OUT_DIR=out-preflight`, `sdk_phone64_x86_64-cp2a-userdebug`) confirmed clean compilation: `ninja: 2m25.96s Build Succeeded: 18 steps`, `#### build completed successfully (02:33 (mm:ss)) ####`, no diagnostics referencing `UserManagerService.java`, independently re-verified via a fresh SSH call rather than trusted from a background monitor alone. | This is compile-time verification only. No dynamic validation of any of the six identified UI surfaces (the QS footer icon, the lockscreen/keyguard status-bar avatar, both power-menu implementations, and three Settings "Allow multiple users" toggle widgets) or the owner-regression check has been performed yet — that is Task 2 (emulator) and Task 3 (physical `lynx`), neither started as of this entry. No other method in this file changed; `getUserLogoutability()`'s own `isMainForUiDisplay` check (`0032`, ADR-075) is untouched and independent of this fix. | Task 2 (emulator dynamic validation across all six surfaces plus the owner regression check) and Task 3 (physical `lynx` hardware validation, signed with the alpha key per this plan's own Global Constraints) remain to close this plan; do not describe the decoy's user-switcher visibility as fixed at runtime until at least one of those tasks produces real dynamic evidence. |
| ADR-081 | 2026-09-06 | Fix two real regressions patch `0033` did not actually cover on physical hardware, plus a third, independently-discovered avatar-color identity leak, and re-validate on a fresh emulator boot: `frameworks/base` patches `0034`, `0035`, `0036`; `packages/apps/Settings` patch `0010`. | Accepted, emulator-validated; physical `lynx` re-validation still open | Task 3 Phase B's real-hardware round (started under ADR-080) found `0033` did not work as claimed: 3 of 6 surfaces (QS footer icon, lockscreen avatar, Settings toggle) still leaked despite `0033`'s bytecode being `dexdump`-confirmed present and correct in the flashed `services.jar`. Per the owner's explicit direction ("find out why it doesn't work" — not "patch around it"), a `superpowers:systematic-debugging` root-cause pass, not a guessed re-fix, traced each surface's real source. Two resolved to genuine, independent code defects, neither a flaw in `0033` itself: (1) `UserRepositoryImpl.kt`'s `getSettings()` read `Settings.Global.USER_SWITCHER_ENABLED` directly via `globalSettings.getInt(...)`, never calling the patched `UserManager.isUserSwitcherEnabled()` at all — a structural bypass, confirmed live on the physical device (`am get-current-user`=`10` captured in the same command block as a `uiautomator dump` still showing `multi_user_switch`). Fixed (`0034`) by deleting the duplicate computation and delegating to `manager.isUserSwitcherEnabled()`, the same API `0033` already patches, so there is one source of truth instead of two silently diverging ones. (2) `KeyguardStatusBarView.setUserSwitcherEnabled(boolean)` stored the new value into `mIsUserSwitcherEnabled` but never called `updateVisibilities()` — the only method that actually applies that field to the real view — so a value change delivered by the controller's async `UserManager` Binder call (dispatched via `mBackgroundExecutor`, posted back via `mMainExecutor`) could be overtaken by an unrelated visibility-triggered `updateVisibilities()` call still using the stale prior value, with nothing re-invoking it once the real value landed. Confirmed genuine, pre-existing upstream AOSP code (untouched by any prior AegisOS patch), dormant only because no caller before Duress Unlock ever needed `isUserSwitcherEnabled()`'s answer to change mid-session. Fixed (`0035`) by calling `updateVisibilities()` directly from the setter; its one real call site already dispatches on `mMainExecutor`, so no thread-safety change was needed. The Settings toggle was investigated and deliberately **not** re-fixed: its real code path (`UserSettings.java`'s `onResume()` → `mMainSwitchController.updateState()` → `UserCapabilities.updateAddUserCapabilities()`) already recomputes fresh via the same correct, patched API on every resume, and had already passed with this exact code on the Task 2 emulator round. A forensic recheck dispatched specifically to explain the physical-hardware discrepancy found the check reused an already-running `com.android.settings` process for user 10 whose cold-vs-resumed task state could not be confirmed either way, and could not go further because the physical device disconnected from USB mid-session before a decisive `dumpsys user`/service probe could run — a raw, unverified Binder transaction-code probe was correctly withheld by the dispatched subagent rather than risked against the one live device holding the project's only registered decoy user. This surface is left open, not forced into a third patch on unconfirmed evidence. Separately, per the owner's explicit standard that the decoy session must present as the device's *only* user, not merely pass a fixed per-surface checklist, a fresh-emulator screenshot of Settings ▸ Users surfaced a third, previously-undocumented leak: `UserIcons.getDefaultUserIcon()` picks a default (no custom photo) avatar color from a fixed 8-entry palette keyed by `userId % 8` — the owner (userId `0`) got `user_icon_1` (red), a test decoy (userId `10`) got `user_icon_3` (blue), a real, standalone identity tell independent of every list-membership/name leak the `getUsersForUiDisplay`/`isMainForUiDisplay` audits already cover, since it fires even when only the decoy's own single avatar is ever shown. Two independent call sites needed the identical fix: `UserSettings.java`'s `assignDefaultPhoto()` (persists the color via `UserManager#setUserIcon`) and SystemUI's `UserIconProvider.kt`'s `getUserImage()` (computes it in-memory, not persisted). Both fixed (`packages/apps/Settings` `0010`, `frameworks/base` `0036`) by routing the color-lookup `userId` — never the icon's real target `userId`, kept correct for `setUserIcon`/cache-key purposes — through the existing `isMainForUiDisplay()` primitive (`0031`, ADR-074): substitute `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM` when the current user displays as main, leaving any ordinary second real user's own distinguishing color untouched. All four patches were authored under `AGENTS.md`'s local-first discipline in disposable worktrees, landed on the real `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch` branches with `git am` (clean, no conflicts, confirmed no build in flight beforehand each time), regenerated fresh via `git format-patch` from the real post-`git am` branch history rather than exported from the worktrees' own commits, and independently verified byte-for-byte reproducible (apply-and-diff against the real branch tip, empty diff in every case). Build verification: `m -j6 SystemUI` clean for `0034`/`0035` (`21 steps`), `m -j6 SystemUI Settings` clean for `0036`/`0010` (`38 steps`), no error or warning referencing any changed file in either run. Dynamic re-validation ran on a genuinely fresh, `-wipe-data` emulator boot with all five patches (`0033`-`0036` plus Settings `0010`) present, using a newly created, cleanly-named decoy ("`Family`", not a test-fixture name that leaks its own purpose) registered through the real `set-decoy-profile` command: QS icon PASS (absent, matching the pristine single-user baseline captured on the same fresh boot before any decoy existed), lockscreen avatar PASS (absent, matching), Settings ▸ Users PASS (single row, "Owner" label, avatar color byte-for-byte matching the pristine owner's own default avatar in a direct screenshot comparison — not inferred from logs). Owner-regression PASS: switching back to Owner shows both real users (expected and correct — the owner's own management view was never meant to hide the decoy from the owner), the switcher toggle is re-enabled and interactive, and the QS icon correctly reappears; no regression from any of the four new patches. The full screenshot set (pristine baseline, decoy session, owner regression) was sent to the owner directly as evidence, not just described. | This closes the Settings-toggle question as *investigated and left open*, not resolved — do not describe it as fixed or as a confirmed non-issue until a repeat check with a forced-fresh Settings process (or physical hardware, where interactive taps have already proven more reliable than this project's headless-emulator tooling) settles it either way. Physical `lynx` hardware re-validation of this whole four-patch batch has not happened: the device disconnected from USB mid-Phase-B and, as of this entry, has not been reconnected — nothing above should be read as hardware-confirmed, only emulator-confirmed. A secondary, deliberately out-of-scope observation from the owner-regression screenshots: once a second real user exists, the owner's own Settings ▸ Users view now shows both rows in the *same* default color (since the "Family" account was itself decoy-registered and so also received the main-user color via this same fix) — this does not weaken the threat model (the owner's own session was never meant to hide the decoy from the owner) but is noted here in case a future session finds it worth a product discussion about whether a genuinely-shared, non-decoy family device should look different from this. | Before the next physical `lynx` build for this feature, reconnect and verify the Pixel 7a's USB state first — do not assume it is still reachable from a prior session. Any future work on `UserRepositoryImpl.kt`, `KeyguardStatusBarView.java`, `UserSettings.java`'s photo-assignment path, or `UserIconProvider.kt` should preserve the `isMainForUiDisplay`-substitution pattern this entry establishes rather than reintroducing a second, divergent computation of switcher-enabled state or default avatar color. |
| ADR-082 | 2026-09-06 | Exhaustively re-check every remaining avatar-color call site the owner's "100% certainty" demand required, finding and fixing two more instances of ADR-081's bug: `frameworks/base` patch `0037`, `packages/apps/Multiuser` patch `0003`. | Accepted, emulator-validated; physical `lynx` re-validation still open | Rather than re-asserting confidence in ADR-081's fix, every real `UserIcons.getDefaultUserIcon`/`getUserIconColors` call site across `frameworks/base`, `packages/apps/Settings`, and `packages/apps/Multiuser` (~15 total) was individually read. Two more genuine instances of the identical bug were found: `SettingsLib`'s shared `Utils#getUserIcon()` — used by several real Settings screens (Battery usage, Running services, Storage, About phone) independently of whether the decoy's own Settings ▸ Users row has ever assigned and persisted a default photo first — and the `Multiuser` home-screen widget's own `ImageRepository#getAvatar()`, a real, user-installable surface with the identical `userManager?.getUserIcon(userId) ?: UserIcons.getDefaultUserIcon(resources, userId, false)` fallback race. Both fixed identically to `0036`/Settings-`0010`: route the color-lookup `userId` through the existing `isMainForUiDisplay()` primitive, substituting `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM` when the current user displays as main; `MANAGE_USERS` confirmed already declared in the `Multiuser` app's own manifest before relying on it, not assumed by association with the sibling fixes' own confirmation for a different app. Every other remaining call site was read and confirmed **not** a bug, not merely skipped: `ApplicationPackageManager`/`CrossProfileApps` badge/cross-profile-item icons don't apply to a decoy session (no sub-profiles exist under one); `CrossProfileApps`'s own "personal profile" icon already hardcodes `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM`, unrelated to any specific real user; `UserSwitchingDialog` (`UserController.showUserSwitchDialog`) is structurally unreachable for a decoy switch — traced to source, not assumed from ADR-053's own label: `DecoyProfileHelper.freezeAndSwitchToDecoy` calls `ActivityManagerInternal#switchToUserSilently`, whose `UserController` implementation calls `sendStartUserSwitchFgMessage` directly and never checks `mUserSwitchUiEnabled` or calls `showUserSwitchDialog` at all; `EditUserPhotoController`'s call uses a user-chosen tint color, not a userId-derived one; `CreateUserDialogController` and `EditUserInfoController` both use `UserHandle.USER_NULL` (the neutral-gray placeholder case) for their dialog previews, never a real userId; `UserCreator.kt` colors a *newly created, ordinary* second user — correctly distinct from the owner, since this path only ever creates a plain `USER_TYPE_FULL_SECONDARY` user, never a decoy; `UserAdapter.java`'s (personal/work profile picker) tint is a fixed Material theme color, never userId-derived; and the bouncer's tablet-only user-switcher grid (`KeyguardSecurityContainer`) is confirmed structurally dormant on `lynx` via its real gating resource, `config_enableBouncerUserSwitcher` (`false` in the base `values/config.xml`; the only `true` override belongs to a `tangorpro` tablet vendor overlay, absent on this device) — verified directly, not inferred from the earlier form-factor comment this project had previously only assumed correct. A second, independent, **already-correct** parallel repository was also found and checked rather than assumed safe by name-similarity to its sibling: `UserSwitcherRepository.kt` (`UserSwitcherRepositoryImpl`), backing part of the QS footer's status model, already calls the same correct, patched `isUserSwitcherEnabled()` API and gates its avatar exposure behind that check before ever combining in a possibly-uncorrected bitmap — needed no fix. A real mistake was caught mid-execution, not silently avoided: `git am /tmp/0003-*.patch` on the build server expanded a stale glob matching three differently-dated `/tmp/0003-*.patch` leftovers from earlier sessions and applied the wrong one first (a `frameworks/base` patch, rejected outright by `packages/apps/Multiuser`'s own tree); caught immediately by the `git am` failure itself, `git am --abort`'d cleanly, and re-run against the exact intended filename rather than a glob. Both new patches were regenerated fresh via `git format-patch` from the real post-`git am` branch history and independently verified byte-for-byte reproducible. Build verification: `m -j6 SettingsLib Multiuser Settings SystemUI` clean (`63 steps`), no error or warning referencing either changed file. A second full `systemimage` build and a second fresh, `-wipe-data` emulator boot (a new decoy, "`Family`", created fresh rather than reusing the first round's) re-confirmed the primary, most-visible surfaces (Settings ▸ Users avatar color, QS icon, lockscreen avatar) still clean with the complete seven-patch set (`frameworks/base` `0033`-`0037`, `packages/apps/Settings` `0010`, `packages/apps/Multiuser` `0003`) — no regression from the two new patches. | The two newest fixes (`0037`, Multiuser `0003`) are source-verified, build-verified, and byte-for-byte reproducible, but **not** dynamically exercised end-to-end: a fresh emulator has no real battery-usage history to render, so `Utils#getUserIcon()`'s actual call site inside the Battery-usage app-list UI could not be triggered this round — an environment limitation, not a fix failure, but still a gap between "compiles and reads correctly" and "seen working." Physical `lynx` hardware re-validation of the complete seven-patch batch has not happened — the device disconnected from USB mid-Phase-B (ADR-081) and remains disconnected as of this entry. Nothing in this entry should be read as a claim that *every conceivable* identity-revealing surface on the device has been found — it closes the specific, exhaustively-re-checked class of bug ADR-081 first identified (userId-keyed default avatar color), not the general question of decoy indistinguishability, which this project's own long ADR history (ADR-060 through ADR-081) has already shown tends to surface new instances under renewed scrutiny. | Any future avatar-rendering code added to this codebase should call through `UserManager.isMainForUiDisplay()` before deriving a color from a raw `userId`, matching the pattern this entry and ADR-081 now establish in five call sites across three repositories. Before the next exhaustive-audit-style pass on this feature, consider whether a real battery-usage/app-usage history can be seeded on the emulator (or deferred to physical hardware, where real usage naturally accumulates) so this specific gap doesn't recur. |
| ADR-083 | 2026-09-07 | Root-cause and fix a real crash found during physical `lynx` re-validation of the seven-patch avatar-color/switcher batch: the decoy's own emulated storage volume was permanently unmountable whenever the owner's real credential had never been entered this boot. Three stacked patches: `frameworks/base` `0038`, `0039`, `0040`. | Accepted, build-verified across all three; hardware re-validation of the combined fix in progress, not yet confirmed | Entering the registered decoy's PIN directly on a freshly-booted device (owner's own credential never entered this boot) crashed Settings ▸ Storage with `IllegalArgumentException: Volume external_primary not found`, confirmed via `dumpsys dropbox`. `adb shell sm list-volumes` showed the decoy's own volume as permanently `unmountable` despite the decoy itself being `RUNNING_UNLOCKED` — not a display artifact, the actual underlying `VolumeInfo.state`. A control test (a freshly created, entirely ordinary `USER_TYPE_FULL_SECONDARY` user, no decoy registration) reproduced the identical state while the owner's session was locked and resolved the moment the owner unlocked for the first time — confirming this is not decoy-specific and not caused by any of `0033`-`0037`. Full record with the complete evidence trail: `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-storage-unmount-incident.md`. Root cause resolved to three stacked instances of the same underlying pattern already found once before in this project (ADR-073's `UserBackupManagerService` bug) — a system service silently assuming user 0 always unlocks before any other user's own lifecycle matters, true on stock Android (you must unlock the primary user to even reach the multi-user switcher) but false by design under Duress Unlock, whose entire point is that the real profile stays genuinely locked through a decoy switch. (1) `StorageManagerService.getVolumeList(int userId, ...)` hardcoded `isSystemUnlocked(UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM)` despite already having the correct target `userId` in scope, used a few lines later for the sibling check `isCeStorageUnlocked(userId)`; `git blame` confirmed the hardcoded line dates to an unrelated 2019 refactor (`157dd1dc40c5a`, "Move legacy obb data migration to a handler thread"), not a deliberate, still-valid decision. Fixed (`0038`, real branch SHA `94c6a5e888ed`) by checking `userId` instead — a real, standalone fix on its own merits, but re-testing after this alone still reproduced the identical crash, confirmed via a fresh `dumpsys dropbox` entry matching the original stack trace and an independent `services.jar` checksum match ruling out a stale-build repeat of ADR-078's own incident. (2) `adb logcat` during a fresh repro (not visible in `dumpsys dropbox` alone) showed the actual proximate cause: `StorageSessionController#onUnlockUser(int userId)` only called `initExternalStorageServiceComponent()` — a one-time, global initialization resolving MediaProvider's `ExternalStorageService` component, identical for every user — when `userId == 0`. If user 0 never unlocks, the component name stays permanently null and no user's storage session, including the decoy's own, can ever bind to it (`StorageUserConnection.connectIfNeeded()` throws "Not ready to bind"). Fixed (`0039`, real branch SHA `ec3b888a36a5`) by guarding on `mExternalStorageServiceComponent == null` instead of which user triggered the check — same one-time-init outcome for every existing Android flow, correct for the decoy-first case too; `mExternalStorageServiceComponent` is `volatile`, and a benign double-init race under concurrent unlocks is possible but harmless (same value resolved either way). This was a real improvement — `adb logcat` confirmed `onUnlockUser(10)` genuinely attempted initialization for the first time — but the attempt itself then failed one layer deeper: `initExternalStorageServiceComponent()` still hardcoded `resolveExternalStorageServiceAsUser(UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM)` internally, a third independent instance of the identical pattern. Confirmed directly on hardware: the identical lookup failed with "No valid ExternalStorageService component found" when resolved as user 0 (never unlocked) and succeeded when resolved as the already-unlocked decoy instead. Fixed (`0040`, real branch SHA `4067c29a100d`) by threading the actually-unlocking `userId` through `initExternalStorageServiceComponent(int userId)` (previously took no parameter) in place of the hardcoded constant; only one call site exists (`onUnlockUser`, already updated by `0039`), so this was a clean, mechanical signature change. All three authored under `AGENTS.md`'s local-first discipline in the same disposable worktree already used for `0034`-`0037`, landed on the real branch with `git am` (each applied cleanly, confirmed no build in flight beforehand each time), regenerated fresh via `git format-patch` as a combined 3-patch series from the real post-`git am` branch history, and independently verified byte-for-byte reproducible against both touched files in one combined apply-and-diff check. Each was individually built (`m -j6 services`, `OFFICIAL_BUILD=true` reconfirmed each time) and repackaged into a fresh `system.img` (`m -j6 systemimage`), with the resulting `system.img` timestamp checked against the freshly-built `services.jar` each round to rule out a repeat of ADR-078's `out-lynx` staleness incident; `system_ext.img` and the other 10 partitions were confirmed byte-identical to the `0037`-era candidate across all three rounds and reused as-is. Each candidate was signed with the alpha key (`SHA256_RSA4096`, rollback index `1783209600`, `--padding_size 65536`, matching every prior duress-unlock candidate), 13/13 independently verified on two machines each round, and flashed by the owner. | Two of the three fix attempts (`0038` alone, and `0038`+`0039` together) looked plausible and were built, signed, flashed, and re-tested in good faith as complete fixes, and both were disproven only by direct hardware re-testing against the exact original trigger, not by further static analysis. The combined `0038`+`0039`+`0040` candidate is the currently-flashed state as of this entry; hardware re-validation of the exact original trigger is in progress and genuinely not yet confirmed — this entry must not be read as closing the incident. Do not trust either symptom check alone when it is eventually re-tested: confirm via `sm list-volumes` showing `mounted`, a clean repeat of the Settings ▸ Storage screen with no crash, a clean `dumpsys dropbox` (no new entry), and no new `ExternalStorageServiceException` in logcat together, not visual "it didn't visibly crash" by itself — that check alone already disagreed with the device's own evidence once earlier in this same investigation. | If a future AOSP/GrapheneOS source sync or manifest bump touches `StorageManagerService.getVolumeList()` or anywhere in `StorageSessionController.java` from `onUnlockUser()` down to `initExternalStorageServiceComponent()`, re-check these exact lines survived the merge. Any future audit of this feature's remaining attack surface should grep the wider AOSP tree for `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM`/`UserHandle.USER_OWNER` comparisons and `userId == 0`-style literal checks inside per-user lifecycle callbacks and their own internal call chains (not just the first method reached from a lifecycle callback), since this exact shape of bug has now been found four times across this project by accident — three of them stacked directly underneath each other in this single incident — rather than by systematic search of a whole call chain up front. |
| ADR-084 | 2026-09-07 | Confirm the combined `0038`+`0039`+`0040` storage fix on physical `lynx` hardware against the exact original trigger; close the incident ADR-083 explicitly left open. | Accepted, confirms and closes ADR-083 | The exact original trigger scenario was repeated on the flashed combined candidate: fresh boot, then the decoy's PIN entered directly with the owner's own credential never entered this boot. Device-side evidence, checked directly rather than taken from a visual report: `adb shell dumpsys user` showed user 0 genuinely `RUNNING_LOCKED` (proving the owner's credential really was never entered) and the decoy (user 10, `org.aegisos.usertype.full.DECOY`) `RUNNING_UNLOCKED`. `adb shell sm list-volumes` showed both `private` and `emulated;10` as `mounted`. `adb logcat` showed the full volume lifecycle for `emulated;10` complete cleanly — `UNMOUNTED` → `CHECKING` → `MOUNTED`, `StorageSessionController` creating and starting its session, the mount broadcast delivered to `UserHandle{10}` — with zero occurrences of `ExternalStorageServiceException` anywhere in the buffer. `adb shell pidof com.android.settings` confirmed the Settings process alive and not killed. `adb shell dumpsys dropbox --print` showed no new crash entry since the boot the trigger was run against (only benign `system_server_strictmode`/`system_app_strictmode` noise, and the `SYSTEM_BOOT` entry itself). One warning-level stack trace appeared in logcat at the exact point storage initializes (`UserManager: The System (uid 1000) is fetching a copy of UserProperties on behalf of callingUid 0...`, from `StorageSessionController.getConnectionUserIdForVolume` via `UserManager.isMediaSharedWithParent`) and was investigated rather than assumed benign: it is AOSP's own pre-existing self-instrumentation warning about a `clearCallingIdentity` best practice, logged unconditionally at every call regardless of outcome, not a caught or swallowed exception — unrelated to the three-patch fix and present on ordinary (non-decoy) boots too. | The storage-unmount incident (`docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-storage-unmount-incident.md`) is now fully closed, not just build-verified. This is the fourth confirmed instance in this project of the same underlying pattern (a system service assuming user 0 always unlocks first) — keep watching for a fifth. Per ADR-083's own explicit instruction, this closure rests on the combination of volume state, logcat, dropbox, and process-liveness checks together, not on a visual "it didn't crash" report alone. | If a future AOSP/GrapheneOS source sync touches `StorageManagerService.getVolumeList()` or `StorageSessionController.java` between `onUnlockUser()` and `initExternalStorageServiceComponent()`, re-run this exact trigger and re-check all four signals before trusting a merge kept the fix intact. |
| ADR-085 | 2026-09-14 | Narrow the decision `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-duress-decoy-user-switcher-visibility-design.md` made — that pre-unlock concealment was out of scope, only the decoy's own session mattered — to cover pre-unlock surfaces too; land Component A (pre-unlock concealment) and Component B (single-credential-entry decoy unlock, a three-grant authorization primitive) of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-07-duress-decoy-seamless-unlock.md` in full; leave the plan's Task 15 (hardware closure) explicitly paused, not complete. | Accepted for Components A and B; plan status is **paused, not closed** | The owner asked (2026-09-07) to make Duress Unlock "perfect," naming three gaps the prior cycle's own design (`2026-09-03-duress-decoy-user-switcher-visibility-design.md:136-142`) had deliberately left open: the lock screen still advertised multiple users before any credential was entered; entering the decoy PIN demanded a second credential entry; the transition was visibly a user switch, not an unlock. This design narrows that prior decision — the owner's own unlocked session remains untouched, but concealment now also applies to every surface reachable while the device is locked. Component A (`UserManagerService.isUserSwitcherEnabled`'s second early return, gated on a decoy existing so a decoy-free device stays bit-for-bit stock) landed as `frameworks/base` patches `0041`+`0044` and closed the one real chokepoint-bypassing surface Task 2's exhaustive re-audit found (the `Multiuser` home-screen-and-keyguard widget, reachable via Communal Hub, rendering with no `isUserSwitcherEnabled()` gate at all — `packages/apps/Multiuser` patch `0004`). Component B was built, tested, reviewed, and hardware-validated across five sub-cycles, each catching a real defect the previous one missed: Tasks 6-9 (patches `0045`-`0053`) built and landed the original one-shot authorization and wired it into `UserController`'s forced-lock-skip decision — deliberately not AOSP's own `credential_capture`/`config_multiuserSkipKeyguardWhenSwitchingToUnlockedUsers` condition, the exact generic mechanism ADR-061 already found was a device-wide keyguard bypass. Task 11's real-touch-input testing (the first task in this whole cycle to drive the actual on-screen bouncer rather than the `cmd lock_settings verify` shell bypass every earlier task had used) found the single-credential promise was **not** actually delivered: a second, fully independent AOSP mechanism, `KeyguardViewMediator.handleUserSwitchComplete()`, still re-armed a live bouncer roughly 500ms after an authorized switch. Tasks 11a-11b (patches `0058`-`0065`) extended the authorization primitive to a second, independent single-use grant and wired it across the `system_server`/SystemUI process boundary to suppress that re-arm, closing the automatic prompt — verified with 3 clean real-touch trials. That still left a swipe-revealed fresh bouncer, since nothing had ever told the newly-current decoy session's own keyguard state that it was already credential-verified; Task 11c (patches `0066`-`0072`) added a third grant and reused a real, existing AOSP "already satisfied" dismiss chokepoint (the same one trust-agent and biometric unlocks already use) rather than suppressing anything — closing the single-PIN promise for real, confirmed 3/3 on real touch input, with the ADR-061 non-decoy regression case re-confirmed intact at every one of these five sub-cycles. Task 12's consolidated build (all 32 `frameworks/base` patches plus the one `Multiuser` patch, reproduced byte-for-byte from real branch history) passed the ADR-061 hard gate on the emulator. Task 13's systematic emulator sweep found two further real, disclosed (not fixed) gaps: Component B's failure-path fallback (`DecoyRoutingFailureMarker.REASON_QUIESCE_FAILED`) is dead code — `quiesceUserApps` cannot currently return `false` — and the owner's own "Select user" dialog can get stuck on stale data after a manual trigger, though the only reachable attack surface into it (the QS footer icon) is confirmed safe. Task 14 built and alpha-key-signed a physical `lynx` candidate (fingerprint `265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`, 13/13 AVB, independently re-confirmed a third time by the controller after the dispatching session was itself interrupted mid-task and had to resume from confirmed-real build-server state rather than assume anything was lost). The owner flashed it from their own workstation (files transferred and independently re-verified 13/13 before flashing, catching any transfer corruption first). The first real physical-hardware trial of the decoy scenario confirmed the single-credential-entry fix holds on hardware, not just the emulator (no second "Verifying primary lockscreen credential" line in the real device's own logcat) — but surfaced two things real hardware use exposes that no emulator trial had: a swipe is still required between the decoy PIN and the decoy's home screen (the design's own "zero interaction, no lock screen at all" framing was never claimed as achieved, only "no second PIN prompt" was — this is that known, disclosed gap, now hardware-confirmed rather than newly found), and total transition latency (~6.5s) sits at the slow end of the emulator's own measured cold-attempt range. The owner also reported, for the first time in this cycle, that the *return* path (decoy session back to the owner's own) requires two full password entries — a mechanism (`ReturnToRealUserHelper`) from an earlier project cycle that no task in this plan has read, tested, or touched. | Components A and B's core mechanisms are real, landed, reviewed, and repeatedly hardware/emulator-validated — do not treat either as unsafe or unbuilt. But do not describe this plan as complete: Task 15's remaining steps (the ADR-061 gate re-run on physical hardware, Task 13's full checklist re-run there, and physical validation of the seven still-open carried-over patches from ADR-082) have not run, and two real, owner-confirmed usability gaps remain unfixed — the owner→decoy swipe requirement and the decoy→owner double-password return path. The owner explicitly chose to pause here and resume in a future session rather than continue immediately; full detail for that session lives in `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-prelock-audit.md`'s "Task 15 (partial)" section, not repeated here. | Before any future session describes this plan as closed: physical hardware must re-run the ADR-061 gate and Task 13's checklist, the seven ADR-082 carried-over patches need their own physical validation, and both open usability gaps need their own investigation-then-fix cycles with the same ADR-061-aware rigor Tasks 11a-11c required — do not assume either is a small fix without first tracing the real mechanism, the same discipline that turned what looked like one bug (Task 11's "second bouncer") into three distinct root causes across Tasks 11a-11c. |
| ADR-086 | 2026-09-16 | Land the return-direction single-credential grant (`frameworks/base` `0073`-`0074`) and eliminate the swipe/pause between the decoy PIN and the decoy's home screen (`0075`-`0078`), closing the two open usability gaps ADR-085 carried forward. | Accepted, qualified — swipe-elimination hardware-confirmed; return-path grant and delay-removal are built, signed, and flashed but **not yet dynamically exercised on hardware** | `ReturnToRealUserHelper.maybeRouteToRealUser` matched the real owner's credential and called a bare `switchToUserSilently(USER_SYSTEM)` with no authorization mint at all — unlike the outbound direction, which needed three single-use grants across five sub-cycles (ADR-085) before a single credential entry actually held. Added `UserController#authorizeRealUserSwitchKeyguardSkip(int callerUserId)`, the return-direction mirror of `authorizeDecoySwitchKeyguardSkip`: always targets `UserHandle.USER_SYSTEM`, checks the *caller* is a genuine decoy-type user (the inverse of the outbound check, same ADR-064-motivated rigor), and mints from the same one-shot, three-grant record every `consumeDecoySwitchAuthorizationFor*` call site already consumes generically (`0073`-`0074`). Separately, real physical-hardware testing this session found the outbound direction's own single-credential fix still required a manual swipe after the PIN: `KeyguardViewMediator#handleUserSwitchComplete`'s authorized branch correctly skipped the automatic bouncer re-arm but did nothing else, leaving the device on a swipeable lock screen until the user's own next gesture happened to redeem the third (active-dismiss) grant. Fixed by proactively calling the same `dismiss()` the unauthorized branch already calls (`0075`-`0076`) — confirmed via real `adb logcat` evidence on a fresh boot (owner's credential never entered that boot, decoy PIN typed into the real bouncer): credential verified, then an automatic `keyguardDonePending` and `LOCKSCREEN→GONE` transition with **zero swipe**, ~2.2s total (down from ~4.8-6.5s including swipe-reaction time). The owner then reported the automatic dismiss still produced a perceptible pause, distinguishable from a real same-user unlock (owner-confirmed: PIN typed into an already-showing bouncer lands immediately, no delay). Traced to the new dismiss call reusing the unauthorized branch's existing `postDelayed(..., 500)` — original, pre-AegisOS AOSP code, there specifically to let a *freshly shown* bouncer's own async layout listeners settle, which does not apply to a branch that never shows a bouncer at all. Changed to `postDelayed(..., 0)` (`0077`-`0078`), keeping the handler-posted ordering and `mDismissToken` cancellation tagging. Both the return-path grant and delay-removal candidates were built (`OFFICIAL_BUILD=true`, all four gated apps confirmed present), signed with the alpha key (`265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`, rollback index `1783209600`), 13/13 AVB-verified on two independent machines and `avbtool` installs each round (fingerprint independently reconfirmed via two different `avbtool` subcommands each time, per the ADR-079 lesson about a self-consistent-but-wrong-key result), and flashed by the owner — the controller never had or requested access to the alpha private key. Both builds required a real, repeated rebuild correction: `m -j6 systemimage` twice left `SystemUI.apk` at its pre-patch mtime despite a clean log (the same `out-lynx` Ninja/Soong incremental-staleness class already recorded against this branch, e.g. `0008`/`0009`'s own incident), caught by checking the artifact's own mtime and fixed by force-`touch`ing the patched sources and invoking `m systemextimage systemimage` directly; the resulting `SystemUI.apk` was independently confirmed via `dexdump` disassembly of `handleUserSwitchComplete` itself to genuinely carry each change (the new branch structure, then the `#int 0` constant replacing `#int 500`), not assumed from the build log. A real incident during the first candidate's flash was found and recovered, not silently worked around: the controller's own flash instructions did not separate physical partitions (bootloader `fastboot`) from logical/dynamic ones (`product`, `system`, `system_ext`, `system_dlkm`, `vendor`, `vendor_dlkm` — require `fastbootd`), so the owner's first attempt failed six `resize-logical-partition` commands mid-run and the device booted in a genuinely mixed partition state; independently confirmed non-fatal (`ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=orange` — AVB is advisory on this unlocked bootloader, not enforced — `sys.boot_completed=1`, zero crash/ANR/tombstone) and recovered by completing the flash correctly in two stages (physical partitions, then an explicit `is-userspace: yes` wait before the logical partitions and `vbmeta`). | The return-path grant (`0073`-`0074`) and delay-removal (`0077`-`0078`) are compile-verified, bytecode-confirmed present in the built image, and flashed on a device that boots clean — but neither has been exercised through a real decoy→owner return or a real felt-latency PIN-entry trial. Do not describe either as hardware-confirmed; only the swipe-elimination fix (`0075`-`0076`) has direct logcat evidence of working as designed. All six patches exported fresh from the real post-`git am` branch history into `aegisos-platform/credential/duress-unlock-decoy/patches/frameworks-base/0073`-`0078`, with `SOURCE.md` updated in the same package. The `aegisos-duress-decoy-switch` branch stays as-is (not merged, PR'd, or discarded), matching every prior stage's precedent. | Revisit before treating either usability gap as closed: the return-path grant needs a real physical trial (decoy session, owner's own credential typed into the real bouncer to return, confirming a single entry and no forced re-lock) and the delay-removal needs a real PIN-entry trial confirming the felt latency genuinely reads as immediate rather than merely shorter. Also revisit if a future `out-lynx` build again silently omits a patched module despite a clean build log — this is now the third recorded instance of this exact staleness class on this branch and may warrant a standing pre-signing check rather than a per-incident rediscovery. |
| ADR-087 | 2026-09-17 | Fix the two defects ADR-086's candidate shipped to hardware: the decoy-to-owner return stranding the owner on the direct-boot placeholder (`frameworks/base` `0079`-`0080`), and the duress unlock visibly swiping itself up (`0081`-`0086`). Correct two claims ADR-086 made that turned out to be wrong. | Accepted — both defects hardware-confirmed fixed on the physical `lynx`; the security review of the new bouncer-retention behaviour is source-and-artifact level, and the timing side channel remains open and unmitigated | **ADR-086 is corrected on two points, per this log's own rule that a decision changes only through an entry naming it.** (1) ADR-086 described `0075`-`0076` as "hardware-confirmed" swipe elimination. That was wrong. The evidence behind it was logcat showing no *user gesture* was needed; it never established that no swipe was *rendered*, and the owner reported the device swiping itself up on the very next real trial. (2) ADR-086 recorded the return-path grant as merely "not yet dynamically exercised". It was in fact broken: `0073`-`0074` removed the second credential prompt, and that prompt was the only thing that ever unlocked the returning user's CE storage, because `verifyPrimaryCredentialNoRouting` (`0013`/`0014`) deliberately skipped every side effect of an ordinary verification and zeroized the synthetic password. Root-caused from the device's own event log, not inferred: `uc_switch_user 0` and `wm_resume_activity com.android.settings/.FallbackHome` at 18:09:43, then `uc_finish_user_unlocking: 0` followed by nothing — no `ssm_user_unlocking`, no `uc_finish_user_unlocked`, no `USER_UNLOCKED` — for 4m44s with no reboot, exactly `UserController.java:1006` returning false at `if (!mInjector.isCeStorageUnlocked(userId))`, one line after the event it did log. `0080` applies the same `onCredentialVerified` side effects an ordinary verification applies, deliberately **outside** the `mSpManager` lock (matching `doVerifyCredentialInner`'s own placement — that call makes binder calls into StorageManager and ActivityManager, and holding `mSpManager` across those is a system_server deadlock shape; verified at bytecode level in the shipped `services.jar`: `monitor-exit` at `003e`, `onCredentialVerified` at `004b`). It deliberately does not mirror `sendCredentialsOnUnlockIfRequired`, so a profile tied to the owner's credential stays locked after a return — accepted scope limit, least privilege over parity. **The swipe took three attempts, and the two failures are recorded because they are the reusable part.** Attempt 1 (`0081`-`0082`) set `KEYGUARD_GOING_AWAY_FLAG_NO_WINDOW_ANIMATIONS`; hardware-confirmed to change nothing, because the going-away is merged into the user-switch transition and animated by the Shell's `KeyguardTransitionHandler`, which dispatches on `TRANSIT_FLAG_KEYGUARD_GOING_AWAY` alone and contains no reference to the no-animation flag anywhere under `libs/WindowManager/Shell/src/com/android/wm/shell/keyguard/`. Attempt 2 (`0083`-`0084`) suppressed the canned unlock animation in `KeyguardUnlockAnimationController#playCannedUnlockAnimation`; confirmed working (`surfaceBehindEntryAnimator` never starts) and the swipe still survived it. Both are kept — each removes a real animation that would otherwise return — but neither was the cause. The cause was only visible once a lossless 64MiB logcat buffer was used; the first two diagnoses were made from a default-sized buffer that was silently dropping SystemUI lines during the switch burst, which is the direct methodological reason two wrong layers were fixed first. The real sequence: `RESET`, then `PRIMARY_BOUNCER -> LOCKSCREEN` (471ms) by `FromPrimaryBouncerTransitionInteractor`, then `keyguardGoingAway`, then `LOCKSCREEN -> GONE` (416ms) by `FromLockscreenTransitionInteractor` — the incoming user's lock screen animated in and then animated away again. An ordinary unlock on the same device, captured for comparison, goes `PRIMARY_BOUNCER -> GONE` in one step and never presents a lock screen. `resetStateLocked()` collapsing the bouncer during the switch drops `primaryBouncerShowing`, and `listenForPrimaryBouncerNotShowing` turns exactly that into a transition to `LOCKSCREEN`; everything after is forced. `0086` uses `resetStateLocked`'s existing `hideBouncer` parameter to leave the bouncer up for an authorized switch, keeping the state machine on the single-step path. Confirmed on hardware after flashing: `from=PRIMARY_BOUNCER, to=GONE` `STARTED`/`FINISHED`, and the owner's own verdict that it works. | The duress unlock now lands the way an ordinary unlock does, and the return path works from a fresh boot — the case that matters, since the defect only appeared when the duress PIN was the first credential entered after boot. **Security review of the new behaviour, since `0086` keeps a live bouncer across a user switch and upstream never does:** the bouncer cannot render its user-switcher/avatar header on this device — `config_enableBouncerUserSwitcher` reads `false` out of the actually built `SystemUI.apk` for `lynx` (`resource 0x7f050017 bool/config_enableBouncerUserSwitcher () false`), and the only SystemUI overlay on the product partition does not touch it, so `configureMode()` can only ever select `MODE_DEFAULT`. Note `vendor/adevtool/config/device/common/overlay-inclusion.yml` does list that key as `= true`, but it is adevtool's extraction inclusion list, not an applied product overlay — the built artifact settles it. The authorization surface is unchanged: the mint lives on `ActivityManagerInternal`, an in-process LocalService unreachable from apps or shell and only called after a real Gatekeeper-backed credential match; the consume side is Binder-reachable but every entry point is guarded by `checkHavePermission()` -> `ACCESS_KEYGUARD_SECURE_STORAGE` (`protectionLevel="signature\|setup"`) and fails closed on `RemoteException`. `0086` changes *when* SystemUI redeems the bouncer-skip grant, not who may. No new IPC endpoint or permission was added by `0079`-`0086`. The pre-switch unlock window `0080` introduces (user 0's CE opening roughly a second before the switch lands, while the decoy is still foreground) is covered at source level: `0010` gates notification suppression on `isUserQuiescedForDuress(userId)` at post time, a per-user check rather than per-process state, and `0011` only unquiesces once the user becomes current. | **The timing side channel is open and unmitigated, and is now the most security-relevant gap in this feature.** Measured on hardware after the fix: an ordinary owner unlock is 771ms from credential-verified to home; the duress unlock is ~2050ms (438ms credential-check plus routing and mint, 493ms switch machinery, 1110ms decoy-launcher cold start, 809ms transition tail). That gap is stopwatch-measurable by anyone who has seen the device unlock normally, which directly undermines the indistinguishability the feature exists for. Note the inverse constraint: making the duress path *faster* than an ordinary unlock would be an equal and opposite tell, so `TO_GONE_DURATION` (500ms, identical on both paths) must not be shortened. Pre-warming the decoy launcher cannot close the gap — `com.android.launcher3` is not `DIRECT_BOOT_AWARE` (confirmed via `dumpsys package`) and a warm-started decoy stays `RUNNING_LOCKED` until its own credential is verified, so its launcher physically cannot start before the duress PIN is typed; only the ~300-700ms overlap between CE-unlock and the switch machinery is recoverable. The obvious implementation of that overlap was deliberately **not** landed: `RootWindowContainer#canStartHomeOnDisplayArea` has no current-user check and `startHomeOnAllDisplays` goes through `startHomeOnTaskDisplayArea(..., onTop = true)`, which would bring the decoy's home forward while user 0 is still current, with only the bouncer between it and the owner's session. Also still open, all pre-existing: 12 of Task 2's 17 pre-unlock concealment surfaces remain UNCHECKED (Findings 3, 4, 8-17), and `credential_capture` is untouched. An apparent six-versus-twelve contradiction between `aegisos-platform`'s `SOURCE.md` ("the six UNCHECKED leak-audit items") and `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-prelock-audit.md` ("12 remain UNCHECKED") was chased down and is **not** a contradiction: they count two different lists. The six are ADR-072's own leak-audit items (Wi-Fi shared badge/dialog, uninstall dialogs, the status-bar icon, the Multiuser widget, Guardian's cross-user AppOps check, and `packages-apps-settings` `0007`), which `SOURCE.md` names explicitly as "the ADR-067/ADR-080 leak-audit's own narrower items". The twelve are Task 2's pre-unlock concealment surfaces (Findings 3, 4, 8-17) out of its original seventeen. Both counts are correct for their own list, and both lists are still open. | Revisit when the timing side channel is addressed — that needs its own design and an explicit owner decision, because the only mitigation that actually closes it is bringing both paths to the same observable duration, which means deliberately slowing every ordinary unlock. Also revisit before any future session attempts to suppress a keyguard animation: check `KeyguardTransitionRepository`'s state sequence with a resized logcat buffer *first*, since the window-animation and surface-behind layers underneath it are both dead ends for this class of problem. Finally, when either UNCHECKED count is cited, name which list it belongs to — the two are independent and have been misread as contradicting each other once already, in this entry's own first draft. |
| ADR-088 | 2026-09-17 | Drop the timing-side-channel work `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-duress-decoy-timing-overlap-design.md` designed, after the owner's own hard constraint (never slow the ordinary unlock) and real evidence together removed the basis for continuing it now. | Dropped, not implemented — the design document is kept as a record of what was investigated, not as a pending plan | ADR-087 recorded the timing side channel as a single-trial measurement (771ms ordinary vs "~2050ms" duress, itself never arithmetically reconciling with its own stated 438+493+1110+809=2850ms breakdown). This session re-measured the identical candidate (`fade68916b06`) directly on physical `lynx` across two reboots and found ordinary unlock stable (785-893ms) but duress unlock varying 1284-2764ms run to run — a spread larger than any plausible fix, including one unexplained 1347ms gap between the decoy launcher's process start and the actual user switch that nothing in the current architecture explains. Separately, and independently disqualifying, read-only research against the live tree found the design's proposed mechanism (pre-start the decoy home task via `onTop=false` during the switch instead of after it) unsafe as scoped: the only cross-user visibility gate in this tree, `ActivityRecord#showToCurrentUser()` -> `UserVisibilityMediator` (`mCurrentUserId == userId`), is already satisfied before a pre-start could occur, because `mCurrentUserId` flips to the decoy's id as soon as the decoy user process starts (`UserVisibilityMediator#assignUserToDisplayOnStart`) — necessarily earlier than switch-complete. `onTop=false` only sets launch z-order; it provides no compositing protection during that window. A safe version needs new, security-critical WindowManager logic (a per-task suppress-compositing flag, cleared only on confirmed switch-complete) that is its own design and its own security review, not an addition to this one. Separately confirmed: `TO_GONE_DURATION` (500ms/200ms, `FromPrimaryBouncerTransitionInteractor.kt`) is a fixed-duration animation not gated on launcher draw-readiness, so even the design's own safe fallback (process-pre-fork-only, no early window) would not shorten the visible transition — only avoid a possible blank-frame glitch during it. | The timing side channel remains open and unmitigated, exactly as ADR-087 left it — this entry does not close it, it records that this session's specific attempt to partially close it is abandoned. The credential-check and grant-authorization mechanics investigated for this work are sound and reusable (`DecoyProfileHelper.performDecoySwitch`, `UserController`'s grant record and intervening-switch invalidation at lines 2775/2810/2932/2973) should a future session pursue the larger WM-suppression redesign instead. No code changed; nothing was built, flashed, or landed. | Revisit only if a future session is willing to scope a dedicated WindowManager-suppression design (new per-task compositing gate) with its own security review, or if the owner reconsiders the constraint that the ordinary unlock may never be slowed — either would change the calculus recorded here. Do not re-attempt the `onTop=false` pre-start mechanism as scoped in the dropped design without first addressing the `showToCurrentUser()` timing gap directly. |

Corrects ADR-091's own citation error for Task 3's squash commit hash (recorded as `949164d7ae30`, which is actually an unrelated commit — the real Task 3 squash commit is `64187dc103b5`, confirmed by `git merge-base --is-ancestor` and by the resource value reading `false` in the current tree). | **Closed.** All 9 tasks complete with real evidence; `atest` for `RealUserQuiescerTest`/`UserRepositoryImplTest` remains genuinely UNEXECUTED (no adb-reachable device existed on the build server; not silently dropped, see Consequences). | Task 7 (build) required two additional real fixes beyond Tasks 1-6, neither anticipated by ADR-091: `5c837bb3c965` migrated `ReferenceGlobalRootComponent.java` to Kotlin (KSP, the module's annotation processor since an October-2025 upstream commit, cannot process plain `.java` `@Component` sources — masked for six weeks by stale incremental Soong cache); `e6f6b9363b83` then `f3f3c9ebc980` fixed a real Dagger dependency cycle Task 6's own `KeyguardRepository` injection introduced into `UserRepositoryImpl` — the first attempt (`e6f6b9363b83`, wrapping in `dagger.Lazy`) compiled clean and passed a dex-level bytecode check, but the `.get()` call still ran synchronously inside the constructor, so the cycle re-entered at runtime as a genuine `StackOverflowError` that crash-looped `SystemUI` on the owner's actual physical device after flashing — caught only because a real boot test was insisted on despite the build and bytecode check both passing; the corrected fix (`f3f3c9ebc980`) defers the call into a cold `flow{}` builder so it only runs after Dagger's `DoubleCheck` has memoized the singleton. Signed with the alpha key (fingerprint `265ade399a0f3ee65003e56daef26a8f4d70e6a4`), 13/13 AVB checks independently verified on two machines with two different `avbtool` binaries. Task 8's real hardware evidence (`docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-prelock-audit.md`'s own "Task 8" section, pasted here, not summarized): flash verification PASS (fingerprint, verified boot state, zero crash/ANR/tombstone over 31+ minutes); ADR-061 gate PASS (`LockSettingsService: Verifying primary lockscreen credential for user 11` → `Successfully verified`, no device-wide bypass); Component A PASS 4/4 surfaces (lock screen, bouncer, QS, power menu — zero `multi_user`/`avatar` matches); owner-regression PASS (`multi_user_switch` icon `enabled=true` immediately after a lock→unlock with zero `Settings.Global` writes, Select-user dialog shows both "Owner" and the decoy — this is Task 6's fix, directly validated on hardware, closing Task 13's own prior narrower residual that the dialog's content itself had never been exercised); Component B real scenario PASS (one credential entry, no second prompt, real user stayed `RUNNING_LOCKED`, decoy reached `RUNNING_UNLOCKED`); Component B failure path UNCHECKED (source-read `CachedAppOptimizer.forceFreezeAppAsyncLSP` — fundamentally an async handler-message post with no organic synchronous `RuntimeException` path; Task 5's fix is real, correct plumbing, but the `catch` block itself has no safe way to trigger under real conditions); Component C latency PASS (warm n=5, 727-735ms, median 732ms; cold n=3 not 5, 1311-1350ms, median 1322ms, two further attempts lost to disclosed `uiautomator` timing flakiness, not a feature defect); all seven ADR-082 avatar-color patches confirmed present on the branch, the two never-before-exercised ones (`0037`, Multiuser `0003`) confirmed at the bytecode level (`UserManager.isMainForUiDisplay(I)Z`) in the real built `Settings.apk`/`Multiuser.apk`, live pixel-color observation UNCHECKED (tooling limitation — `uiautomator` has no color data, `screencap` already documented unreliable for this Compose SystemUI). Squashed-patch record (Task 1's audit, `docs/devices/lynx-duress-unlock-patch-audit.md`): three dead-end sequences reduced `frameworks-base`+`build-release`'s combined patch count from 104 to 102 net (before Tasks 5-9's own additions) — `build-release`'s `credential_capture` enable/revert cycle (four patches → one, `0ded9b0e3972`), a `frameworks-base` resource flip reverted inside a later, differently-purposed patch (two patches' worth → dropped, real squash commit `64187dc103b5`, correcting ADR-091 as above), and a pure path-fix follow-up (two patches → one, `babea734ee48`). Accepted residual risks, each already established and re-affirmed here, not newly discovered: (1) the timing side-channel work was dropped (ADR-088) — the owner's own hard constraint (never slow the ordinary unlock) removed the only real mitigation; (2) Finding 13's re-lock sub-scenario (ADR-090) is proven untestable on this device — a stock GrapheneOS/AOSP Communal Hub gap, not an AegisOS defect; (3) 8 of 10 remaining pre-unlock concealment findings (Findings 9-12, 14-17) are source-verified code-absence facts with no dynamic test possible or needed; (4) ADR-072's six UNCHECKED leak-audit items have no trigger fixture on this device and match this project's own established precedent for that class of gap. | `frameworks-base` real tip: `f3f3c9ebc980` (90 patches: 87 from ADR-091 + the three Task-7 patches above — `5c837bb3c965` the Kotlin migration, `e6f6b9363b83` and `f3f3c9ebc980` the two-attempt Dagger-cycle fix). This is the first time in this plan's history that the candidate actually flashed, booted, and survived real-hardware verification — and the first attempt at that exact step (Task 7's own dex-checked build) silently shipped a crash loop despite every automated check passing, which is the strongest evidence yet in this project's own history for its standing rule that a build/bytecode check is not hardware verification. `RealUserQuiescerTest`/`UserRepositoryImplTest` remain never executed via real `atest` — carried forward as a genuinely open item, not closed by assertion; the underlying diffs are small, direct, and independently source-verified, and Component B's real scenario (which exercises `RealUserQuiescer.quiesce()`'s success path directly) and the owner-regression evidence (which exercises `UserRepositoryImpl`'s fixed flow directly) both provide real, if partial, dynamic coverage of the same code without the named test classes themselves ever running. | Re-open this plan only for: (1) actually executing `RealUserQuiescerTest`/`UserRepositoryImplTest` via real `atest`, which needs either a build-server-reachable device or a working build-server emulator with `adb` attached — neither existed as of this closure; (2) Component B's failure path, if a safe, non-source-modifying way to trigger a real process-freeze failure is ever found; (3) live avatar-color pixel verification, if a reliable screenshot method for this Compose SystemUI is ever established; (4) either open UX gap this plan never touched (the owner→decoy swipe residual, the decoy→owner double-password path) — both explicitly out of this plan's scope per `docs/devices/lynx-duress-decoy-prelock-audit.md`'s own "Task 15 (partial)" section, not silently forgotten. |

Attempt 2: `delta_generator` (payload signing, part of GrapheneOS's own unconditional `ota_from_target_files` step inside `generate-release.sh` — confirmed against `github.com/GrapheneOS/script` branch `17` that this step cannot be skipped even when only an image bundle is wanted) crashed `exit -11` (SIGSEGV on a null pointer) right after swap had been disabled for the key-in-swap-risk fix; kernel log showed `__vm_enough_memory: ... delta_generator, no enough memory for the allocation`. Attempt 3: `zram` (2GB, RAM-only, no disk exposure) was tried as a safer substitute for disk swap but the OTA payload is dense, poorly-compressible data, so it provided little real headroom; `delta_generator` was OOM-killed for real (`exit -9`). Fix: restored the original disk swap (uncommented in `/etc/fstab`, matches the exact configuration all three earlier successful ceremonies — `2026081901`, `2026082507`, `2026082611` — ran under). Attempt 4 succeeded: `AVB verification passed`, `otacerts.zip subject in the signed target_files: subject=CN = GrapheneOS`, published `lynx-img-20260927140013.zip`. | The theoretical unencrypted-swap key-exposure risk (see the engineering notes) is back in effect — no different from every ceremony this project ran before 2026-09-26, but explicitly re-accepted here rather than quietly reverted. Independent re-verification (checklist step 9, "two separate machines") was not completed: the current release's AVB public key exists only inside the vault, not as a pre-extracted PEM on the build host, so `avbtool verify_image` there failed with "Embedded public key does not match" against the stale `<build-root>/keys/alpha/alpha_key_pub.pem` (a different, earlier key generation, confirmed by the mismatch itself). What was checked from the build host instead: the bundle's own sha256, its 25-file/13-AVB-partition set matches the proven flash list exactly, rollback index `1788220800` is higher than the last accepted candidate's `1783209600`, and `otacerts.zip` shows the real cert subject. | ADR-107 | 2026-09-28 | Amend ADR-106: correct design §6.2's Device-ID (SDP) scoping mechanism from `BTA_DmSetLocalDiRecord` to `system/stack/sdp/sdp_server.cc`'s `process_service_search_attr_req` per-connection attribute-response path. | Accepted | Task 1 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-airpods-platform-integration.md`, executed against the real pinned Bluetooth stack source on the build server (idle at the time; no writes made — pure `grep`/`sed -n` reads), found `BTA_DmSetLocalDiRecord` (and the `SDP_SetLocalDiRecord` it calls) takes no peer/BD_ADDR parameter at all: it is called once at Bluetooth-enable time and writes a single global local SDP record answered identically to every peer's SDP query. | ADR-110 | 2026-09-28 | Adopt the Personal Stable channel exactly as specified in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-personal-stable-trust-tier-design.md`: a single-owner, single-device production-trust tier that keeps Stable's technical controls (release key material only in the signing host's encrypted vault, a logged ceremony, independent reproducibility, bootloader relock with rollback enforcement verified by a clean post-relock boot) and drops only Stable's multi-custodian and separation-of-duties requirements. | Accepted | The owner approved the design on 2026-09-27.
