No existing channel covers one owner who wants a relocked, release-signed phone for their own daily use: Alpha provides no production trust, and Stable requires a second custodian who does not exist. The dropped controls stop one malicious operator from shipping trojaned code to *other people's* devices; with exactly one device, owned and operated by that same person, they add no protection. ADR-028 used the same reasoning for single-operator Alpha. | `docs/release/supply-chain-and-signing.md` gains a Personal Stable row. The design's scoping guardrails bind every Personal Stable build: one named device, serial kept only in protected operations records, the single owner as sole custodian, a key distinct from any future public Stable key. Public Stable is unchanged and Personal Stable evidence never carries over to it. ADR-111 and ADR-112 apply the tier to `stallion` with recorded deviations. | A second device, a second person, or any public distribution of a Personal Stable build (must go through real Stable instead); any proposed change to the design's guardrails. |
| ADR-111 | 2026-09-28 | Make Pixel 10a (`stallion`) the owner's daily-use device under Personal Stable (ADR-110); `lynx` stays the development and Alpha device. Four scoped decisions: (1) once relocked, `stallion` receives only `user`-variant Personal Stable builds, never Alpha or development builds; (2) the `stallion` key set generated in the signing host's vault on 2026-09-28 (AVB public-key SHA-1 `089b874ee4419a8c39c37374386a161a6cf2ee26`) is re-designated from Alpha to `stallion`'s Personal Stable key; (3) amend the design's guardrail (b): `stallion`'s Personal Stable OTAs may be published on the project's existing private update server as `stallion` `stable` channel metadata; (4) every `stallion` ceremony runs with no disk-backed swap active on the signing host. | Accepted | (1) Owner decision 2026-09-28: daily use on the newer device, keep developing on `lynx`, update by OTA. Relocking and unlocking both wipe userdata, so the device is relocked once, before any personal data, and updated only by signed OTA afterward. `user` because a `userdebug` build keeps `adb root`, which undoes much of what relocking buys on a daily device. (2) The key set was generated the same day, per grapheneos.org/build, in the same encrypted vault and with the same handling the design requires. It is distinct from any public Stable key (none exists), which is the design's only key-distinctness rule. A second `stallion` key set would add a key nobody uses, in a vault already near its 3.8MB capacity. (3) The guardrail exists so that no other person's device receives the build. A `stallion` Personal Stable OTA installs only on a device whose `otacerts.zip` carries this release certificate and whose bootloader has this AVB key enrolled: the owner's one unit. The existing server reuses the hardware-proven pipeline (ADR-050) and the Updater's built-in URL; the alternative, `adb sideload` from the workstation, gives up automatic updates. Residual: anyone who guesses the URL can download the signed OTA zip, the same exposure ADR-050 already accepted for `lynx` Alpha builds; it contains no secrets. (4) The design retains "no swappable disk exposure for key material during a ceremony". The 2026-09-27 `lynx` ceremony re-enabled disk swap on the signing host to get past an OOM (`docs/product/2026-09-27-upstream-update-2026091900-session.md`); tolerated for Alpha then, not allowed for Personal Stable. | Supersedes the Alpha designation in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-27-stallion-device-onboarding.md` Task 5, and that plan's Tasks 6-8, replaced by `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-stallion-daily-use-personal-stable.md`. Anything this key signed before this entry (the owner's 2026-09-28 signing attempts on candidate `2026092700`, a `userdebug` build) is not a Personal Stable release: it is never published and never installed on the relocked device. The `stallion` key never signs an Alpha or development build. Before opening the vault, every `stallion` ceremony confirms `swapon --show` lists no disk-backed device; if the ceremony runs out of memory, the fix is more signing-host RAM, never disk swap. The expected post-relock state is `ro.boot.verifiedbootstate=yellow` (custom root of trust) with `ro.boot.vbmeta.device_state=locked`, not `green`. Owner decision 2026-09-28: reproducibility comes later and is not a priority now. Recorded as an explicit deferral instead of a silent skip, so the gap stays visible. | Until an independent rebuild matches byte-for-byte, every `stallion` ceremony record and `docs/devices/support-policy.md` entry marks the device's Personal Stable status as "reproducibility pending". | ADR-113 | 2026-09-28 | Retire every `stallion` key generated on 2026-09-28 as exposed, and generate a fresh `stallion` Personal Stable key set before any Personal Stable ceremony. Two rules are also enforced on the signing host for every device: a ceremony refuses to run unless `<vault>` is an actual mountpoint, and it refuses to run while any non-zram swap is active. Memory shortfalls are fixed with more signing-host RAM (owner target 6GB, up from 1.9GB). | Accepted | Owner asked for a new key; read-only inspection of the signing host the same day (agent access granted by the owner) confirmed it is required, not just preferable. (a) With the vault closed, the plain directory under the mountpoint (`<vault>/keys/stallion/` with the vault unmounted, on the unencrypted `/home` LV) held a complete private key set in plaintext: all nine `.pk8`, `avb.pem`, `id_ed25519`, dated 09:28. These were writes made while the vault mount had silently failed. The in-vault `avb.pem` is the same AVB key (SHA-1 `089b874ee4419a8c39c37374386a161a6cf2ee26`), so that key has sat on unencrypted disk. (b) `/proc/swaps` showed disk-backed swap (`/dev/dm-2`, the `swap_1` LV) active by default on every boot, including during that day's attempts, one of which was OOM-killed in re-verification. (c) One attempt (09:23) completed `generate-release.sh`, so this key signed a `userdebug` OTA. (d) The scripts' vault check tested only that `releasekey.pk8` exists, which the plaintext copy satisfied. With the vault closed, a ceremony would have signed from the unencrypted copy. (e) Every failed attempt's `generate-release.log` lived in `/dev/shm` and was wiped by the shutdown trap. That is why seven failures that day had no readable cause. | Owner actions, all on the signing host: destroy the plaintext copies under the vault mountpoint with `shred -u` (best effort only on a journaled filesystem on LVM, which is exactly why the keys are retired rather than cleaned and reused); delete the retired set inside the vault; `swapoff` the `swap_1` LV, remove it from `/etc/fstab` and zero it; raise the VM's RAM. Then generate a new set with the vault mount verified (`mountpoint`) immediately before and after, `.pk8` via `make_key` with a blank password and `avb.pem` via `openssl genrsa 4096 \| openssl pkcs8 -topk8 -nocrypt`. The latter is required by `decrypt-keys`' `-n $password` check (`.claude/local-server-memory.md`). Record the new AVB fingerprint. `tools/signing/sign-ota.sh` and `sign-baseline-images.sh` now enforce both host rules in a preflight that runs before any staging dir or shutdown trap exists (verified on the host: vault closed → `VAULT NOT MOUNTED ... refusing to run`, exit 1, VM stays up), and keep the last 300 lines of `generate-release.log`/`verify.log` in `ceremony-log/` on failure. Not decided here: the `lynx` Alpha key went through the 2026-09-27 ceremony with disk swap deliberately re-enabled, the same exposure class as (b). Whether to rotate it is an open owner decision. | New key generated and fingerprint recorded; any future finding of key material outside the mounted vault; any ceremony that needs disk swap to finish. |
| ADR-114 | 2026-09-29 | Treat a decoy registration whose user no longer exists as "no decoy" when routing a wrong PIN, and leave upstream's "Already tried that PIN" duplicate-guess handling exactly as it is. Ship the fix in `stallion`'s `2026092802` candidate and later. | Accepted | Owner-reported on `stallion` and `lynx`: after a wrong PIN the screen turns off, and repeating the same PIN shows "Already tried that PIN". Root cause, from `stallion` logcat and its `locksettings.db`: `decoy_profile_state` stayed `0:10` after the decoy user was removed with `pm remove-user`, so the first wrong PIN was verified against user 10. That user has no protector, its `LskfIdentifier` reads as a special credential, and `SoftwareRateLimiter.writeStats` threw `IllegalArgumentException: Unknown special user id 10` out of `checkCredential` into SystemUI, which crashed and restarted (six identical `am_crash` entries in one log buffer). A repeated identical wrong guess is a duplicate: no hardware write, no crash, and the bouncer shows the upstream message. That message and the duplicate handling are unmodified upstream code and a rate-limit mitigation, so they are deliberately untouched. The fix is one guard in `DecoyProfileHelper.maybeRouteToDecoy`: a missing or `partial` decoy user takes the same path as no decoy at all, including the equivalent-cost dummy check, so timing matches a device with no decoy. No new permission, IPC endpoint or API. | Real branch `frameworks/base` `sovereignos-2026091900-r2` commit `c013345b2161`; package patch `credential/duress-unlock-decoy/patches/frameworks-base/0011` with root cause and evidence in that package's `SOURCE.md`. Hardware evidence on `stallion` (`user` build `2026092802`): all three core Duress scenarios still pass, and with the decoy removed and the registration left in place, five verifications of user 0, none of user 10, no `am_crash`, no SystemUI restart. Limits, stated plainly: no unit test exists for `DecoyProfileHelper`; on a `user` build the registration cannot be read, so its persistence rests on the same procedure leaving `0:10` earlier the same day; not tested on `lynx`; the registration is still not cleared when the decoy user is removed. | `lynx` rebuild (unverified there until then); a user-removal hook that clears the registration (this guard then becomes defence in depth); any proposal to change duplicate-guess handling needs its own ADR. |
| ADR-115 | 2026-09-29 | Amend ADR-108 and correct landed patch `0001`: replace the interop-database registration with a dedicated exact-match, in-memory, bond-gated registry; serve the SDP vendor override from a per-response copy; clear the registration on unbond; add the missing client wrapper `BluetoothDevice#setAirPodsIdentity`. Landed as additive patches `0002`-`0005`; `0001` alone must not ship. | Accepted (uncompiled, see Consequences) | A pre-build review of `0001` against the live pinned tree found five defects. D1: `sdp_dynamic_change_airpods_vendor` mutates the shared SDP record (`sdpint.h:96`, `sdp_db.cc:564`) with no restore, unlike `hfp_fallback` (`sdp_server.cc:182`), so after one AirPods SDP query every peer sees Apple: the device-wide spoof design §3.2 forbids. D2: length `6` is passed to `interop_database_add_addr`, which asserts `length < sizeof(RawAddress)` (`interop.cc:1170-1172`) with a fatal log (`log.h:202-210`), aborting the Bluetooth process on first registration; length 5 would only prefix-match (`interop_database_match`, `INTEROP_BL_TYPE_ADDR` case). D3: interop entries persist to `/data/misc/bluedroid/interop_database_dynamic.conf` and are never removed on unbond. D4: `interop_match_addr_or_name` also matches by device name. D5: no client path to `IBluetooth#setAirPodsIdentity` (`BluetoothAdapter#getBluetoothService()` is package-private). ADR-108's intent (per-accessory scoping, bonded BD_ADDR only, dedicated signature permission) is unchanged; its mechanism "reuse the interop database" is replaced. | Registry: header-only, max 16 exact 6-byte peers, in memory only; `airpods_identity_applies()` = registered AND `BTM_IsBonded` (injected checker, fails closed). Registration cleared in `AdapterService#handleBondStateChanged` on `BOND_NONE`; refusing to register a non-bonded device is kept, clearing is always allowed. New `bt_interface_t::set_airpods_identity`, `setAirPodsIdentityNative`, `AdapterNativeInterface#setAirPodsIdentity`. `INTEROP_AIRPODS_IDENTITY` removed. Landed on the build-server branch `sovereignos-airpods-identity` as `593ddedbad`, `98c91e16d3`, `8a31c37d88`, `b86ac07d9f` (`git am` exit 0; all 17 touched files byte-identical to the worktree by sha256); committed in `sovereignos-platform` as `1ace719` (patches `0002`-`0005`, `airpods/SOURCE.md`). **None of this is compiled or run yet**: per owner directive the single rebuild follows all planned work; the build plan starts with host compiles of `net_test_device` and `net_test_stack_sdp`. New validation cases `AIR-029`-`AIR-032` cover the second-peer regression, lifecycle, process stability, and permission enforcement. | ADR-119 | 2026-09-29 | Land patch `0006` on the Bluetooth module: `BluetoothDevice#setAirPodsMetadata` (`@SystemApi`) and `IBluetooth#setAirPodsMetadata`, gated by `MANAGE_AIRPODS_IDENTITY`, an 18-key allow-list, and a device that is bonded and registered through `setAirPodsIdentity`. Design points, each read from the pinned tree: the write reuses `AdapterService#setMetadata` and so the same storage and change-listener path; the platform already deletes a device's metadata on unbond (`BluetoothStorageManager.kt:254-256`, `:862-866`); the bond state is set (`BondStateMachine.java:650`) before `AdapterService#handleBondStateChanged` runs (`:709`), so the unbond hook sees a bond already gone. The native registry has no getter, so the registered set is kept in Java (`AirPodsIdentityTracker`) under one lock shared by register, unbond and write, held across the storage write: a write cannot run after an unbond has completed, and a failure to clear native still drops the Java entry. Keys refused on purpose: `ENHANCED_SETTINGS_UI_URI` (makes Settings launch a URI), `COMPANION_APP`, `DEVICE_TYPE`, manufacturer, model and version. | ADR-122 | 2026-10-01 | `AirPodsApp` serves Settings' own Bluetooth device page through the DeviceSettings contract: the contract classes are **vendored** into the app (40 files, byte-identical to the build tree, guarded by `airpods/tools/check-devicesettings-vendoring.sh`); the config service and the device-settings service are `exported="false"` and additionally check that the caller is the system uid or ourselves; the ring icons come from a read-only, unexported provider with a fixed allow-list and a persistable grant to `com.android.settings`; metadata is written only through `BluetoothDevice#setAirPodsMetadata` in the order icons, untethered flag, battery and charging, key 25 last, and the first set-up is repeated until it succeeds; a debuggable build (`Build.TYPE` not `user`) can be fed a state through the global setting `airpods_debug_state` and has no component or endpoint for it; Settings' own action buttons (Forget, Disconnect) stay on the page. | Accepted (compiled and seen on a device with a debug state; no real session) | SettingsLib is a platform-API module and Soong refuses it as a static dependency of a `system_current` app, so vendoring is the only route without a platform signature. | ADR-124 | 2026-10-02 | The AirPods session speaks the real accessory protocol (`docs/research/airpods-aap-protocol-facts.md`) through `AapCodec`, which replaces the placeholder parser; the channel is opened by reflection on the hidden `BluetoothDevice#createL2capSocket(int)` under the existing single-package hidden-API allow-list (the constructor route of ADR-118's evidence no longer exists in the tree); the session runs in a foreground service of type `connectedDevice`, started from the protected Bluetooth broadcasts and at process start, which adds `FOREGROUND_SERVICE`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_CONNECTED_DEVICE` and the privileged `START_FOREGROUND_SERVICES_FROM_BACKGROUND`; a device is a candidate when it is bonded and lists the accessory service UUID and is published only after a valid frame; the page state follows only what the AirPods report; while not connected the page offers no control. **Decisions at the capture gate (owner, 2026-10-02, after `AIR-121` to `AIR-123`), and what became of them:** (1) hide the off button until the device reports off, (2) show the written value of the comfort settings because they are not reported back, (3) hide the ear detection switch because this pair never reports it. **After seeing the result the owner reversed (1) and (3): every designed control must be present and must work.** Now: Off is always offered and the app first enables the AirPods' "Allow Off" option (`0x34`), then asks for off (proven with the probe); the ear detection switch is always offered with the value the phone last wrote (kept in the app's private storage, default on), and its real effect on the AirPods is **still to be proven** (`AIR-126`); (2) stands (conversation awareness takes effect, proven by a fresh read); (4) state changes that depend on the current state go through an atomic transform. The public socket APIs open LE channels only. A cached app is frozen after about 30 s (ADR-122), so a long-lived session needs a foreground service, and the background start needs the privileged permission or an exemption we do not have. Evidence-based controls avoid a model table. | Three new permissions (one privileged, allow-listed). One new hidden-API use (a method, same package). A silent notification while connected. The listening-mode, ear-detection and conversation-awareness commands are sent to real hardware. The comfort switches can show a written value the device silently ignored until the next connection corrects it (ear detection is never corrected: this pair does not report it). Enabling "Allow Off" changes a persistent setting of the owner's AirPods. The protocol notes are third-party and model-specific: the capture on the owner's pair is the check, and other models are untested. | Any change of the hidden-API enforcement or of `BluetoothDevice#createL2capSocket`; a model whose frames the codec rejects; Apple changing the protocol; a decision to ship to other users (model allow-list, consent for the grant). |
| ADR-125 | 2026-10-02 | Ear detection is a host function. The app acts on the AirPods' placement report (`EarState`, opcode `0x0006`): one pod out pauses and putting it back resumes only what it paused; both out (a pod in the case counts as out) stops playback, never resumes by itself, and takes the AirPods out of the active A2DP/HFP slots so audio and calls go to the phone; the switch off disables all of it. The slot change is made by one narrow `@SystemApi` call, `BluetoothDevice#setAirPodsAudioRoute(boolean)` (patch `0007`), behind `MANAGE_AIRPODS_IDENTITY`, for a bonded and registered device only, which undoes only what it did and is undone on disconnect, unbond, adapter off, caller death and session end. The app does **not** receive `MODIFY_AUDIO_ROUTING`. The session also starts after boot (`RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, normal permission). Conversation awareness is a host function too: the AirPods' speech signal (opcode `0x4B`, captured on the owner's pair) lowers the music stream volume to the level the owner chose on the page (20, 35, 50 or 70 percent of the current volume, default 50) and gives it back when the signal says the conversation is over, only if the owner did not change the volume meanwhile; it needs no permission. The case battery keeps its last reported value (per device, app-private storage) so the ring is shown at all times. **Updated 2026-10-04:** the sentence that conversation awareness "needs no permission" is superseded by ADR-127 (the audio hardening ignores the app's own volume calls); the end of a conversation is the level `04`, `06` (in the source, not in image `2026100210`), `08` or `09`, or 1 s after a `0B` or 8 s after any other level with nothing following (`AIR-155`, `AIR-158`). | Proposed (2026-10-04: device evidence for `AIR-131` to `AIR-135`, `AIR-137`, `AIR-140` and `AIR-149`; still Open: `AIR-136` (a call), `AIR-138` (a second peer), `AIR-139` (probable only), `AIR-142` to `AIR-145` (skipped by the owner), `AIR-147` and `AIR-148` (live case value, moved to Plan 7B). Accepted only when every row has evidence) | Evidence: `AIR-126` log of the 2026-10-02 session (six `EarState` frames, zero AVRCP pass-through); Apple Support on Automatic Ear Detection; `MODIFY_AUDIO_ROUTING` carries 53 `AudioManager` APIs (`core/res/AndroidManifest.xml:7595`), so a narrow call in the Bluetooth module (the ADR-117/119 pattern) is the only option that does not widen the app. Alternatives rejected: `MODIFY_AUDIO_ROUTING` for the app (reduces security), `BluetoothAdapter#setActiveDevice` (needs `BLUETOOTH_PRIVILEGED`, rejected in ADR-117), pausing only (does not meet "exactly like an iPhone"). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-02-airpods-plan7a2-ear-detection.md`. |
| ADR-126 | 2026-10-02 | Adaptive is the third of four buttons of the native Listening mode group, in the iPhone's order Off, Transparency, Adaptive, Noise cancel; it is offered once the pair reports adaptive mode or the adaptive strength (control `0x2E`), and it sends mode 4 through the existing command path. The strength slider is not part of the native page (the DeviceSettings contract has no slider item) and belongs to Plan 7B; `2E` is never written because the pair gives no report back. This replaces the three-button decision of section 4.2 of the settings UI design for the native page. | Proposed (2026-10-04: `AIR-146` Pass on the device; `AIR-147` (font scale) still Open; Accepted with ADR-125) | Owner request 2026-10-02 ("iPhone original"). Evidence: capture B (mode 4 accepted and reported back, `2E 32` in the connection burst, no report for `2E` writes). Apple lists Adaptive on AirPods Pro 2 and some other models only, so the button is evidence-driven like every other control. |
| ADR-127 | 2026-10-03 | The music volume is lowered and restored by one narrow Bluetooth call, not by the app's own volume API. On the device (2026-10-03, image `2026100206`) the audio hardening of this OS ignored every `AudioManager.setStreamVolume` of `org.sovereignos.airpods` (`AS.HardeningEnforcer: AudioHardening volume control for api 100 ignored ... level: full ... exemption: 0`); the plan's assumption that these calls need no permission was wrong for this OS. The call is `BluetoothDevice#setAirPodsMusicDuck(boolean duck, int percent)` (`@SystemApi`, patch `0009`) behind `MANAGE_AIRPODS_IDENTITY`: only for a bonded, registered device that is the active A2DP device; `percent` is one of 20, 35, 50, 70; it lowers STREAM_MUSIC to that share of the current volume (never below the stream minimum, never raising it) and restores only if the volume is still the value it set; it also restores when the caller dies, the device disconnects or is unbonded, or Bluetooth turns off. The Bluetooth process is exempt from the hardening by its uid; the hardening is unchanged for every other app. The app gets no new permission. | Proposed (2026-10-04: `AIR-141`, `AIR-153` and `AIR-154` Pass for lowering, restoring at the `04` frame and the death restore; `AIR-142` to `AIR-145` Open (skipped by the owner); the 1 s and 8 s silence timeouts are in image `2026100210` and not yet tried. Accepted only when every row has evidence) | Alternatives rejected: `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS_PRIVILEGED` for the app (lets it change any volume and the ringer, which is what the hardening forbids, and breaks the rule of no new permission); a lower `targetSdk` so the hardening falls to its partial level (a regression of a mitigation); audio focus `MAY_DUCK` (the owner's chosen level cannot be honoured and the focus request is hardened as well). Evidence: `AIR-153`, source `frameworks/base/services/core/java/com/android/server/audio/HardeningEnforcer.java` `blockVolumeMethod` (exempt: `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS_PRIVILEGED`, `MODIFY_AUDIO_ROUTING`, `MODIFY_PHONE_STATE`, uid below 10000, exact alarm). Supersedes the sentence of ADR-125 that conversation awareness needs no permission. |
| ADR-128 | 2026-10-04 | Close `AIR-148` (the live case battery like an iPhone): the page keeps the last known case value, the app does not scan Bluetooth and gets no `BLUETOOTH_SCAN`. | Accepted | Measured with the debug probe on the owner's pair (`AIR-160`, 30 s runs): no Apple type `0x07` proximity message while the pair is connected (pods in the ears, and pods in the case with the lid open and the link up), while other Apple message types arrive (`0x10` 563 to 621, `0x12` 80 to 300, `0x16` 10 to 11), so Android is not filtering them. Third-party notes (LibrePods `BLEManager.kt`) say the case value is a nibble of that message and that newer firmware encrypts its last 16 bytes with a key from the accessory channel; the project's own earlier design (`2026-09-28-airpods-compatibility-and-finding-design.md` 6.6, 8) allows only bounded scanning for the bonded identity. The page exists only while the pair is connected, and then the case value comes through the accessory channel or is not known; a scan would add a dangerous permission, two stored secrets (key and IRK) and a parser for untrusted radio input from every Apple device in range, for no value on the page. | Closes the Plan 7B item. Not measured: the layout of type `0x07` itself (it only appears while the pair is not connected). What the iPhone does is not documented by Apple and was not guessed. The case location (Find My) is Apple's network and is not possible here. | A pair that sends type `0x07` while connected; an owner request for a case value while the pair is disconnected (then a new design, review and ADR first). |
| ADR-129 | 2026-10-04 | Plan 7B: the own AirPods pages are one unexported activity `AllSettingsActivity` built from the small SettingsLib modules (`SettingsLibSettingsTheme`, `SettingsLibCollapsingToolbarBaseActivity`, `SettingsLibPreference`, `SettingsLibSegmentedButtonPreference`, `SettingsLibSliderPreference`, `SettingsLibFooterPreference`), opened from the "All AirPods settings" row of Settings' device page; it holds Listening mode with the Adaptive strength slider (three positions 0, 50, 100), Comfort with a continuous lowered-volume slider (10 to 90 percent, through patch `0011`), Press and hold (a cycle of at least two listening modes, frame `1A <mask>`), Name (frame `1A 01 <len> 00 <utf8>`, validated text, 1 to 32 bytes, the phone's alias follows) and About (model number and firmware from the device information frame `0x001D`, serial numbers skipped at parse time). Equalizer and Find (Last seen, Play sound) leave the page. Patch `0011` widens `setAirPodsMusicDuck` from four constants to an integer from 10 to 90. A connection sheet is shown once per connection from the `ACL_CONNECTED` broadcast through a full-screen-intent notification (permissions `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`, normal, and `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, runtime), only with the owner's yes recorded in Task 7. | Proposed (2026-10-04: `AIR-165`, `AIR-167`, `AIR-173`, `AIR-174` and `AIR-176` Pass on image `2026100211`; `AIR-166` Fail (conformance, 15 violations on `2026100211`, 17 on `2026100214`); `AIR-170` Pass for the alias on `2026100214` (patch `0012`) and `AIR-171` partial (the model number reads `A3048`); `AIR-168` and `AIR-175` Pass and `AIR-172` Pass for an unlocked phone on `2026100214`; `AIR-177` partial. Accepted only when every row has device evidence) | Owner decisions 2026-10-04 (chat): scope "everything with a known protocol", continuous volume slider through patch `0011`, case value closed (ADR-128). Evidence: spike (module build exit 0, theme resolved, SettingsLib classes in the APK); captures `AIR-161` to `AIR-164` (device information field order and anchor, rename accepted, press and hold mask proven by the owner's stem test, adaptive strength rounds to 0, 50 or 100 and is readable only after a new session). Alternatives rejected: a Compose page (does not match Settings by construction), a scan for the case (ADR-128), `START_ACTIVITIES_FROM_BACKGROUND` for the sheet (privileged and broad), an Equalizer or Find row without a protocol (a control that does not work). | New radio input: the device information strings (bounded, anchored on the vendor field, charset-checked, serials never kept). New frames sent: `2E <0..100>`, `1A <mask>` (at least two modes), rename (validated text). The press and hold state is not reported by this pair, so the page shows the last value written (stored app-private, no backup) or nothing chosen. The adaptive strength page value is the last value written until a new session reads the real one. Name limit 32 bytes is a conservative choice, not measured. The page needs the SettingsLib modules at every rebase of GrapheneOS. | A rebase that changes the SettingsLib modules or `Theme.SubSettingsBase.Expressive`; a pair whose device information has another layout (then the About rows show nothing and the frame stays unrecognised); an owner request for Equalizer or Find (a new design). |

Tier 2 is what the LibrePods protocol notes document: charging case sounds, headphone accommodation equalizer, transparency customisation, sleep detection, press timing and swipes, chime volume. Patches of `sovereignos-platform` `launcher/app-drawer/patches/packages-apps-launcher3/`: `0001` themed icons in the drawer, `0002` grouping logic and its unit test, `0003` group items, container view, opened card and adapter wiring, `0004` fixes after the first device round (space above each container, an app starting from its icon in the open card, the stock folder icon size in the card, the grip), `0005` a group rebinding on every update so its icons follow the theme, `0007` a group loading a missing themed icon in the background, so an app without a monochrome icon follows the theme too, `0008` the opened group readable in the dark theme and with a large font, `0009` the icon cells of the opened group sized by their label (a regression of `0008`'s first form, found on the device), `0010` the fixes of the code review before the next image (accessibility counts, the opened card's pre-draw race and touches during its animation, no category read for a system app). `0006` is named by ADR-142. | Proposed (patches `0011` and `0012` are in ADR-155) | Owner request 2026-10-06 (chat): the drawer ignores the owner's icon theme and is one flat list; the owner judged the result on `lynx` good on 2026-10-07 (chat; evidence `DRW-001` to `DRW-015`). The images on tag `2026100600` set it to `true` with `mmd.zram.writeback.max_bytes_per_day=25769803776` (24 GiB a day) [measured: `build.prop` of `lynx` `2026110204`], so there the daily cap bounds the writes, not the file. There is no unpatched image to show that the callback fires there; that is upstream behaviour read in source, not a run. | An app or a compatibility report that needs the callback; upstream adds another way to detect a capture; the patch stops applying after an upstream rebase. |

Measured on `lynx` (`AIR-200`): before the fix the AAP stream ended 4 ms after the ACL timeout, the app logged `actions [ROUTE_BACK]`, the Bluetooth stack made the pods the active A2DP device while A2DP was already disconnecting, `ACTION_AUDIO_BECOMING_NOISY` was broadcast and the player paused 0.9 s after the stream ended. After the fix the same sequence (pause, route to the phone, play on the phone speaker, disconnect) logged `ROUTE_BACK_IF_LINKED`, no active-device change and no noisy broadcast, and the music kept playing. Verified in source (`BootAnimation.cpp`, `drawTexturedQuad`): y is mapped with `mapLinear(yStart, 0, height, -1, 1)`, GL's y-up convention, so a texture sits on the bottom edge of the mask; the 2160-row shine therefore showed its row 1912 in the window and its track was under the opaque bottom of the mask. | ADR-157 | 2026-10-11 | The first version of the Live Data Map is accepted as designed in `2026-10-11-live-data-map-design.md` (visual reference: `live-data-map-2026-10-11.html`): an observe-only globe on which every app and every company behind it stands at its base, with a line from an app to each company it talks to, dashed lines between companies that are documented as connected, flags for the countries, and four tabs (Map, Apps, Companies, Links). (1) The interface is a new privileged system app `DataMapApp` that declares no network permission, service, receiver or provider, holds `READ_DATA_MAP` and `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` (icons and labels only), and shows each app's own icon from the system; no logo file of any app is shipped. (2) A collector in `system_server` takes the DNS and TCP connect events through a fourth `INetdEventCallback` slot (`CALLBACK_CALLER_DATA_MAP = 3`) and keeps aggregates per user, app and service; they stay in memory unless the user turns a history on, and the user chooses its length (1 to 90 days). (3) Every place on the map is a company or publisher base from a read-only classifier file, each with a source id; no address is looked up and no GeoIP database is used; a service with no known owner or place is listed, not placed. (4) A connection between companies is shown only with a source id, and is worded neutrally. (5) Bytes per destination, blocking and a separate identity per app are not in this version. Verified in source (build server, `frameworks/base`, branch `sovereignos-2026091900-r2`): `onDnsEvent` carries the host name, up to 10 addresses and the uid, and `onConnectEvent` the address, port and uid but not UDP (`system/netd/server/FwmarkServer.cpp`); neither carries bytes; the resolver keeps a set of listeners and registering needs `NETWORK_STACK`; `NetdEventListenerService` forwards each event to one `INetdEventCallback` per caller type and the three slots are taken (the intrusion detection `NetworkLogSource` uses the device-policy slot). The design avoids the `NETWORK_STACK` permission by running in `system_server`. Own privileged apps with their own `privapp-permissions` file already exist (`GuardianApp`, `AirPodsApp`). | The map shows only what the system's name and connect events reveal: an app that looks names up itself, a fixed address over UDP and QUIC without a lookup of its own are blind spots, shown as such. Every change of the app is an image build (a system app update needs the fs-verity signature). `DataMapApp.apk` and its privapp file join the `REQUIRED` list of `tools/build/check-required-packages.sh` with the implementation. | ADR-158 | 2026-10-12 | The classifier of the Live Data Map, and the choices that complete ADR-157 for the first image. (1) The classifier file is built from three layers: the AdGuard companies database (CC-BY-SA-4.0, built on whotracks.me, CC-BY-4.0) for tracker domains, companies and categories; Wikidata (CC0-1.0) for where a company is based, found by its official website; and facts read in company filings and documentation, each with a source record, for the owners, services and relations that were checked by hand, which win over the database. The file therefore carries CC-BY-SA-4.0 and an attribution notice. The tracker lists of Disconnect, DuckDuckGo Tracker Radar and Ghostery TrackerDB (CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0) are not used. (2) A company without one clear base gets no owner row; its services are listed with their category and an unknown owner, not placed. (3) The app finds the service by hidden-API reflection with a sysconfig entry for its one package, as `AirPodsApp` does (ADR-118); SELinux lets only `datamap_app` find the service, and `system_app` and `traceur_app` are excluded from it. `READ_DATA_MAP` is `@hide`. (4) The tab bar is blurred by recording the screen behind it into a render node with a blur effect, redrawn only when what is behind it changes; its cost is measured in `MAP-018` and the blur is dropped if the cost is not acceptable. (5) The sheet over the map (half and full height) is part of the first image. (6) Flags are the emoji of the system font; no flag artwork is shipped. (7) The relations shown as current are `owns` and `merged_with` (true once done) and `partner_of` and `buys_ad_space_from` only with a source that shows the arrangement is current. Supersedes: nothing; completes ADR-157 points (1) and (3). | Accepted | Owner decisions 2026-10-12 (chat): the classifier sources (first a hand-made list from primary sources, then the AdGuard repository and more locations), the Binder route as in `AirPodsApp`, the blur built now, the sheet required, and no further subagents. Evidence: the licence statements read in each source on 2026-10-12; the importer and builder checks with 11 of 11 mutations killed; the device parser accepting the built file (3698 services, 310 owners, 7 relations, 231 KB); three module builds on `lynx` of which the third passed with the policy `neverallow` checks and the 13 service classes confirmed in the output ([`datamap-engineering-notes.md`](../architecture/datamap-engineering-notes.md)). | The share-alike licence covers the classifier file, not the Apache-2.0 code around it. About 31 percent of the services have an owner with a place (Wikidata has a headquarters for about 15 percent of the companies of the database); the others are shown with a category and no owner, which the app says. Criteo is placed in Luxembourg after its 2026 redomiciliation and plans to move to the US in January 2027. The classifier changes only with an image or an OTA. The permission does not separate the app from apps of the system uid, so SELinux is the only gate. | Reopened if the coverage of places is too low to be useful (a company register as a further source needs its own entry), if `MAP-018` shows the blur costs too much, or when the Criteo base changes in January 2027. |

| ADR-164 | 2026-10-13 | The whole-branch review of the server places (ADR-161) corrects four of its statements and replaces its rule for networks that steer by DNS. (1) What is kept for a service is the kind, country, city, coordinates rounded to 0.01 degree, AS number and operator name of the server, never the address; this replaces "only country and ASN are kept" in ADR-161 (2). (2) The lookup runs on the service thread of the Data Map service, only for a service that has no place to draw yet; the callback of netd only hands an address on; this replaces "in the collector at the event" in ADR-161 (2). (3) A network is treated as steered by DNS when its operator name holds, as whole words in any case, one of `amazon`, `akamai`, `microsoft`, `google`, `cloudflare`, `fastly`, `facebook`, `meta platforms`, `apple`, `edgecast`, `edgio`, `limelight`, `cdn77`, `stackpath`, `imperva`, `incapsula`; this replaces the list "Amazon, Akamai, Microsoft" of ADR-161 (3), and `tools/audit-steered.py` prints the matches so that the list is read against the inputs before a file is shipped. (4) A server place is never merged with another and is named after its own city, or its country when the data has no city; a service has one answer, the best of what its entries said (a place that can be drawn over one that cannot, the first at equal rank). (5) A city is drawn when the city data and the country data name the same country or the country data names none; a country alone is drawn at its anchor; where they name two countries there is no place. Accepted when a `lynx` image on the tag has booted and the ADR-096-class checks pass on the device, and the `stallion` candidate on the tag is signed and installed. | Three releases (`2026092500`, `2026100200`, `2026100600`) are out: security patch level 2026-10-05, Pixel drivers and firmware on Android 17 QPR1, and a kernel that restores the Android 17 mmap read-ahead behaviour that stalls under memory pressure. Evidence of 2026-10-14: the tag signature is good (`contact@grapheneos.org`, the allowed-signers hash of ADR-008 unchanged); 42 of 1057 projects change revision, 6 of the 12 patched projects among them; a whole-branch `git merge-tree` of each old branch gave no conflict except `vendor/adevtool` `device-common.mk`, where upstream and every package append at the end of the file (both kept, upstream first); the exported packages applied with plain `git am` on the new revisions give the trees of the new build-server branches in all six changed projects (`frameworks/base` `a759681c0630`, Settings `fa553172abd8`, Bluetooth `790eda61b13a`, `build/release` `89ad23af4ccd`, `system/core` `4db206bc1c76`, `vendor/adevtool` `f2cef3527ae1`). `adevtool generate-all` ran for `lynx` and `stallion` and showed that `memory/zram-size` and `memory/cached-process-cap` had only dropped the stock values from the inclusion lists, which adevtool treats as unknown entries and includes: the regenerated modules carried `mmd.zram.size=50%` and `config_customizedMaxCachedProcesses=1024` again. Both are now on exclusion lists (`zram-size/0002`, `cached-process-cap/0002`); against the unmodified regeneration the only differences are those two entries (`MEM-018`). `lynx-cur-userdebug` `2026101404` built (38111 steps, exit 0, package gate passed; one `mmd.zram.size=100%`, cap 48 in `framework-res`, none in the vendor overlay, `MEM-019`) and the `stallion` `user` candidate `2026101405` the same way (39046 steps, exit 0, package gate passed, cap 48, one `mmd.zram.size=100%`, no debug harness). Details: `sovereignos-manifest/provenance/2026100600.md`. | The vendor modules are regenerated with `--noVerify` because they differ from upstream's FileTreeSpec by design; adevtool then writes `$(warning` into the pin file, which was put back to `$(error` by hand. The `lynx` flashset requires bootloader `lynx-17.0-15902555` and baseband `g5300q-260604-260807-B-16036236`. The `adevtool` caches (about 32 GB) return with every `generate-all`; `-k` keeps the unpacked images of the named devices only. | ADR-167 | 2026-10-14 | Harden the identity surface of apps as designed in `2026-10-14-identity-surface-hardening-design.md`. (1) Channel A: the `ro.boot.*` properties that identify a unit or a production batch stop being readable by `appdomain`; no allow rule is added for apps, and the list is fixed only after the probe has run on `stallion`. (2) Channel C: for a non-system uid the bias estimates of the uncalibrated accelerometer, gyroscope and magnetometer are folded into the readings and delivered as zero; direct sensor channels for these three types are refused to non-system uids if the plan's probe shows that they carry the bias. (3) Channel B (boot id, boot time) stays a documented residual. (4) The model of section 11 is adopted: a per-app identity mode (Hardened by default, or Real) and, per permission class an app demands, Real, Decoy or Deny, with one policy store in `system_server`; channel A stays global because SELinux domains are fixed when the image is built. (5) Order: channels A and C global, then the policy store and its settings screen, then the sensor decoy, then the location decoy; each is its own plan. (6) A separate network exit per app is not decided and not designed (D3 of the design). | Accepted (owner, 2026-10-14, all recommendations of the design). | A probe in two apps with different signing keys on `lynx` (`userdebug`) found that an app without a dangerous permission reads, equally in both apps, unit properties of `ro.boot.*` that `private/domain.te:519` allows every domain, the bias estimates of three uncalibrated sensors, and the boot identity; `ANDROID_ID` and the DRM device identifier already differ per app; `uncalibrated - bias` equals the calibrated stream within the noise of two 5-second windows (`lane-feasibility.md`). | The claim is limited to one device, one `userdebug` image and `targetSdk` 36: equality across apps is measured, uniqueness across devices is not, persistence of the bias over a reboot is not, and the residual error of calibrated sensor streams is not changed. Two patches in the package `privacy/identity-surface` of `sovereignos-platform` (`system-sepolicy`, `frameworks-native`), authored by the worktree rule of `AGENTS.md`. `capability-boundaries.md` and `threat-model.md` get a row each in the same change. The probe settled consequence (2) on 2026-10-14: on the baseline image an ordinary app configures a direct sensor channel (type `HardwareBuffer`) for all three types, so the refusal is part of the change; that the bias is written into such a buffer is not read (Java cannot read it) and stays an assumption. A fresh review of the patches on 2026-10-14, checked in source, narrowed two points: the read rule also excludes `app_zygote` and `native_app_zygote` (app code runs there and they are not `appdomain`), and the sensor fold and the direct-channel refusal apply to app uids (`uid % AID_USER_OFFSET >= AID_APP_START`) instead of every uid but `AID_SYSTEM`, so native daemons and platform uids keep the unchanged events. | A property on the list turns out to be read by a platform component; the probe shows the bias on a `user` build or on `stallion` after the change; a new upstream release changes `sendEvents`. |
| ADR-168 | 2026-10-14 | Accept a second narrow exception to the anti-falsification rule of the product compass, beside ADR-037: an owner-initiated, per-app, opt-in decoy for the sensors an app reads (accelerometer, gyroscope, magnetometer, pressure, light and the like). The boundary of the Location Anchor design against faking motion sensors (2026-08-12) is restated: it holds for the system-wide Location Anchor, whose reasons (crash detection and the emergency-location flow) concern system consumers, and it does not apply to a per-app decoy that leaves the system uid alone. | Accepted (owner, 2026-10-14). | Apps that demand the sensors permission as a condition are the owner's stated problem; users report that zeroed data crashes apps, so a decoy must be plausible (`lane-feasibility.md`, section 3). Opt-in by the owner does not stop a third party's behavioural-biometric fraud check from being defeated, which the owner accepts knowingly, as for the location signal. | The product compass is amended in the same change (Daily-driver promise): two exceptions, each scoped to its own signal, neither a precedent for attestation, signatures, DRM, payment, subscriptions or identity checks. | ADR-169 | 2026-10-15 | Add grab detection as designed in `2026-10-15-grab-detection-design.md`. Phase 1 locks the phone with `STRONG_AUTH_REQUIRED_AFTER_USER_LOCKDOWN`, shows one line above the PIN entry and vibrates. Phase 2 is the existing GrapheneOS auto-reboot timer, armed through `sys.auto_reboot_ctl` for a fixed 60 seconds before the lock; a PIN unlock cancels it. The first build ships a shadow mode (log only) with thresholds tunable by `Settings.Global` keys; one on/off switch in Settings; identical behaviour in the decoy session; no exception during a call. BFU without a restart is parked (`docs/research/bfu-without-reboot-feasibility.md`). | Accepted (owner, 2026-10-15, after review of the written design). | An additional layer that reuses the lock and the restart path of upstream. `init` is not changed (the timer, its 20-second floor and its "ignore while running" rule are used as they are), no Binder interface, permission or network access is added, and the touch monitor keeps only a contact flag and a release time. `SecureLockDeviceService` was rejected: it is flag-gated, persists across reboot and needs two-factor to disable. | ADR-171 | 2026-10-15 | Define a source-only publication stage that precedes the binary public beta, as designed in `2026-10-10-source-and-site-public-beta-design.md`. (2) Licences and identity: Apache-2.0 for own code; each patch keeps the licence of the upstream file it changes and is never edited, so its hash and provenance stay intact; CC-BY-4.0 for documentation; the name and logo are not licensed. The copyright holder named in the `NOTICE` files is the owner, and the commit author address is accepted as public. `AGENTS.md` is replaced by `CONTRIBUTING.md` in the public repositories. Gates that apply only when binaries are offered stay as in `phase-roadmap.md`: firmware currency, signed production artifacts, relock, OTA and recovery evidence, compatibility, carrier and emergency evidence. Open: the site, the claim review, the legal review, an SBOM, the company logo files in the history of `freeholdos-platform` and the owner's read of the final set. The project operates and pays for no relay. | Proposed (owner direction 2026-10-18 after the Tor measurement: Direct default, Tor and own profile per app; not approved, nothing built; D-A to D-D open). | Per-app Tor exists in the Tor Project's own Tor VPN (beta, per-app circuits, Arti, Cure53 audit June 2025) `[web]`; no free, operator-free UDP exit was found `[web]`; per-uid selection without the VPN slot exists in the tree `[verified-in-source: ConnectivityService.java:16300-16367]`, but `setOemNetworkPreference` is automotive-only for production preferences (`:15953-15990`), so a one-line Connectivity patch is part of the decision. Rejected: Tor as the default for every app (measured 2026-10-18: 10 MB takes 6.6 to 89 times as long as Direct and a request starts 11 to 18 times later; the owner refused that regression); a relay run by the project as the default (the owner cannot pay for one, and an operator would see the traffic); a silent fall back to Direct (`requirements.md:501`). | Tor is slower than Direct and sites that block the public exit list fail on it, so it is a choice and not the default; apps that need UDP fail on Tor until switched to Direct; an own profile trusts the server or provider the user picked and needs the user to bring one; the packet layer and a user-space WireGuard are large new privileged components; version 1 has two exit classes (Tor and one profile), more need a per-app network patch; the review of 2026-10-18 found open problems (enforcement against an app that selects another network, DNS attribution, a VPN, boot and install windows) listed in the design, section 10a. | The Tor measurement is not the issue any more; the packet layer cannot be reused or built within reason; the selection fails on a device (`NET-001a`, `NET-001b`); enforcement against explicit network selection cannot be built; the owner decides that an exit that an app can leave is not worth shipping.

It does not claim invisibility (the rejection reasons of ADR-177 hold) or a stronger sandbox. No USPTO or EUIPO search was done `[assumption]`. | A bare common English word is a weak mark; a public name likely needs a suffix such as "FreeholdOS Compartments". The name must not be read as a sandbox or profile feature (Android already has user profiles and Private Space). | A clearance search or a domain check finds a conflict; the owner picks another name. |
| ADR-181 | 2026-10-19 | Amend ADR-178: add a default exit class, masked destinations, as designed in `2026-10-19-compartments-masked-destinations-design.md`. Flows to hosts of a mask list (the classifier categories advertising, analytics and attribution minus a deny list) leave through Tor; everything else stays Direct and on the kernel path. A host that fails or is slow masked is retried on a fresh circuit, then refused at once, then the user gets one notification with the choice to show the address to that host or keep hiding it; nothing goes Direct without that choice. The Tor client starts only after a one-time confirmation in Setup Wizard (or one notification on a device already set up). A two-hop relay replaces Tor for the list when an operator exists. The per-app Tor and own-profile classes of ADR-178 stay. | Proposed (owner direction 2026-10-19: no regression, handled failures, user choice by notification; the confirmation at setup is a recommendation the owner has not yet confirmed; not approved, nothing built; D-E to D-I open). | Joining of apps by address happens at shared third-party endpoints, which carry small requests and are a bounded list `[measured: classifier.tsv, 3367 domains in the three categories]`; Tor's late start (11 to 18 times) matters little for such requests but its failures on exit-blocking hosts do, hence the failure ladder and the user's choice. Apple's relay is paid and Google's covers a tracking-domain list only `[web]`; no free all-traffic path exists. Rejected: Tor for all traffic of every app (ADR-178); a relay operated by the project; a silent Direct fall back (`requirements.md:501`); fake responses for a failed host (the stream is encrypted); hosts of integrity, banking, payment and anti-fraud services on the list. | Apps see no change except hosts that need the user's choice; the app's own servers still see the real address (the per-app classes remain for that); routing per destination needs a DNS-driven address set or an SNI split that is not built or measured; the exit service learns which listed hosts each app contacts and keeps it in RAM only; an ECH or in-app DoH client escapes the list `[assumption]`. | `MSK-002` shows a regression that the failure ladder cannot remove; the routing mechanism cannot be built without slowing unlisted traffic (`MSK-006`); Arti on `lynx` is unusable; the owner decides that a list-only exit is not worth shipping. |
| ADR-182 | 2026-10-20 | `DeviceSettingServiceConnection.getService` in SettingsLib treats a `SecurityException` from `Context.bindService` as a failed bind (patch `0002` of `airpods/patches/frameworks-base`), so a caller that may not bind a device settings provider gets no setting from it instead of an uncaught exception. The AirPods provider services stay `exported="false"` and keep their caller check (`Callers.enforceSettings`: system uid and own process only). | Proposed (landed and type-checked, not built, not run on a device; `AIR-202`). | Owner report 2026-10-20 (`stallion`): with AirPods connected the volume slider's sound panel crashed SystemUI or hid the slider, and the sound settings button under the slider hung the phone until a reboot. The path is `[verified-in-source]`: SystemUI's volume panel reads the setting `DEVICE_SETTING_ID_ANC` (1001) of the current audio device through the same SettingsLib repository as Settings, finds the AirPods provider through metadata key 25, and binds it as uid 10150 `[measured on lynx: dumpsys package]`; Settings is uid 1000. ActivityManager answers "not exported from uid" (`ActiveServices.retrieveServiceLocked`), `ContextImpl.bindServiceCommon` throws `SecurityException("Not allowed to bind to service")`, and `getService` does not catch it. That the exception ends the SystemUI process, and that the panel then crashes again after a restart, is `[assumption]` until a `logcat -b crash` of `stallion` shows it. Rejected: exporting the services with a permission and letting SystemUI call them (a new IPC endpoint on a privileged path, `Callers.enforceSettings` would have to admit SystemUI, and the toggle icons are blank bitmaps drawn only by Settings' own button); a catch inside SystemUI (a larger upstream divergence than one guard in the shared repository). | The volume panel shows no AirPods listening-mode button (it never had a designed one); Settings is unchanged because its bind succeeds. Every device whose provider cannot be bound by SystemUI is now harmless. One robolectric test was written and is not run. | A `stallion` crash log shows another cause; the owner wants the listening mode in the volume panel (then the exported variant, with real icons and a reviewed caller check). |

| ADR-184 | 2026-10-20 | Amend ADR-178, and with it the masked-destinations class of ADR-181: the exit service is two components, as set out in section 2a of `2026-10-18-network-exit-design.md`. (1) The exit daemon is a native Rust program in its own SELinux domain, started by `init` with the one capability `CAP_NET_ADMIN`. It creates the tun interface, runs the Tor client and the packet layer (the library crate `onion-tunnel` of Onionmasq with Arti, pinned by `Cargo.lock`), looks up the owner uid of every new flow with `sock_diag`, and holds no framework permission. (2) The exit app is a platform-signed app that holds `NETWORK_FACTORY` and `CONTROL_OEM_PAID_NETWORK_PREFERENCE`. It registers the network for the interface the daemon reports, sets the `OemNetworkPreferences`, posts the notifications and hosts the Settings rows. It holds no capability and not `NETWORK_STACK`. The daemon's own uid stays outside every preference, so its sockets use the normal network and the daemon sets no socket mark. A flow that the daemon cannot attribute to a uid is refused, not given a shared key. The service does not run in `system_server`, is not a `VpnService` and is not one app. The daemon is built with `panic = abort` and restarted by `init`, and the memory quota of Arti is set explicitly. The SELinux domain grants the tun device with `TUNSETIFF` and `TUNGETIFF`, the `sock_diag` netlink socket, the sockets that Arti needs and the control channel, and no routing-table write, no netfilter and no other Binder service. The control channel carries typed messages only and is authenticated by SELinux domain and uid. | Accepted (owner agreed to the two-component split 2026-10-20). An amendment of ADR-178, which stays `Proposed`, itself amended by ADR-186 (a separate route helper, the DNS mechanism for masked destinations, the control sockets from `init`); the daemon core and a userdebug probe app are built and host-tested (milestones 1 and 2 of the plan, none of it on an image); the SELinux domain, the `init` service, the exit app and the control socket model are not built or reviewed; D-K and D-L of the design are open. | Permissions and the tun `[verified-in-source: ConnectivityService.java in packages/modules/Connectivity, AndroidManifest.xml in frameworks/base, system/sepolicy/private, all on the build server, 2026-10-20]`: registering a network or an offer needs `NETWORK_FACTORY`, `setOemNetworkPreference` needs `CONTROL_OEM_PAID_NETWORK_PREFERENCE`, and the owner-uid call needs `NETWORK_STACK`, all at signature level. Only `system_server`, `netd`, `bluetooth`, `clatd` and `crosvm` may create a tun; an app may only use a descriptor that the framework hands it. The `sock_diag` rules belong to `system_server`, `network_stack` and `netd`. An app process has no `CAP_NET_ADMIN` `[assumption: general Android behaviour, not read for this image]`. The split puts kernel reach (tun, `sock_diag`, `CAP_NET_ADMIN`) in a small memory-safe daemon and framework authority (network registration, the preference) in a Java app without capability, so that no component holds both. The Onionmasq library `[verified-in-source: crates/onion-tunnel at commit 849a8d14d165]` takes a tun descriptor and has no Android dependency; its `TunnelScaffolding` trait lets the daemon choose the isolation key; `oniux` runs it on a plain Linux tun `[web]`. The upstream Linux scaffolding sets `SO_MARK` 0xc185 on Arti's sockets (`linux.rs:49`) and finds the owner by scanning `/proc/net/tcp` and `/proc/net/udp` for every new flow (`linux.rs:91-128`); Android reads the low 16 bits of a socket mark as a network id `[assumption: not tested with a uid other than 0; the bootstrap on `lynx` ran as uid 0, which has its own routing rules]`, so the daemon does not use that scaffolding as it is: it sets no mark and uses `sock_diag`. The owner of a plain DNS query is the app: the DNS sockets of Reddit and Instagram showed the app uid in `/proc/net/udp` and, for Reddit, in `ss` (`sock_diag`) `[measured]`; DNS-over-TLS is tagged `AID_DNS` in the source and is not measured. Cost `[measured: NET-006, lynx 2026101802]`: about 50 MB resident after bootstrap, 62 to 69 MB at the peak of a start or a consensus refresh, about 0.2 MB for each further isolation key (53.0 MB with twenty), 53.6 MB after an hour with eight keys and no leak seen in that hour; the default quota of Arti is 5.46 GiB, so a cap is needed. The tree builds it `[measured]`: AOSP `rustc` 1.93.1 (the default), a throwaway sysroot, `panic = abort` because the Android standard library of the tree has no unwind strategy, and two `cfg` gates widened to Android (2 files, 16 lines). Rejected: one platform app with the Rust code as a library (an app cannot create the tun and has no capability); the service inside `system_server` (a crash or a bug in Arti or the packet layer would take the system down, and its heap is shared); a `VpnService` (it uses the VPN slot, ADR-178); giving the app `NETWORK_STACK` to look up uids over Binder (a wider permission than the daemon's own `sock_diag` use, and a Binder call for every new flow); a daemon that also registers the network (the registration API is Java). | Two components and a control channel instead of one. A new SELinux domain and an `init` service with `CAP_NET_ADMIN` are a privileged path that gets the higher bar of AGENTS.md: the rows added to [`capability-boundaries.md`](../security/capability-boundaries.md) and [`threat-model.md`](../security/threat-model.md) with this entry are `Proposed`, and a whole-branch review and a `neverallow` review come before any image. The daemon brings a dependency tree of 552 packages `[measured: exit-daemon/Cargo.lock; upstream Onionmasq's lock has 613]` that includes `aws-lc-sys` (C and assembly, a second copy of BoringSSL-derived code), so the project's preference for memory-safe code does not hold for the whole tree (D-L). A panic ends the daemon, `init` restarts it, and apps set to Tor have no network meanwhile, as ADR-178 says for a down exit. The daemon's own traffic to the Tor network is Direct, so a network operator sees that the device uses Tor; the consent of ADR-181 section 5 stays. About 11 percent of 944 requests failed in the one-hour run for a reason not yet found `[measured: NET-006]`, which touches the requirement of no regression. Whether `netd` configures the interface from the link properties that the app registers, or the daemon must bring the link up and set its address, is untested `[assumption]` (`NET-001b`). Soong needs `smoltcp` and the Arti crates added to the tree (none is in `external/rust/android-crates-io`), or a locked and vendored Cargo build; the plan chooses. The two upstream `cfg` changes are kept as a documented patch in the package of the daemon. | `init` cannot start the daemon with only `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, or its domain cannot get `TUNSETIFF` or `sock_diag` without weakening a `neverallow`; a daemon with a uid other than 0 cannot bootstrap or reach the Tor network (a socket mark or a network rule is in the way); `NET-001b` fails on an image; the resident set on `stallion` exceeds the budget the owner sets (D-K) or a run of many hours shows growth (`NET-006`); the owner refuses the C crypto code (D-L); the failed requests of `NET-006` turn out to come from the daemon; `netd` needs more from the daemon than this entry allows. |

(2) Key hierarchy: a random 256-bit vault key in one sealed file; a passphrase wrapper from Argon2id mixed with a non-exportable hardware secret in the Keystore; a biometric wrapper that needs a fresh strong-biometric authentication for every use, with no screen-lock fallback; the passphrase is required after a restart, a biometric enrollment change, five failed biometric attempts and 7 days; the vault locks after one minute in the background and when the device locks; no recovery and no master key. (3) While the vault is locked the keyboard chip appears only on sites or apps with an account, found through an HMAC index of exact registrable domains and package names under a separate Keystore key, with the residual leaks of section 4.1 of the design accepted and no index padding. (4) The app is Jetpack Compose with Material 3 and the cryptographic core is Rust with vendored RustCrypto crates, gated by the feasibility task of the plan. (5) Logos come from the installed app icon, a bundled icon pack and a generated pattern as fallback; nothing is fetched. (6) Version 1 holds passwords only, each with a notes field. The inline chips in the keyboard strip need inline-suggestion support in LatinIME and are a second plan. | Accepted | The owner decided the points above on 2026-10-20 or left them to the design (chip rules, Checkup scope, the detail items, toolkit and core language). Prior art: the requirements ask for hardware-backed keys, per-world vaults and biometric convenience with a passphrase fallback and have no key design; the tree has the autofill and credential provider services, a combined Settings picker and two opt-ins for inline suggestions, and `GuardianApp` already builds Compose and Material 3. | The plan `2026-10-20-password-manager.md` implements it; the app lives in `freeholdos-platform/passwords/` and the core in `freeholdos-services/vault-core/`. Downloads of the vendored crates, builds and device tests need separate owner approval. | The feasibility gate (Task 1) shows that the vendored crypto does not build with the AOSP Rust; `lynx` or `stallion` lacks strong biometrics or hardware-backed keys; Keystore keys turn out to expire on an update; the autofill framework lets the requesting app see whether a chip was offered; the trademark review refuses the icon set; the dropdown fallback proves unacceptable and the chips cannot be built. |
| ADR-186 | 2026-10-21 | Close out Compartments with one plan (`2026-10-21-compartments-close-out.md`) and amend ADR-184 and ADR-181. (1) Scope of v1: per-app Direct or Tor, plus masked destinations as the default tier after one consent. Not in v1: an own WireGuard profile, a two-hop relay (D-I), bridges (D-A), UDP over Tor, interception of DNS-over-HTTPS or of strict Private DNS, a Setup Wizard step (the consent of ADR-181 section 5 is asked by one notification at the first unlock, for every user), the `stallion` image and signing round. (2) Three components instead of two: the data plane `exitd` (tun, Arti, `sock_diag`, DNS split, no routing right), a route helper (`exitd --routes`, a second `init` service of the same binary in its own SELinux domain, with `NETLINK_ROUTE` rights only, no internet socket, no Tor, accepting only a closed set of rule shapes inside a fixed priority band and one table), and the platform-signed exit app, which owns the policy: it registers the Tor network, sets the preference, sends the route specification to the helper and the uid classes (Tor, Masked, Exempt) and per-company overrides to the daemon. The daemon therefore still cannot change routing, as ADR-184 and the threat model state; a compromised exit app can ask the helper only for the closed shapes. (3) Masked destinations work by DNS, replacing the per-address set of design section 4.2 option 1: rules steer the port-53 queries of masked uids and a fake address range (`198.18.0.0/15` and `fd66:7265:6568::/48`, not the `10.0.0.0/8` that Onionmasq uses, which collides with LANs) into the tun; the daemon answers a listed name with a per-app fake address that it carries through Tor by name and forwards every other query verbatim to the resolver the app used; flows to a fake address are accepted, any other flow of a masked uid is reset, QUIC to a fake address is refused with ICMP port unreachable (D-J). (4) Rule band `13100` to `13999` and table `4747`: after the secure-VPN rule (13000), so an always-on VPN wins (F04), and before the explicit-network rules (15000, 16000) that the resolver's DNS sockets match. A guard in the band (goto and prohibit rules) stops a uid set to Tor from selecting another network by id (F01). (5) The control sockets are created by `init` and authenticated by the peer SELinux context of the exit app, not by a uid, because a platform app gets its uid at install. (6) Owner decisions: D-K quota 64 MiB for Arti and a hard limit of 160 MiB in the service definition; D-L `aws-lc-sys` accepted as a known risk (own domain, no framework right, no routing right); D-E first scope `advertising`, `analytics`, `attribution` of the classifier minus a hand-checked deny list; D-F the unit of the user's choice is the company; D-G notify after 3 failures or 5 s of slowness within 10 minutes; D-H consent once, preselected on, by notification (changed from Setup Wizard plus notification, because the wizard was never read); D-J refuse listed QUIC. | Proposed (owner chose the scope, D-K, D-L, the route helper and to have the other choices filled in with the recommendations, 2026-10-21). Nothing of it is built. The steering mechanism was tested on a phone before any code depended on it (`MSK-008`, `NET-008`, 2026-10-21): steering of an app's DNS and of a fake address range works, the F01 guard works in the kernel, and the fall-through when the daemon is gone is as stated below; the gate of Task 2 is passed. | Route rights and netd priorities `[verified-in-source: system/netd/server/RouteController.h:34-82 and system/sepolicy/private/dhcp.te, app_neverallows.te on the build server, 2026-10-21]`: netd uses rule priorities 10000 to 32000, `dhcp` is allowed `nlmsg_write` on its own route socket with the same pattern, and no `neverallow` blocks a new domain from it. The resolver marks its DNS sockets with the app's network id and the explicit-select bit, and their owner is the app uid `[verified-in-source: NetworkController.cpp:237-266 and the F03 measurement of ADR-184]`. Onionmasq already dispenses fake addresses per isolation key and maps them to host names `[verified-in-source: exit-daemon/third_party/onionmasq/crates/onion-tunnel/src/dns.rs:46-217]`. The owner's phone runs an always-on VPN with lockdown on `stallion` `[measured: project notes 2026-09-29]`, which is why the band must lie behind the VPN rules. | One more `init` service and SELinux domain (about 300 lines and 1 to 2 MB), and a closed rule language to review. | ADR-187 | 2026-10-22 | Implement Compartments v1 with four choices that amend ADR-186: (1) the Tor client is created when the exit daemon starts and bootstrapped only when the exit app's class table first holds a Tor or masked class, and the app asks the daemon to exit when nothing needs it any more, instead of starting the daemon on demand; (2) the consent of the masked tier is one per device, given by the owner, and the exit app runs in the owner user only (its copies in other users idle), instead of one consent per user at its first unlock; (3) the control sockets of the daemon and the route helper are created by `init` with mode 0666, protected by the SELinux policy (only `exit_app` may connect) and by the services' check of the peer's SELinux context before the first byte; (4) the neverallow rules of the exit domains are checked by `check-neverallows.sh` of `freeholdos-platform` (`privacy/exit/tools`), because the Pixel board configurations set `SELINUX_IGNORE_NEVERALLOWS` and the build checks none. | Proposed | (1) An app cannot set a system property (`SystemProperties.set` is not in the SDK stubs), so it cannot start an `init` service on demand; a daemon that waits for its first class gives the same guarantee, that no Tor traffic leaves before the user asked, and `MSK-005` checks it on a device. (2) One controller and one peer for the daemon; a second user's controller would take the connection. The decoy user must not show the owner's choices. (3) The exit app has its own uid, which no group of the services holds; the discretionary mode cannot name it. | ADR-188 | 2026-10-22 | Protect what an app waits for from the masked tier, at run time, and give every app a way out. (1) A third stand per app, "Direct, never hidden": the app's uids join the Exempt class, so its DNS is passed on and nothing it connects to is masked; the user sets it for a browser or a video app that the tier slows down. (2) The speed guard in `exitd`: the daemon counts the bytes that a masked company sends to an app over Tor; 4 MiB or more from one company to one app within 60 s makes the daemon pass that company's names on to that app for 30 minutes (RAM only; the same effect as the user's "Show my address", applied from the next DNS answer), reports it to the exit app in the state reply, and the app notifies once per promotion and lists it in Settings with "Hide again", which releases it and keeps the company hidden for that app until the daemon restarts. The signal is the amount of data, not time: an idle keep-alive connection of a tracker stays open for minutes, and the first connection after a cold circuit takes more than 3 s, so either would unmask nearly every company; slowness to open stays with the ladder of ADR-186, which asks the user. (3) The deny list is no longer the only protection and grows from evidence: `online-metrix.net` (device fingerprinting for banks) and `clickbank.net` (checkout) now; the hosts that the owner's own apps contact, from the Data Map, once the tier runs on `stallion`. (4) The warning for Private DNS says that hosts may still see the address (the tier does not mask where DNS-over-TLS works), and the consent text names attribution and crash reporting. | Proposed (owner approved the guard and its notification, 2026-10-22) | Tor is 7 to 89 times slower for 10 MB and 11 to 18 times slower to start a request `[measured: NET-005]`, so a masked host that serves content, not a beacon, is a regression the user sees; the classifier files whole domains and content delivery hosts under advertising (`hwcdn.net`, `phncdn.com`, `line-scdn.net` are masked rows `[measured: exit-mask-list.tsv, 2026-10-22]`), and a name filter finds nothing (406 of 3062 masked names contain a content-like word, 118 of them "media" of ad companies `[measured: same file]`). Other breakage lists are per site and for browsers, and the lists of DuckDuckGo, Disconnect and Ghostery are non-commercial (ADR-158). The library already counts bytes per app in its copier `[verified-in-source: exit-daemon/third_party/onionmasq/crates/onion-tunnel/src/proxy.rs InteractiveCopier, BandwidthCounter]`; it lacks the company of the flow, which patch 0006 adds. | A host can unmask itself by sending 4 MiB to the app: a tracker that knows the tier could do it on purpose. The effect is bounded (30 minutes, one company, one app, RAM), visible (notification and Settings) and the same as the user's own choice; the guard is a usability protection, not an anonymity claim, and the threat model says so. The first 4 MiB of a heavy flow have gone through Tor and a long single connection is not moved. | The device rounds show that real pages or video still lag beyond what the guard catches (then a lower threshold or a deny list from the sweep); the guard unmasks companies that the owner's traffic shows to be beacons only (then a higher threshold); a review finds a way for an app to force the promotion of a company for another app. |

(2) The file is written through the system document picker (`ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT`: a new file, named by the user or accepted with the suggested `atlas-services-<date>.tsv`) and the content URI that the picker returns; the app gets no storage permission, no `INTERNET`, no provider, receiver or share target, and no new component. (3) The file is plain text, tab-separated, one row per app and service of the snapshot: package, service key (the classifier suffix, a registrable domain or `ip:<address>`), company name, category, count, foreground, background, first and last seen (UTC, to the second), via DNS. It holds no host name below the service key, no bytes, no server place and no relation between companies. (4) The data is the snapshot of the calling user at the moment the picker returns; the app keeps nothing between the dialog and the write, writes at no other time, and has no setting that exports by itself. (5) Narrows ADR-157 and the Data Map design section 10 ("No export path exists") and the entry "writing files of its own, sharing or exporting any data" in the row "Data Map app" of `capability-boundaries.md`, for this one user-driven path only. | Proposed (owner request, 2026-10-22) | The review of the masked tier (`MSK-013`) needs the services that the owner's apps contact, and there is no other way to read them on a retail build: `stallion` runs a `user` build (`adb root` refused), the history is in credential-encrypted storage that the shell cannot read, the Binder API needs the signature permission `READ_DATA_MAP` and the service type is findable only by the app's domain, and `dumpsys datamap` prints counters only `[measured: stallion 2026101405, 2026-10-22]`. | ADR-195 | 2026-10-26 | Amend ADR-176: an impact after a short free fall is a set-down and does not lock. `GrabDetector` tracks the runs of accelerometer samples with a magnitude below 4 m/s² and, at an impact (deviation from gravity at or above `grab_impact_peak_dms2`), takes the longest run that ended within the 400 ms before it. No free fall, or one of at least 250 ms (a fall of about 30 cm), locks as before; a shorter one does not, and the rest of that pulse is not judged again for 250 ms (the pull rule of ADR-169 still sees it). The owner chooses the strictness in Settings (exploit protection page; the placement is amended by ADR-197), stored as `grab_impact_level`: 0 strictest (today's behaviour of ADR-176, every impact locks), 1 standard (default; the rule above), 2 relaxed (the rule above and twice the impact peak, for running). The log line of an impact names the free fall, the verdict and the level. | Proposed (owner request 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-26; patches `frameworks-base/0010` and `packages-apps-Settings/0002` host-tested, 59 of 59 mutants killed; landed on the build server 2026-10-26 as `23ee105cd833` and `d31f2300fb2`; `GRB-020` is `PARTIAL` and `GRB-021` partly run on `lynx` `2026102302` on 2026-10-27, `GRB-022` and `GRB-023` not run). | The owner called a lock after a set-down from about 3 cm unreasonable (`GRB-018`, 2026-10-25), asked for a setting from the strictest level to a relaxed one for running, and wants a real fall (a train) to lock. `[measured]` (`GRB-019`; the real detector replayed over the recordings at 50 Hz, the rate of `SENSOR_DELAY_GAME`, in five phases of the sample grid, 2026-10-26): 12 staged grabs have no free fall in any phase; 15 to 17 set-downs of 3 cm have one of 20 to 101 ms, 13 hard set-downs 20 to 159 ms, so at level 1 all 12 grabs lock and none of 28 to 30 set-downs does; at level 0 every one locks; at level 2 (60 m/s²) 4 to 6 of the 12 grabs lock through this route. The limit of 250 ms leaves 91 ms of margin above the longest set-down measured. Prior art: ADR-176 rejected a free-fall check because a lock after a drop was accepted; that reason falls with the owner's request, and a real fall still locks (a free fall of 250 ms or more). Not rejected here, left out: a minimum pulse width, which would stop the one 4 ms spike of a pickup that still locks at level 1, because a missed snatch is worse than a false lock and no short grab pulse is measured either way. | One more key (`grab_impact_level`, global, default 1), a free-fall run tracker in `GrabDetector` and a list in Settings; no new permission, Binder interface or sensor. The result stands on 12 grabs and 29 set-downs of one hand and one phone `[assumption]` for other hands, other surfaces and the real sensor rate. Level 2 for running is `[assumption]`: no run is measured and the peak factor of 2 is a guess. A fall from 50 cm to 1 m, a thief who lets go and walking, which should lock or not lock by the rule, are not measured. (1) `exitd` and `exitd_route` accept a control peer of the type `exit_app` only when its uid belongs to user 0 (below 100000) as well; a run with a uid (the tests, root on a phone) still compares the uid exactly and never reads the context.
