This supersedes the parts of ADR-197 (1) that say the shadow mode reads as Off and the consequence that a level outside 0 to 2 reads as Standard. | Accepted (owner instruction 2026-10-28: yes to the user check, do what is best for the row, ask for the PIN or fingerprint; landed on the build server as `vendor/freeholdos/exit` `4760de5` (`exitd` built from `freeholdos-services` `2b7d137`, twice with one hash) and `packages/apps/Settings` `290bc735639`; host tests and mutations pass; built into `lynx` image `2026102303` (`IMG-017`), not on a device) | `[verified-in-source]` (review B1): after `system-sepolicy/0004` the MLS constraints pass for an `exit_app` in any user, the daemon compared only the SELinux type (`context_has_type`, levels and categories are not compared) and a new connection of an accepted peer replaces the old one, so a copy of the exit app in the decoy or a work profile would have been accepted; the exit app itself runs only in user 0 (`ExitRuntime.runsHere`), which is app code and not a barrier. Android gives each user a block of 100000 uids. `[verified-in-source]` (review C1): `RadioButtonPickerFragment2.setDefaultKey` returns without writing when the chosen key equals the current one, so Off could not be chosen while the row showed Off for the shadow mode; the picker shows no selection and no summary for a current value that is not an entry. `[verified-in-source]` (review C2, C3): the service clamps the level to 0 to 2 when it arms, and `AutoRebootPrefController` asks for the credential. | The daemon and the route helper share one check, so both refuse a peer of another user; the check is a pure function of the two readings and a test stands in for the peer (`check_peer_with`), ten mutations are killed. The app calls no API that changes a user. (2) `exitd` runs in the group `vpn` (1016) as well as `inet`, and the policy gives `exitd` `create` on its own `tun_socket` and `exitd_route` the message type `RTM_SETLINK` (`system-sepolicy/0005`). (3) The route helper reads an interface index with `RTM_GETLINK` on its netlink socket and not with `if_nametoindex`, so that its domain keeps no inet socket, as ADR-186 says (`exitd` `0e3d50b9...`). (4) No exit image is built before `privacy/exit/tools/check-syscall-policy.py` passes on the compiled policy and `exit.rc` and the static check of the app passes: the gate holds one row per operation that the two services perform, read from `strace` of the real binaries on a phone, and the static check lists the permission that each platform call of the app needs. | Proposed (the fix set is the owner's instruction of 2026-10-29; the choice of `MANAGE_USERS` over `CREATE_USERS` or a design that does not list users is the assistant's and open to the owner) | `NET-009` result of 2026-10-29: `[measured]` the open of `/dev/net/tun` fails with EACCES for the daemon's groups, the denial `create` on `tun_socket` shows with the node opened, the helper sends `RTM_SETLINK` and opens four `AF_INET` sockets per apply, the controller ends in a `SecurityException` and sets no preference; `[verified-in-source]` `UserManagerService.getUsers` and `ConnectivityService.setOemNetworkPreference` on the build server. `netd` has `allow netd self:tun_socket create` for its tun `[verified-in-source]`. | The gate and the static check would have failed on `2026102301` to `2026102303` for causes (1) to (3) and (5); a host test cannot see a group, an xperm number, a socket that a library call opens or a permission, so the gate rows are the record of what the services do. The three denials of the optional rows stay in the log. |

| ADR-204 | 2026-10-30 | Amend ADR-195 (owner, 2026-10-29: "I pick it up and it already locks"): an impact with no free fall locks only after a pulse. The impact route of grab detection decides at the first sample whose deviation from gravity is at least `impactPeak` (30 m/s^2). An impact after a free fall of at least 250 ms (a real fall) locks at once and one after a shorter fall (a set-down) does not, as before. An impact with no free fall now waits until the magnitude has been at or above 15 m/s^2 for 120 ms (`minPulseMs`): the rise before the first sample over `impactPeak` counts, the time after it is summed within 400 ms, and the lock comes as soon as the sum reaches 120 ms; an impact that does not show that pulse is refused like a set-down (decided once, no cool-down, the rest of its pulse is not judged again). The impact line of the log gets `pulseMs`. Patch `frameworks-base/0011`; no new setting, the 120 ms is a constant of `GrabDetectionService`. | Proposed (owner's instruction to use the recordings and not to measure again; module-built, in `lynx` image `2026103001`, `GRB-024` not run on it) | `[measured]` the recordings of `tools/grab-recorder` (200 Hz, `lynx`, 2026-10-18 and 2026-10-25) with `tools/grab-recorder/events.py`: the 12 recorded grabs are 171 to 310 ms above 15 m/s^2, the 8 pickups and bumps with no free fall 4 to 13 ms (a pickup that was lifted slowly, 79 and 84 ms, did not reach 30 m/s^2); `[measured]` the real `GrabDetector` replayed in 5 phases of a 50 Hz grid: with the rule 12 of 12 grabs lock and no pickup, bump or set-down does, without it the pickup spike (46 m/s^2, 4 ms) locks in 2 of 5 phases and a spike of 13 ms in 1 of 5; `[measured]` on the phone the pickup of 2026-10-29 locked (`peak=30.4 fallMs=0`) and the 12 grabs of the recordings give 30 to 50 at their first sample, so the peak does not separate them (`GRB-024`); `[verified-in-source]` the same detector runs in shadow and active mode; 89 of 89 mutants of the detector are killed. | A pickup, a bump and a hand that taps the phone no longer lock it; a grab, a real fall and (as before) a set-down behave as before; the lock of a grab comes up to 120 ms later than at the first sample (the lock screen, the restart timer and the vibration follow the decision). | Rejected: a higher `impactPeak` (45 m/s^2) misses the owner's gentler grabs, whose first sample is 31 to 41, and does not separate a pickup that peaks high; a rule on rest before the impact (a phone that lay flat), because a phone held still in a hand also lies still and a snatch from a table is not the threat model; a minimum pulse for every impact, because a real fall lands in 40 to 140 ms and must lock. The risk that remains is a grab shorter than 120 ms, none of which is recorded (the shortest of 12 is 171 ms, those of 2026-10-29 were not recorded): the log shows `pulseMs` of every impact from the next image on, and `minPulseMs` is one constant. The first 4 ms spike of a pickup that locked in 2 of 5 phases in `GRB-019` is the case this closes; ADR-195's remark that a minimum pulse width was left out is superseded. |

An Isolated app lives in one persona: a place (`Automatic` is a place in the user's own country, or a place the user chooses from an offline city list), the time zone of the place, a network exit in that country, substitute or real-motion sensors, and matching answers of the location-derived channels (cell identity, network country, Wi-Fi and Bluetooth scans); the location is the real track translated by one fixed vector per app. Compartments never lowers the protection of an app by what the app says about itself; only the user sets Trusted. | ADR-214 | 2026-11-03 | Amend ADR-208 and section 4.2 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-compartments-profiles-design.md`: an app with Location on Substitute data and a place gets a standing fix at that place while no real fix arrives, so exact location works indoors. | ADR-216 | 2026-11-04 | A validation result proves a code state, not an image. A round runs only the rows its build can have changed: the rows that a commit of `freeholdos-platform` or `freeholdos-services` between the two images names, the rows that cite a patch that changed, the rows whose own `row` paths changed, and the rows that the `impact` line of the build lists (written when the changes are reviewed, before the build). A row in that set that has a script `tools/round-checks/<ROW-ID>.sh` is proved by `tools/docs/round-run.py` on the phone with no owner and no agent step; every other row keeps its result and is carried by `tools/docs/round-gate.py`. A result of class X (other device) or class O (owner step) carries until its cause ends. An image that is not built yet gets a preview of the rows to run after its flash, which never holds a build. A release candidate runs `round-gate.py --full`: nothing carries except class X and class O. No row is dropped, and no row is run again without a change that can affect it. | Proposed (owner's requirement 2026-11-04, chat; built 2026-11-04, first used on `2026110204`; the part that gave a row with a check script to a runner is superseded by ADR-220, and the runner, its self-check and the script were removed on 2026-11-05) | Owner 2026-11-04 (chat): the same proven rows were done again after every image, which cost days of the owner's time, energy and tokens; the validation must stay complete and cost far less. Measured on `2026110204` before this decision: 109 open rows, after it: 31 to run, 1 by the runner, 78 carried, 0 unproven `[measured: tools/docs/round-gate.py 2026110204 ACH BOOT CMP GRB HRV IDS IMG KBD LNK MEM MSK NET PRIV PWM]`. Rejected: (1) every non-PASS row recorded again on every image (the practice until now): 109 rows per image for code that did not change. (2) Carry by the code paths of the prefix alone: 63 rows were then carried without any proof that the change spared them, which drops coverage; the owner rejected it. (3) Retire the seven superseded `IMG-*` rows: they block nothing (recorded `NOT_RUN` with the reason) and ADR-209, ADR-211 and the status records cite them as the source of a measurement. (4) A script that runs on every image: it proves code again that did not change (`CMP-034` ran twice on the image where it had passed). (5) One runner for every `[agent]` step: the steps are prose; a script is written per row and only when it proves the whole expected result, so a row with an unprovable part is split first (`CMP-034`, `CMP-049`, `CMP-050`). | Every fix commit names the matrix rows it affects. Every built image gets an `image` line (platform and services commits, base tag) in `tools/docs/round-areas.tsv`, and a build gets an `impact` line from the review of its changes. The carried rows rest on those lines: a dependency that is missing carries a row that the change broke. The `impact` list is a reviewer's judgement `[assumption]`; the host tests and mutation runs cover the code, the device covers the rows named. Amends the practice of the skill `fz-hardware-validation` and the build gate sentence of `AGENTS.md`; the rule there that nothing is called complete before an on-device check holds for every row at its last proof and for a release candidate in full. Records the owner's wish of 2026-11-03 that no row is dropped: none is. | Reopen if a defect is found on a carried row (add its dependency as a `row` line and prove the row), if the round of `2026110204` still costs the owner more than the PIN moments of the rows that need one, or before a release candidate (run `--full`). |

| ADR-223 | 2026-11-10 | The mask list of Compartments takes the whole-host rules (`\|\|host^`) of EasyPrivacy and EasyList as a fourth rule beside the three of ADR-186, and grows from 3,699 to about 91,500 rows. A listed host is masked unless a row of the classifier, the deny list or the allow list is at or above it: a protected row shields everything below it, so no platform domain (`google.com`, `microsoft.com`, `youtube.com`) is masked as a whole, only a dedicated host below it such as `vortex.data.microsoft.com`. The company of such a host is the host itself, and a name over 64 bytes is cut at the left, so a long attacker-chosen host cannot show as the name of a trusted one. Company ids and the row limit move to 32 bits and 262144 rows in the generator, the daemon, the resolver and the exit app; the control protocol is version 5 (a company is four bytes). The largest list file is 16 MiB in the daemon and the resolver (4 MiB no longer holds 262144 rows; the list is 4.2 MB at 91,573 rows), and the exit app holds a company id in an `int` and refuses an id past 31 bits, which the ids of the generator (up to 262144) never reach (owner, 2026-11-10, on both). Rejected: a `long` company id in the exit app (about 40 signatures changed for ids that cannot occur). The importer is `privacy/exit/tools/import-easylist.py`: it takes only a rule that names one host with no condition (no path, wildcard, `domain=`, negated or unknown option), drops the host and what is below it for an exception without a site condition, drops only that host for a conditional one, and ignores page exceptions (`generichide` and the like). one shared company id for the tail beyond 65,535 (a choice for one failing host would unmask the whole tail for that app); the registrable domain as company (tenants of `pages.dev`, `b-cdn.net` and `github.io` would be one company); putting the hosts in `classifier.tsv` (the device parser takes 20,000 lines). | Proposed (generator, importer and tests on branch `masklist-easylist` of `freeholdos-platform`, commits `f4fba7d`, `d529f59`, `9e0c3aa`, `8d80561`, not pushed, not merged; 2026-11-10: the 32-bit change is written and host-tested in the daemon (`freeholdos-services` branch `masklist-easylist`, `7beab16`: control protocol version 5, 262144 rows, `MAX_FILE` 16 MiB, 293 + 7 + 1 tests and 93 of 93 mutations on the build server), the exit app and the resolver (`freeholdos-platform` `803e1a4`; the app holds an id in an int and refuses one past 31 bits; resolver patch `0002`; 116 of 116 and 62 of 62 mutants killed; differential check 0 mismatches); landed on the build server on 2026-11-10 on the owner's word: `packages/modules/DnsResolver` `725a7c0` (patch 0002) and `vendor/freeholdos/exit` `45cd0d5` (the app files and `exitd` `ddf5ddd4…`, built from `7beab16`, two builds identical); only progress, no regression; chose EasyPrivacy and EasyList and 32-bit ids everywhere. `[measured 2026-11-10]` EasyPrivacy and EasyList `202611101405` hold 47,324 and 44,193 whole-host block rules; the importer keeps 91,309 hosts, 88,208 of them not yet covered; Cause of the cold case: vold logs `Evicted fscrypt key` 0.2 s after each stop of `0015` (the plain stop), so the next lock-time start stays `RUNNING_LOCKED`; the key comes back at the duress credential (`fscrypt_unlock_ce_storage`, 0.36 s after the check) and the launcher process starts 0.54 s after the check and draws at +3.1 s; the stop on the return from the decoy is the stock delayed-locking stop and keeps the key. A locked decoy resolves only `com.android.settings/.FallbackHome` as home (`cmd package query-activities --user`), so Launcher3 is not direct-boot aware. Video: 3 s of a black screen with no movement on a cold duress unlock; about 0.6 s of one flat pale colour between the bouncer and the dark decoy home on a warm one; the return to the real session is smooth. | ADR-229 | 2026-11-13 | The Duress unlock is always cold and reaches about 0.90 s from the credential check to the end of the keyguard exit (owner, 2026-11-13, "a duress unlock is always cold, the profile is opened only when it is really needed, so it never gets the chance to be warm"), by PIN and by finger; the decoy home may load late behind an exit that looks and times like the ordinary one, and the owner's profile is mirrored to the decoy wherever a second person could see or hear a difference at the switch (owner, 2026-11-13: find these settings and fix them; fix every defect found on the way). Eight patches, numbered `frameworks-base/0022` to `0029`, written in the disposable repository on the build server's tree (landed on 2026-11-14 with the five later commits named below, and built into `lynx` `2026111401`): 0022 copies `ui_night_mode` and the dark-theme times (the display configuration changed at the switch); 0023 binds the decoy's `KeyChain` on its own thread; 0024 copies the volumes, the refresh rate, the theme setting and the lock-screen and status-bar values the decoy lacked, by an allowlist; 0025 copies the owner's wallpaper to the decoy when the device locks; 0026 starts the decoy's home after the keyguard has gone away and holds the going-away until the window manager has applied the user change; 0027 requests the exit of an authorized switch right after the keyguard reset of the before-switch callback and keeps the ordinary unlock animation (the cut of the animation is removed); 0028 returns from the decoy credential check as soon as the decoy's unlock has started and starts its home 150 ms after the going-away; 0029 adds the global setting `oblivios_duress_fast` (0 turns 0026 to 0028 off without a build). The decoy differs from the owner in `ui_night_mode`, the theme setting, the volumes, `mode_ringer_streams_affected`, `peak_refresh_rate`, `show_touches` and the wallpaper. The lock-screen notification stack is drawn at `shadeNotifAlpha=0.498` in every unlock of the day, ordinary ones included, and shows for 3 frames on the black stage of a duress unlock. Source read on the build server's tree: `UserController.continueStartUserInternal`, `WindowManagerService.moveUserToForeground` and `startUserSwitchTransition`, `ActivityTaskManagerService.keyguardGoingAway`, `Transition.java` (the screenshot sync), `AppCompatResourceOverlayPolicy`, `UserTrackerImpl`, `SecurityControllerImpl`, `WallpaperManagerService.setWallpaper`. | Rejected, each with its reason: keeping or predicting a warm decoy (the owner: it is opened only when needed); the exit at `USER_SWITCHING` (the switch is complete 0.3 s later, so the exit ends after about 1.2 s); the exit at the credential check (it would uncover the owner's windows before the window manager has switched the user); dropping a going-away that arrives early instead of holding it (a dropped request leaves the keyguard up); a splash screen for the decoy home (it shows the launcher's icon on a flat colour, itself a tell); a placeholder window of SystemUI over the switch (a new window and lifecycle, and not needed once the home start is deferred); copying the owner's whole settings namespace by a denylist (it would copy identifiers, accounts and the media ids of tones; `android_id`, the Bluetooth address, the autofill and credential services, ringtones and the VPN settings are deliberately not copied); the removal of the cut is revisited if the swipe of the lock screen returns. `[assumption]` A going-away of the keyguard with no activity of the incoming user shows the decoy's wallpaper and plays the exit; the going-away may merge into the user switch transition, so the home start waits 150 ms; the first switch after the wallpaper copy may still relaunch the launcher once (its overlays change once), so a measurement starts with a throwaway unlock; the wallpaper service of the decoy rebinds at the switch (a gap of about 0.13 s behind the fading bouncer); the owner's wallpaper image sits in the decoy's data. Five commits follow in the same repository (2026-11-14), `frameworks-base/0030` to `0034`: 0030 posts the deferred home start after a going-away only when one is waiting, so an ordinary unlock posts nothing; 0031 copies the owner's Auto confirm unlock to the decoy at the lock when both PINs have the same known length (and switches it off when the owner's is off), so the decoy's lock screen no longer asks for Enter where the owner's submits (16 JVM tests, a mutation of the length rule fails them; type-checked like the rest); 0033 and 0034 are stage logs for the timing rows (`Exit held`, `Exit released after N ms`, `Home start deferred`), on debuggable builds only: 0034 gated them after an automated security review, because a log line that exists only for an authorized switch would show on a user build that one ran. The notification card (cause read from the source and the logs of 2026-11-13, not measured after the fix): the stack's alpha is raised to 1 when the bouncer-to-gone transition ends (`PrimaryBouncerToGoneTransitionViewModel#notificationAlpha`, `onFinish`), 500 ms after SystemUI calls going-away; the held going-away of a switch (transition 102, 787 ms) starts the exit later than that, so the keyguard is still up with the stack at full alpha over the empty stage, a card for a few frames, stuck until the status bar state is SHADE. The fix (`0032`) keeps the alpha at 0 at the end of that transition while `AuthorizedSwitchExit` is set, for an authorized switch only; the ordinary unlock is unchanged; the Kotlin files type-check and three tests were added, not run. Not closed by these patches: the other differences of the decoy session (its notifications differ, not read), a decoy home with six icons against the owner's five, the 0.76 s before the switch is released beyond what 0028 saves, and the finger route. Log lines that existed before and name an authorized switch or a mirror failure (`Skipping keyguard for an authorized switch`, the warnings of the mirror and the wallpaper copy) are on user builds too: a residual side channel, not changed here. `0021` stays in the series until the series is measured. | `DUR-019` on the built image is still above 1.0 s cold; `DUR-039` shows a window of the owner; the keyguard stays up or the decoy has no home after 1.5 s; the owner sees a tell the patches did not remove; the removal of the cut brings the swipe back. |

Replaces ADR-229's rejection of a placeholder window (owner, 2026-11-14: "if this is the best solution, do it", after a better one was looked for). (2) Device: the next software update leaves no Guardian on the phone: no `GuardianApp`, no privapp allowlist, no location fixture. `check-required-packages.sh` no longer requires them and refuses all three in every build type, so a tree that still builds Guardian fails the gate. Done in the sources: `oblivios-platform`, `oblivios-services`, `oblivios-manifest` and this repository, committed locally. Not done: landing the regenerated patches on the build-server `vendor/adevtool` and `device/generic/goldfish` trees with `git am`, the pin update of `adevtool-version-check.mk`, removal of `vendor/oblivios/guardian` from the build-server tree, the next image, and the read on the phone. | The owner decided that Guardian leaves the product. The chain of twelve patches was rebuilt offline: the old landed order applies, and the new chain ends in the old `device-common.mk` minus the Guardian block (diff read 2026-11-16). `[assumption]` Whether Android deletes the data of a system app that disappears in an update is not verified; the device read decides. | The regenerated patches carry no `index` lines, because the blobs of the new chain are unknown until it is landed; the records of each package say so. The fixes are committed locally; `oblivios-manifest`, `oblivios-platform` and `oblivios-services` are not pushed yet. Coverage of the claim review: the root README, NOTICE and SECURITY.md of each repository, the source records and READMEs named in the consequences, the README and NOTICE of `exit-daemon`, and every number or table row that the build can compare; the prose of the other package READMEs and `SOURCE.md` records (about 45 files in `oblivios-platform`) was not read sentence by sentence, so the ADR-171 gate "every sentence of the READMEs and the NOTICE files" is met for the root files and not yet for those. This is an intention and not a change of stage: ADR-171 and ADR-248 stand, there is no date, and the site says on `Devices`, `Status` and the FAQ that nothing is promised and that the five gates of a public beta and the open rows of the Pixel 10a bring-up record come first. | Accepted (owner, 2026-11-18, in the order of the six points of the message that asked for it and the answers to three questions: pages `Devices` and `Get involved`, the intention on `Status` and the FAQ, and the ownership line without a third party's name). (3) The page `Get involved` is replaced by `Contact`: an issue on GitHub is the way the owner likes best, for a bug, a question or a criticism of his code, and he takes the responsibility for it; for anything private an email to the address of `SECURITY.md`, in plain text, with encryption optional; a vulnerability is better sent by email, and the page still points to the key. (4) The decision that the owner's phones are described only by the owner is added to the rules of the site: a state of a device is written on the site only from the owner's own statement or from a record that is newer than the build under discussion. | Accepted (owner, 2026-11-18: the menu is far too much and must look like the rest of the site; Compartments is on the Pixel 10a; SIM, camera, calls and the rest work; \"real data\" must be on the site and the wrong data replaced at once; issues are welcome; a contact page without encryption). | The records that ADR-256 read are older than the phone. `docs/devices/support-policy.md` still says for the Pixel 10a \"Mobile core, hardware, accessibility: not run, no SIM was inserted\", and `docs/product/status-stallion-and-memory.md` and `docs/product/status.md` still say that Compartments goes to `stallion` when its remaining `lynx` rows are shown; the owner states that Compartments is on the Pixel 10a and that calls, SIM and camera work. The agent copied the old state onto the site as a present fact, which is the failure the public launch design names as an anti-goal (a site that claims more or other than the records support, here less than is true). (3) The Data Map is called Atlas on the site, as in ADR-160, ADR-162 and ADR-165. (4) The wording that made the records look like a restriction is gone: the Contact page no longer says that `oblivios-docs` cannot be changed, and its README says to open an issue for an error; documents are edited by hand where that is the right way. (5) The records that were behind the facts are corrected by hand in `oblivios-foundation`: `docs/devices/support-policy.md` (the mobile core row), `docs/product/status.md`, `docs/product/status-privacy-and-identity.md` and `docs/product/status-stallion-and-memory.md`. | Accepted (owner, 2026-11-18: a drawer of 75 percent of the screen and the full height with an animation, no box around the button; real data on `Devices` found in the other files of `oblivios-foundation`; the name Atlas; documents may be edited by hand). | ADR-261 | 2026-11-18 | Turn on Vercel Web Analytics for `oblivios.org`, and make the issue rule one. (1) The owner wants to see whether there is interest, so the site counts page views with Vercel Web Analytics. It amends the privacy rule of the launch design (section 5.4: no analytics) and the line of the site \"no tracking\". Vercel documents it as cookie free, a visitor as a hash that lasts a day, and the data points as the page, the referrer, the country and the browser, system and device type, with no tie to an IP address. It needs one script; the site carries it as `<script defer src=\"/_vercel/insights/script.js\">`, from its own address, only in a build that Vercel makes (`VERCEL=1`), so the local build, the review build and the snapshot have none. `check-build.mjs` accepts exactly that tag in a Vercel build, requires it on every page there, and fails on any other script (checked: a second script fails). Speed Insights and the toolbar stay off. The Privacy page, the footer line and the README of `site/` say so. (2) The sentence of the Privacy page that the owner uses only GitHub's traffic page is removed: the owner is not sure it is true. (3) The rule for a vulnerability is one rule: an issue is fine for anything, an email is for whoever wants the problem not public before it is fixed, and encryption is optional. `SECURITY.md` of the four repositories (commits `9d8ef6f`, `1f42fcb`, `70f6fd0` and the source copy in `tools/docs/`), the Security, Contact and FAQ pages say it. Issues are on in all four repositories (`gh api`, 2026-11-18). | Accepted (owner, 2026-11-18: Vercel Analytics on, to see whether there is interest; the issues are on, resolve the rule). | The product is a privacy system and the site said it ran no script; a script of the platform that serves the site is the smaller cost against not knowing whether anyone cares, which the owner weighed. The count is anonymous by Vercel's own description, and the script comes from the site's own origin, so the site still makes no request to another host and the policy `default-src 'self'` holds. Rejected: no analytics (GitHub traffic only), a third-party counter, a script in every build (the local build and the review build would carry a path that does not exist off Vercel). | Done and read on 2026-11-18: the tag appears once on each of the eleven pages in a build made with `VERCEL=1` and on none in a local build; the check accepts it and fails on a second script; the page texts and the launch design are amended; the issue settings read with `gh`. Not done: the analytics itself (it works only after the owner clicks Enable under Analytics in the Vercel project, which adds the routes `/_vercel/insights/*` at the next deployment; the path of the script is the one the plain HTML documentation of Vercel names `/<unique-path>/script.js`, and the classic path is used here, which a first deployment has to confirm); the limits and the price of Web Analytics on the owner's plan (Vercel's documentation says it is available on all plans; the limits page was not read); the wiki and the projects of the four repositories are on, and `R-3` says they stay off. | A deployment shows that the script path is not served; Vercel changes the terms or the data points; the owner turns the analytics off or wants a different measure of interest. |
| ADR-262 | 2026-11-18 | Harden the headers of `oblivios.org`, add structured data and the robots meta, and let the first deployment stay public. (1) Headers: the CSP is `default-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self'` (the three added directives are not covered by `default-src`), `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` is added for old browsers, and `Permissions-Policy` lists fifteen interfaces as off (accelerometer, autoplay, camera, display capture, geolocation, gyroscope, HID, magnetometer, microphone, MIDI, payment, serial, USB, XR). The empty `Permissions-Policy` of ADR-248 is replaced: the live response of 2026-11-18 had no such header, because Vercel does not serve an empty value `[measured, curl -I]`. `bluetooth` is not listed: Chromium reports it as an unrecognised feature `[measured, console of the pane]`. Vercel adds `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` to the static files; it is kept and the Privacy page says so, because the site holds nothing that is not public. (2) Structured data: every page except the 404 page carries one schema.org JSON-LD block (Organization, WebSite, WebPage, and a BreadcrumbList or, on the home page, a SoftwareSourceCode that names the four repositories and `isBasedOn` GrapheneOS). Every value is a fact that the page or the footer already states; the block has no licence, rating, application type or price. `check-build.mjs` allows exactly this block, parses it, requires it on each page but the 404 page, and still fails on any other script. (3) The robots meta is `index, follow, max-image-preview:large, max-snippet:-1`, and `noindex` on the 404 page only; `check-build.mjs` fails on a page where this is the other way round. (4) The owner said on 2026-11-18 that the deployment at `oblivios-docs.vercel.app` may stay public until the domain is linked, that it must be secure, that SEO is done as technique now and as content later, and that the owner links the domain. | Accepted (owner, 2026-11-18). | Search engines read the page, not the intent: titles, descriptions, canonical, Open Graph and the sitemap were there already, so what is left on the page is structured data, the robots meta and a sound set of headers; the larger effect on being found comes from content that answers what people search for and from links to the site, which the owner decides next. The CSP of `default-src 'self'` alone left framing, forms and the base address open. Rejected: `lastmod` in the sitemap (one date for every page, rewritten at each regeneration, is not a modification date, and a search engine ignores a date that is not accurate); `FAQPage` markup (no expected effect on this site `[assumption: from memory of the search engine's rules, not checked against its current documentation]`); a `license` property (the licence of the patch repositories is not stated in one place); `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` (it could block other sites from showing the social preview image, which was not assessed); the HSTS preload submission (D-2: after the quiet week); IndexNow (it sends the page addresses to a third party); new pages for search questions (the owner chooses them next, each needs a claim review). | Done and read on 2026-11-18: `npm run build` with and without `VERCEL=1`, `check-build: OK` on 11 pages with the same 307 links; three mutation runs fail as they must (broken JSON-LD, a second script, `noindex` on the home page); the headers read with `curl -I` on the local server; the JSON-LD of the home page read; in the browser pane 44 loads (11 pages at 320, 390, 798 and 1920 px) show no overflow, no policy violation, no blocked same-origin frame, and the fonts load. Not done: a check of the live headers (they reach Vercel with the next push and deployment); a structured-data validator or a rich-results test (both are external services and were not run); the Search Console and Bing Webmaster verification (needs the owner's accounts and the linked domain); the Enable of Web Analytics and a redeployment, which the owner does, and a read of `/_vercel/insights/script.js` after it; the linking of the domain, the primary domain and the redirect of the Vercel address to it (the owner); the description, topics and homepage of the four repositories and the wiki and projects switched off (R-3, the wording is not yet approved). | A search engine reports an error in the structured data; a header breaks a feature of a browser; the licence of the code is stated in one place; the owner turns the site private or asks for the content pages. |
